| `authz/orgchart` | `authz.Chart` over ONE `org.Organization`: who leads a person, a project, a unit and a page container, whether somebody leads anybody, whether a seat SITS in a unit or below it (its own unit chain compared by pointer; leading a unit from outside it is not sitting in it), which is what a share with a team admits, and whether a seat's ROLE carries a grant (`SeatGranted`: an AGENT seat's `health_tools`, a human seat and an unknown grant opening nothing) — with `GrantedSeats`, the same rule over the whole tree, so the seats a grant admits and the seats a wake about it reaches cannot differ — pure arithmetic over a tree, tested against a tree rather than through a running engine (measured, the walks inside the engine's own methods could have their loops deleted with that suite still green). Its own package because TWO callers hold a tree: the engine asks about the company it is RUNNING (`engine.ChartAuthority`, which delegates here per call), and a `/config` write asks about the two DOCUMENTS it compares — the revision it replaces and the one it proposes — which the running company is neither of. A nil tree answers `authz.ErrNoChart`, never `false` |
| `config` | The two tiers. Tier A (`Bootstrap`) is ops-owned, on disk, restart to change — including the retention estate (`stream.tracker_retention`, `store.snapshot_dir`, `retention.backup_owner`), because how a deployment backs up and how long its disk holds a replay window are facts about the OPERATOR rather than about the company. Tier B (`Company`) is founder-owned, versioned in the store, edited live — the org chart included (`roles:`, `units:`), whose every seat handle and unit key a write mints into the stored document (`MintIdentities`), so correcting a name in the STORED document never moves an identity and a different handle is a different seat — a FILE is minted afresh from its names on every import, which is why the shipped examples declare every handle and key. A person's INBOX HORIZON is the COMPANY's (`inbox.retention_days`, `Inbox`), never a domain's block: a person's inbox is every native domain's notices under one set of rules, so a horizon under the tracker left every other source on a clock of its own — the split ADR-0018 ended for the company's clock — and the epoch states it with its default applied (`Inbox.Days`), so the applier that declines a notice and the sweep that deletes one read the company's horizon from one function. A third channel beside errors: `Warnings()` on each tier is what is VALID and worth knowing before applying — a declined fsync's window, a trim that will never advance, a human seat nobody can be messaged at, a company that declares NO human seat and so can admit nobody (every person holds one, ADR-0032 — right for a company its agents run alone through Tier A tokens, so a warning and never a rule), a seat ceiling the company's leaves idle, a merge queue whose depth and batch at `code.checks.max_timeout_seconds` land fewer changes a day than the seats that may merge onto it ask, one each (`LandingCapacity`, M5-D28: the same arithmetic the `code_merge_rate_over_capacity` alarm prices a queue by at its observed round, those seats counted through the chart index's `Members`) — printed by `crewlet validate`, and LOGGED where every deployment sees them, because most never run `validate` (an image, a chart or an operator renders the document and starts the node): Tier A's by `crewlet run` once per start, a `config_advisory` line each, judged on the document with the run's flags FOLDED IN (`overrideNode`, `overrideLogging`) and held to every Tier A rule again before a line is written (`validateFolded`, whose refusal names the flags), so a `-debug` run is not told to pass `-debug` and no advisory precedes a refusal a flag drew, and a company's by every node once per epoch it APPLIES (the same event, `config=company`, on the reconcile's success branch beside the dangling-reference and admission lines), which is the only report a company seeded from a file ever gets — never affecting an exit code, because a warning that can fail a build is one somebody suppresses. `TierWarnings` is the one place the two tiers are read TOGETHER, and it only warns, since no rule needs both documents: printed by `crewlet validate` with both files and carried in a configuration write's answer, comparing an EXPLICIT cache budget only, and logged by each node as `config_tier_warning` once per applied epoch through `NodeTierWarnings`, which compares the budget that node RESOLVED (derived from its volume by default) — one of its two documents is the node's own Tier A, so the same revision warns on one node and not another. Whether this node is in a FLEET is `Bootstrap.clustered`, ONE predicate — peers, a cluster name, an external broker, a leaf, or a member opening a leaf listener — which the topology's refusal of local coordination reads; the embedded broker admits only keys derived from `secrets.keys` (ADR-0064), so a peer or leaf URL carrying a userinfo (`user:pass@` or `token@`, with a scheme or without one: any `@`, read off the text because a parse misses a schemeless one) is refused, because the engine derives the fleet's credentials and presents them itself, and no sentence quotes a fleet URL with its userinfo (`withoutUserinfo`), and `stream.type: nats` must present one of its own — `credentials`, `token`, `tls.cert` or a userinfo in any `stream.url` entry (`Stream.presentsCredential`) — because a node presenting nothing reaches only a server that authenticates nobody, which the dial refuses by its INFO; a `direct` coding box in such a company is warned at its seat's `run_in`, or once at the default (`directBoxWarnings`, over `Company.SeatBoxes`), since it runs as the engine's own user and reads the keyring that signs every run's git token. The names a coding run's git environment is the engine's (`ReservedSandboxEnv`) are refused in any run environment by an ADMISSION rule, since the engine's value wins over them on every run. Whether the company needs a node role at all is `Company.NeedsRole` (git only with `code.native`), the ONE rule each node's unmanned-role warning and the fleet view's `unmanned_roles` read. Tier A is the root of trust and therefore resolves with `EnvOnly` — it holds the keys to the secret store, so it can never read a value out of it — and resolves BEFORE it decodes, TYPED by the field each reference lands in: into text the value is that text, character for character (a resolved `null` is not an absence), and into a number or a switch it must be the WHOLE value and is read, trimmed, as the literal it spells — because substituting text everywhere left no number field able to take a reference while the docs promised one anywhere. A reference spliced into a number, or one resolving to nothing there, is refused by name rather than guessed at, and so is a fraction bound for a whole number, which the decoder used to truncate without a word (`api.port: 8080.9` ran as 8080). Tier B's secrets are `${VAR}` POINTERS, stored verbatim, resolved only where a provider or transport is constructed — so a Tier B reference is judged as the text it is, and a field whose consumer resolves only a WHOLE one (the Mattermost and Slack bot tokens, the Mattermost username) refuses one spliced into other text rather than sending the braces to the vendor. The schema admits what the DECODER reads rather than what a Go type spells, and a test offers every position of both tiers every such value. It also marks every credential position `x-crewlet-secret: true`, read through the same predicate (`secrets.Field`) that redaction masks by, and a test runs the redactor over a fully populated document and holds the marked positions to the masked ones in both directions. A protection rule's MERGE GATE is bounded by constants both tiers and the code domain read, defined here because config cannot import the domain: `CodeMaxHumanPaths` and `CodeMaxRequiredChecks` (32 each), `CodeMaxGateBytes` (8 KiB of the record's JSON, so a repository's 64 rules carry at most 512 KiB of gates), `CodeMaxQueueDepth` and `CodeMaxQueueBatch` (8 each); and THE ONE CHECK-NAME RULE, `ValidCodeCheckName` (`^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9._-]{0,63}$`, `CodeMaxCheckNameBytes` 64), lives here for the same reason and is the code domain's too — a checks file's names and every check row's key are held to it — so no gate can require a check no repository can name, and no repository names one a gate cannot require. So do the CELLS A CHECK RUNS IN (`CodeCheckCells`: container, e2b, with `direct` and `self` refused BY NAME, `CodeCheckCellRefusal`, U1 a), the one rule a check's secret is named by (`ValidCodeCheckEnvName`, `CodeMaxCheckEnvNameBytes` 128) and the longest a check runs (`CodeCheckTimeoutCeiling`, six hours), each read by the company's `code.checks` here and a repository's checks file in the domain. `code.checks` (`CodeChecks`) is how the company runs those checks: `run_in` and `allow_run_in` (a cell must be one the catalogue configures, and the allowed ones must hold the one a check naming none runs in), `env` (a credential map, its keys none of `ReservedSandboxEnv`, which holds beside the code host's own the seven `ReservedCheckEnv` a check's box is told of its check), and `max_timeout_seconds` (60..21 600; unset `CodeDefaultCheckMaxTimeout`, fifty minutes, the shortest E2B session less a check box's setup grace, a sum internal/code holds), and `remote` (`CodeChecksRemote`, U45 a: `max_running` 0..1 024 unset `CodeDefaultRemoteMaxRunning` 16 and `box_hours_per_day` 0..100 000 unset `CodeDefaultRemoteBoxHoursPerDay` 192, both POINTERS since 0 starts no remote box and both PROVISIONAL, derived from E2B's 20-sandbox Hobby limit and one queue saturated at ten-minute checks; `Resolved` is THE ONE PLACE the defaults apply, answering minutes a day, which a check runner reads at every walk). `Company.CheckRunIn` is THE ONE RESOLUTION of a check's default cell (`run_in`, else the catalogue's default where a check runs there, else none), and `SandboxPlacements` reaches every cell a check may run in, so the engine builds a check's backend as a seat's and a check's container asks for the image. A `branch` rule of `code.repos[].protect` DECLARES that gate (`merge_by`, `approvals`, `approvals_unless`, `human_paths` of `CodeHumanPath`, `checks`, `landing` of `CodeLanding`, `owners_approve`, `delete_source`, `agents_edit_checks`): any field set forms one, `CodeProtection.Gate` the ONE PLACE its defaults resolve (writers, none, a queue at `CodeDefaultQueueDepth` 4 and `CodeDefaultQueueBatch` 2, measured by the queue's throughput benchmark, seats) into the `CodeGate` the reconcile records, whose JSON is the record's length (`EncodedBytes`, so `CodeMaxGateBytes` refuses exactly what the domain would); its rules are RUNNABLE — a gate field on a tag rule refused once at the first field it sets, every closed set (`CodeMergeBys`, `CodeApprovalCounts`, `CodeDeleteSources`, `CodeChangeClasses` with `CodePathClasses` the only ones an exempting list takes, no_code_change refused there by name), every list's bound and uniqueness, every glob `gitref.ParsePathGlob` reads — each set held to the domain's in order by an engine test; and its idle fields WARN, never refuse, at the field (`idleGateFields`: owners_approve with no path owners, landing with no checks, approvals_unless beside approvals none). `code.merge_by_floor` (`CodeMergeBy`, unset writers) is the least any protected merge needs, refused without `code.native`. A seat's code tier is ONE FIELD, `role.code.access` — what a GitHub app is created with and every token it mints carries, and the native code host's cap on what the chart grants — never a vendor block's: it was `integrations.github.tier`, which left a company on the native host nowhere to write it and changed what a seat may do when it moved between hosts. AND SO IS A STREAM THAT SURVIVES A RESTART on every broker MEMBER (`stream.store_dir` on an embedded member, a single node included, `Stream.Durable`, asked by `Validate` and again at `engine.New`'s door before anything opens), while a leaf — a node without `data`, which keeps no stream — is REFUSED one: every data node keeps its state logs on the stream from boot, company or none, so an in-memory member is a node whose first restart leaves it unable to serve. It was a CROSS-TIER rule (`CheckTiers`, deleted with it) asked only of a company, which let through exactly the node started with none, and no rule needs both documents any more. THREE MORE SETTINGS STOP BEING OPTIONAL the moment a node SERVES THE API (`Bootstrap.ServesAPI`: `api.port` non-zero AND the `ingress` role — on the Bootstrap rather than the API block because half the answer is the node block's roles — and a probe-only port needs none of them), and `api.go` is where the rule lives: `api.external_url` (the cookie's Secure flag and __Host- prefix, the origin a write and a socket handshake are judged against, and every link a person follows — the engine is behind a TLS-terminating proxy and can read none of it off the request; it is the vendors' address too unless `api.public` opens a listener for them, whose own `api.public.external_url` is then REQUIRED and is what `API.WebhookBase` answers, so Tier B carries no address at all), `api.auth.admin` (`iam.AdminPosture`: whether THIS node serves `admin` — `serve`, or `refuse`, which clamps admin away and keeps every app, the apps expanded before the clamp — applied per node per request so a mixed fleet is a LEGAL state each node publishes its posture for; the zero is refused naming the field, and an unknown name in a Tier A token's `grants` is refused naming the three, as is `admin` on a `refuse` node) and at least one `api.auth.tokens` entry (a fresh estate is empty, so its session is what creates the company and invites the first person onto a human seat it declares, and the way back in when an administrator has locked themselves out). A token's value is checked on what the `${VAR}` RESOLVES to, which is the only place the floor bites, and `api.trusted_proxies` is a CIDR list and never a bool, because the question is whether THIS peer is the proxy. `api.auth.company_writers` names the Tier A tokens that alone may change a MANAGED company document (ADR-0030), each entry held to the token-id grammar and judged on the CREDENTIAL a write came through (`MayWriteCompany`, which the viewer answer reads too), so a person's session, a machine token and a lead's subtree write are all refused while it is set. `allow_anonymous_read` and `api.auth.disabled` are DELETED, and a file still carrying either is refused at its line like any unknown key (`KnownFields`, so no retired posture can be silently inverted when the key itself is refused) but BY NAME, through `removedKeys` (`removed.go`), which says the key was removed and what replaces it — because the generic refusal tells somebody upgrading an old file to check a spelling no spelling passes — keyed on the block's Go type, since `disabled:` anywhere but `api.auth` is an ordinary typo and reads like one: the first was a bool whose safe value was its zero and which was `omitempty`, so closing it did not survive an export round trip, and the second authenticated the EMPTY credential into full operator authority — and so is `api.auth.oidc`, because nobody signs in through a third party any more — and with it went the BACKEND SELECTOR: password sign-in is always served, since a deployment nobody was invited to already signs nobody in, and `api.auth.totp` (UNSET IS `required`, so `optional` is always somebody's explicit choice), `accept_insecure` and `min_password_length` sit directly under `api.auth`; and so is `api.auth.bootstrap`, because the Tier A token IS the credential a company has before it has anybody, so the first person is invited under it like everybody after them — onto a human seat. `DiffOrg` is what a `/config` write CHANGES in Tier B, object by object: every seat (by handle) and unit (by key) added, removed, moved or edited, each with the PLACES it reaches — where it was and is, a unit itself when its own fields change, what a NEW `lead:` or `manages:` entry names, every object whose `lead:` or `manages:` entry already states the id an ADDED seat or unit takes (a dangling `lead: ghost` is taken over by adding `ghost` anywhere) — plus the KEYS another system finds a seat or unit by that it sets, clears or alters (a project, space, channel, email or contact identity, all `admin`'s: a project outlives the unit that declared it and an account exists at its vendor, so who else holds a key is not a fact two documents state, and judged against the document's own objects a lead declared an orphaned project their team's and became its lead), the credentials it touches, each credential field compared WHOLE, keys included (an `mcp_env` key with nothing under it starts that per-seat server for the seat, and a walk of the strings alone found nothing in it), with every `${VAR}` it touches in ANY field counted among them (a seat's `email` and contact ids resolve a whole reference through the secret store and the engine's own environment, and are recited to whoever looks the seat up), and the settings keys that differ, because the org chart's leads may write their own subtree and each of those places is a subtree decision (internal/authz's). The GRANTS a seat's role carries that it sets or clears (`OrgChange.Grants`, today `health_tools`) are listed apart and are `admin`'s too: a grant opens what only an administrator reads, so a lead who could set one in their own subtree would read it through their seat. A human seat carrying `health_tools` is refused at admission (`validateHumanSeatGrants`): the grant decides only an agent, and a person reads those tools as an administrator. Only what the write changes is judged, and two objects on one id are placed at the root, nobody's subtree `code.scratch` bounds a seat's own SCRATCH repositories, which only its tools create: `idle_days` (7 unless set, 1 to 365 — the floor is a day because a turn holding a repository idle for 22 hours records its hold, `code.HoldRecordAfter`, and the record must land inside the idle window a pass in flight read, which internal/objstore/references holds) and `max_per_seat` (a pointer: unset is 50, 0 allows none, 1000 at most); it is refused without `code.native`. A declared repository's name is a PROJECT's (`gitref.ValidProjectName`), since a dotted name is a seat's scratch repository, and `CodeMaxRepos`, the admission bound on `code.repos`, is also the runtime cap a tool's project create meets (`repo_limit`, declared and tool-made projects counted alike) |
| `configplane` | The control plane: how a fleet agrees which company configuration it is running, and what a node does when it does not have the current one. An append-only ACTIVATION POINTER names the epoch and every node writes its own apply status; a node reads both and picks a posture. Append-only because re-activating an UNCHANGED revision is the credential-rotation gesture, and a pointer keyed on revision id would rebuild nothing on exactly that operation. Lag alone never sheds: it must be CONFIRMED — a failed attempt of the node's own, or behind past `LagGrace` — and `FleetView.LagConfirmed` is that rule stated once, which the posture and the engine's irreversible judgements (the mailbox retirement, the code purge) both read, so a node the posture reads as waiting is never one another reader warns is behind |
| `seat` | Which seats this node runs. The placement policy is deliberately dumb — greedy claim to `ceil(seats / live nodes)`, converging in BOTH directions, `preferred` orders the attempt and never gates it; a change to the seat set — an apply adding or removing a role — asks for a pass at once (`Resweep`) rather than leaving every new seat unclaimed until the tick. A STOP'S COORDINATION STEPS DRAW ON ONE ALLOWANCE (`StopBudget`, one heartbeat interval of the lease TTL, carried on the context and charged only while a step is in flight), so a member that lost its store spends one allowance stopping rather than a bound per step; a layer where a stop may begin takes it with `WithinStop`. `watchdog` is the other half: a wedged event loop cannot be signalled, so the thread's only honest move is to exit — and it is the engine's ONE unilateral exit, so a node whose embedded broker declares itself dead after its boot (nats-server's Fatalf, carried as `jetstream.Death`) leaves through it too (`Watchdog.Fail`), at once and armed or not, because every step a drain would take rides the broker that died — with its OWN exit code (69, `EX_UNAVAILABLE`, beside the wedge's 75), because a restart log is often all a deployment keeps and the two send a reader to opposite places. The heartbeat stamps its beat as it asks each renew, never only once a pass is done, because a renew the store leaves unanswered is held to its read's whole bound and a pass over many seats on a node that cannot reach its store is TURNING — keeping each seat to its own lease's TTL, then dropping it — where a beat stamped once a pass read that as a wedge once the bounds summed past the TTL. The unmanned-role check asks `RoleNeeded` first: `git` is needed only where the company sets `code.native`, and a role that stops being needed while nobody held it is `fleet_role_unneeded`, never `fleet_role_manned`. The PRESENCE ROW each beat writes (`presenceMeta`) is the profile, the host's own counts (`seats_held`, `seats_withdrawn`) and, on a git node, what its git cache gives its background work (`Config.GitBackground`, `coord.GitBackgroundKey`) — each from memory and written on every beat, the budget's zero included — and the STATUS, from a hook bounded to `StatusBudgetRatio` of the beat and published whole or not at all; a placement input never rides the status, because a beat that gives up on the hook drops it whole, and every peer would read that node as not saying |
| `estate` | The replicated estate ROUTED, from every node — ADR-0025 and its amendment. ONE `Router` runs on EVERY node, with or without `data`, and every seam the tools, the operator's surfaces (the API's tracker and knowledge-base routes, the operator's MCP) and the duties consume is answered through it, so a tool cannot tell which kind of node it is on. Every data node holds the whole estate, so the router answers in-process where THIS node's copy serves — held to this node's floors exactly as a remote data node is — and otherwise asks the data nodes its `Placement` names (every live data node, off the WATCHED presence view): the one that answered last first, then the `rendezvous` order on its own id, a node gone silent last for thirty seconds, over that serving node's OWN subjects rather than a scatter or a queue group, because the asker must know which node an unanswered write went to — one for every operation but a push's proposal, which a node answers on a LANE of its own (`crewlet.estate.<node>.proposals`, `proposalAnswers` 3 136 wide: the SUM of what the committer and its two sibling writers each hold node-wide — `code.CommitterNodeProposals` 1 088, `code.ChangeWriterNodeWrites` and `code.ChecksWriterNodeWrites` 1 024 each — every one refusing busy at once past its own share, so no component's burst holds a slot another's requests need), because a proposal holds its answer slot while it waits for its batch, and on the shared registration sixty-four queued pushes held every slot the node answered its reads, its other writes and a git fetch's resolve through; a lane answers only the operations declared on it (`op.answeredOn`) and refuses any other unserved, and a node registers every lane or none. A copy that is WRONG is OUT OF SERVICE — a peer asking is told `out_of_service`, and its node keeps its seats while their calls go to peers — and a copy that LAGS its logs is a worse node to ask, never no node: asked last, and told to answer anyway — and so is a node confirmed BEHIND THE COMPANY'S CONFIGURATION (shed or stuck, never lag alone: `Backend.Current`, the engine's `admits`, the reconciler's `Admits`), on every operation but admission's ungated ping, because what it supplies from its own epoch — the chart a people field and a lead resolve by, a unit, a search's scope, the code limits — is the revision the fleet replaced, a value every reader meets anyway when a revision changes one, while a refusal would cost a fleet whose only data node failed an apply its tracker, pages and usage; it answers a reason of its own, `behind_config`, which an asker REMEMBERS for `suspectFor` (per node, never per copy: the posture is the node's) and meanwhile skips the node for — asking it only as the last resort, after every copy that only lags (whose answer holds the current configuration, in that order whether or not the asker has heard the refusal yet, so every asker sends a repository's pushes to one node), told at once to answer anyway, and a push's runner-up instead — because unlike a lag it LASTS (until the apply succeeds, a stuck node's until a person acts), and forgotten between requests it cost every request one refusal per such node and every push to a repository it commits for its whole proposal; `engine.TestEveryEpochSeamAServingDecideReadsIsClassified` holds every seam a serving decide reads (`tracker.WriterDeps`, `Backend`, `ServerSeams`, the code committer's, change writer's, store's and reader's options, and the options each searcher, reader and store behind a `Backend` field is built from — `pages.SearcherOptions`, `pages.ReaderOptions`, `pages.Options`, the search fan-out and the engine's item ranker) to a class — `grant`, `value`, `gate` or `none` — so a grant judged from the chart, which being asked last cannot make sound, is never added unseen; and an operation that JUDGES one DECLARES it (`op.judgesGrant`, a predicate over its decoded arguments: `code.read` and `code.resolve_ref` where the asker is a SEAT; `code.change` wherever the chart judges the write, a seat's or one that mentions anybody (`code.ChangeProposal.JudgesChart`); `code.queue` for every enqueue, whoever asks, and a seat's dequeue (`code.QueueProposal.JudgesChart`); `code.merge` for every merge, whoever asks (`code.MergeProposal.JudgesChart`); and `code.conversation` wherever the chart judges the write — a seat's, an ask of reviewers by anybody, or a comment or review that mentions anybody (`code.ConversationWrite.JudgesChart`) — and so wherever a read names the colleague it is made for, which rides beside a seat's asker alone — a person's read consulting no chart; and `code.checks` for a seat's rerun, which judges whether it may write the repository or wrote the change and whoever its turn works for (`code.ChecksRequest.JudgesChart`), a person's rerun and report and the engine's own records consulting none), which such a node answers `behind_config` with the hard arm's own detail EVEN TOLD TO ANSWER ANYWAY — in its server and in this node's own in-process run alike — so the last resort meets the refusal again and the walk ends unserved rather than answer from a revision that may have revoked the grant (M3-O8's hard arm; `TestEveryOperationThatJudgesAGrantIsDeclared` holds every operation on a domain's copy whose arguments carry a `code.Principal` named `Asker` to the declaration, and no git operation to it, whose git node refuses a seat itself) — and a READ a copy refuses about ITSELF (`statelog.ReadRefusal.Elsewhere`: `behind` a linearizable read's barrier, stalled, deferred, evicted, below the floor, cut off from its broker or a quorum) is asked of the next data node, the refusal the answer only where nobody does better, while `log_full`, `broker_refused` (the one stream every node appends its barrier to refused this one's, sealed or out of resources) and `maintenance`, true of every node alike, are answered at once: returned as the answer, a git fetch through a node whose applier fell a second behind was told 503 while two peers could serve it; and the walk PAYS FOR THE READ ONCE, since every node asked after a refusal is handed what the refusal says it paid for (`statelog.Spent`: the read index its barrier established, which the next node waits for rather than append its own; every position a node waited its whole bound for, a floor among them, which no later node waits for; a log a barrier ran out on, which gets no second barrier), where each node asked afresh appended a barrier to the log they were all behind on and waited a whole read budget, two seconds and a barrier per data node for one final `behind`. The failover rule is PER OPERATION CLASS — a read and a tracker write carrying its operation id move on, from an `unvouched` answer too, under the same id; a page write (whose op id the store mints per call) reported unanswered is `ErrOutcomeUnknown` and never repeated — and every request carries this node's ONE SESSION-FLOOR table, on the operation's OWN domain's log only (a wake's trigger position among them), so whichever data node answers has applied the node's own writes or says it is behind — and says so while the asker is still listening, because every wait a node makes before answering another (that floor, a read's barrier and level, a write's own record) ends `code.ReplyMargin` short of the asker's deadline (`statelog.WithReplyMargin`): a `behind` sent after the asker stopped waiting is heard as a node that is gone. Nothing answering is `ErrUnserved` (a git walk's saying it was the GIT nodes that could not answer, `ErrUnserved.Git`), never an empty answer — except the knowledge search, which is best effort and answers that it did not run — and it says whether a node it asked WENT SILENT (`Silent`), because a node that refused ran nothing and a silent one may have: a push the walk could not place is `unavailable` only when every node refused it, and `unknown` otherwise. What cannot cross a wire (the chart seams, the lead map, the searching seat's org) is supplied by the SERVING node's epoch, and three gates hold the wire honest: every field an operation's types carry arrives or is named with who supplies it, every sentinel and error type of tracker/pages/search/usage/code/statelog — and the identity estate's own (`iam.ErrNoHolder`, `iam.ErrHolderUnseated`, `iamdomain.ErrUnavailable`, `session.ErrNoCompany`) — keeps its errors.Is/As identity across it, and the envelope itself is pinned byte for byte. The IDENTITY reads a node without `data` needs are estate operations too, READ-CLASS and floored on the identity log: who holds a login (its binding row, for `iam.Holders`, `iam.OwnerOf` and a Tier A token's seat), who holds each human seat (`SeatHolders`, read whole, for the party registry's standing and a turn's withheld contacts) and who holds ONE (`SeatHolder`, two seeks on the serving node, for the person a seat's turn acts for, composed on every tool call) and the row a presented machine token is checked against (`identity.machine_token`) — the chart and the asking node's own Tier A token list applied on the ASKING side, since neither is the serving node's to know. Each crosses NARROWED: a binding without the row's verifiers and grants (`Binding`), and a token's row whole but for its VERIFIER, in whose place crosses a MAC over it and a nonce the asker drew for that question alone, under a key both nodes derive from the fleet keyring in a domain of its own (`TokenFacts`; the asker names its active entry and a node holding none runs nothing) — the asker MACs the verifier the presented token implies and takes it only where the two agree, so `credential.CheckToken` stays the one table, neither the secret nor the verifier reaches the broker, and an answer MAC'd for one question verifies under no other question's nonce; the keys ride in `Envelopes`, the one value both halves are handed. And every request and every reply is SIGNED under the fleet keyring in a domain of its own (`estate`) and judged by the verdicts a state-log record is (ADR-0031), through `signedask` — the one implementation every instruction channel shares, because the server dispatches a write AS the actor its envelope claims, and an unsigned channel beside signed records would let anything the broker admitted write as anybody — its admission vouches for a connection, never a request: a key the server does not hold answers that the request DID NOT RUN, so the router moves on, and a tampered envelope is refused and logged. A custody batch is an event envelope rather than an estate request, and stays outside the rule. A push, a compaction, an expunge record and a change's write are the KEYED operations (`op.keyedBy`: `code.propose`, `code.compact`, `code.expunge` — a person's purge request, or a maintainer's begin or commit, keyed by whichever it carries — and `code.change`, an open, an edit, a draft's state, a retarget, a close or a reopen, keyed by the repository its record names, and `code.queue`, an enqueue or a dequeue of a target's merge queue, and `code.merge`, a change's merge as one record its gate decides (ADR-0066), each keyed the same way — and beside them `code.conversation`, a write of a change's conversation, and `code.checks`, a write of a repository's checks — an attempt queued, claimed or ended — each keyed by the same repository and answered by the change writer or the checks writer beside that node's committer, raising this node's session floor but never the repository's own, since neither moves git content — all on the proposals lane, a compaction, an expunge record and a change's write waiting in the repository's one queue beside its pushes): each asked of its repository's committer — the first of `rendezvous.Order(repo id, every live data node)`, this node at its place in that order and never ahead of it, with no sticky node and the same out-of-service and silent demotions every order has — rather than of this node first, so a repository's pushes meet at one committer whichever node received them, and a committer that runs nothing (`code.ErrNotServing`: stopping, a node that publishes nothing, a refusal about itself) hands them to that repository's runner-up with the same push id — or compaction id, which the domain's ledger collapses alike — and is asked LAST for pushes for the `suspectFor` window after (no sticky node moves a keyed order off a node that refused, so every later push re-sent it its whole proposal first); even the lagging last resort asks in the key's order. A repository's GIT WORK is asked of its HOME GIT NODE instead (ADR-0059, amending ADR-0025): a second placement beside the data nodes, `RouterOptions.Git` (every live node with the git role, off the same watched view; required, an empty one the way to say there is none), for an operation declared `keyedOnGit` on `GitCopy` — no domain's copy, so no copy verdict, no dispatch floor and no lag gate, its floor travelling in its arguments as the git node's cache's sync level (`CodeSeam.FloorOf` hands it this node's own writes to that repository) — asked in `rendezvous.Order(repo id, every live git node)` with the keyed order's demotions (a `no_backend` asked last for `suspectFor`, a silent node last of all) and none of the data walk's preferences, never `remembersBehind` or the last resort; this node at its place, in process, where its git half is built (`LocalGit`, resolved PER REQUEST, because the code host is built at boot or at the first apply that hosts code, after the router and the server exist), every other git node on LANES of their own, the reads' and the engine writes' (`laneGitReads` `queue.ConcurrentAnswers` wide, since the node's seat git-work share bounds the git running; `laneGitWrites` HOLDING at most `gitWritesHeld` (64) writes for its peers, mostly through waits that run no git, and `queue.ConcurrentAnswers` wider for the answers it gives AT ONCE — a write past the held ones is answered `behind`, at its share, before its arguments are decoded, never left waiting for a slot in the broker client's buffer, which drops past its 64 MiB, where its asker heard nothing for a whole write attempt and took the busy node for a gone one; a held write holds its request, its admitted copy and its decoded write, about 3 MiB at the payload's bound, measured; registered both or neither, only where `ServerOptions.Git` is set and from boot, answering `no_backend` until the code host is built, never silence), its server's git arm after the envelope's admission and the lane check and before the copy's verdict and the readiness gates, one attempt `Timing.GitReadAttempt` (17 s: a share wait, two read budgets, the read's own git's ten seconds of run time and the reply margin, whole; the read lane's queue and the short checks of what a read names are left out and come out of the attempt), each attempt SERVED UNTIL ITS ANSWER IS DUE, a reply's margin before it ends (`code.AnswerBy`) — the git arm for a peer and this node's own half in process alike, the in-process run held to the same attempt — so every wait the read makes ends there and is answered unready while the asker still listens: served to the request's deadline and merely marked with the margin, which nothing on the git path reads, a read waiting on its share or a slot answered after its asker gave up, and the asker suspected a busy node and sent its repositories' reads to a runner-up that downloaded each. A git node that cannot answer for a reason about ITSELF (`code.ErrGitUnready`: syncing, its share, no chart, behind its configuration, a failure of its own, or no copy held) answers `behind` — `behind_config` for a seat on a node behind its configuration, the node's posture, which the asker remembers for its data operations too — with the reason beside it (`reply.Unready`), never an error on the wire, and is NOT demoted — the syncing home is the right node a moment later — and ONE node a walk FILLS a copy at a time: the walk stamps its effective home's attempt (`code.GitQuery.Home`, the first node once the demotions apply) and every other git node holding none answers `not_held` having started nothing, so a repository nobody holds costs the fleet one download, never one a git node; the fill moves to the next node of the walk only past a node bringing no copy up for the read (`code.UnreadyReason.PassesFill`: behind its configuration, a failure of its own, no code host), never past one that may be filling — kept on the first node, a home stuck behind a revision left every seat unable to read a repository it alone held — and never past a home at its share, which holds its copy and is only busy: the share is the node's, so a repository's seat reads are bounded by the shares of the nodes holding a copy of it. `Router.Home` names a repository's home — the node its reads and engine writes are asked of first, and the one its maintenance falls back to — the first node of the git walk's order with NO demotion, through the one function every keyed order is built by (`Router.keyed`), so on a quiet fleet the home and the reads' first node never disagree, and a home that went quiet is still the home, answered or the error of a placement that could not be read (`ErrNoGitNode` where it lists no git node), never a guess. A home's own work is its repositories' CHANGE WORK (`code/home`) — a repository's compaction and publication are its maintainer's (ADR-0061) — and `Router.GitOrder` answers a repository's WHOLE git order — every live git node in its rendezvous order, the home first, with no demotion — which a check's claim is ranked by on every git node alike and a running check's runner is judged present by (internal/code/checkrun); `Router.HomeOf` answers which of MANY repositories this node is the home of off ONE reading of who serves git — what the home loop's backstop pass asks once over its workers' listings, which asked one repository at a time read the presence view, decoded every node's lease and sorted the git nodes for each — three-valued: the set, or for every one the error of a placement that could not be read (`ErrNoGitNode` where it lists no git node), so a node that cannot tell does none of a home's work. `Router.GitOrders` is `GitOrder` of MANY repositories off ONE reading, for HomeOf's reason — a code search's scatter (`codesearch`) assigns every repository an asker may read, up to `config.CodeMaxRepos` of them, to the first node of its order and its runner-up to the second, and a reading a repository would read the presence view again for each and could place one scatter on a fleet that moved part way through it. `Router.Maintainer(repoID, charge)` is the git node a repository's MAINTENANCE — the compaction of its packs, the publication of its layers — is placed on: the first of the SAME order (`Router.keyed`, the rendezvous order Home reads, still with no demotion) whose ADVERTISED background budget holds the charge (each git node's `coord.GitBackgroundKey`, which its presence row carries on every beat BESIDE its status, so a beat whose status read overran still says it), else the home — the order and every figure read off ONE LISTING (`GitPlacement.Listing`, the git placement's `RouterOptions.Git`: the git nodes and what each says, read together), so a node is weighed by the listing that names it rather than ordered off one listing and weighed off the next a refresh put in its place, and a node that listing does not carry — this one included, wherever its own git half is built — is GONE, never not saying; so on alike nodes the maintainer IS the home, and on a fleet with one large git node every repository only it can hold is maintained there; NOTHING MOVES PAST THE HOME ON MISSING INFORMATION: a node whose row carries no figure is NOT SAYING, never one with nothing to give, and is passed over where it is a runner-up — kept in its place, a small runner-up not saying would take a large node's repositories, each beginning with a cold sync of its whole history only for its own admission to refuse the work — and keeps the repositories it is home to, as a quiet home keeps them, since the home holds their copies and a quiet home that cannot hold the work costs that repository its pass, refused at its admission over the copy it already holds; `Router.MaintainerOf` answers which of MANY repositories, each at its own charge, this node maintains off ONE listing for them all — what a maintainer's two passes ask once over their due listings, which asked one repository at a time read the presence view, decoded every node's lease and sorted the git nodes for each — three-valued: the set, or for every one a listing that could not be read, UNKNOWN, or one that carries no git node, `ErrNoGitNode`; `Router.Maintainer` and `MaintainerOf` judge through one function (`maintainerOver`), so the two can never disagree; and `Router.Listed` is whether that listing carries THIS node at all — by the listing, never by its git half — which a maintainer's two loops wait on before their first pass, since a booting node is listed only once its host beats. The code host reaches it through `Router.Code()` (`CodeSeam`), which raises this node's floor only for its OWN writes — a proposal that landed, a push's, a compaction's or an expunge record's, and a push it proposed found landed — never for a read's position, and raises it TWICE: the session's on the whole code log, which every routed code operation carries, and the written repository's own (`FloorOf`, `repoFloors`), which a read by object id of that repository carries and a read of any other does not — floored at the session's, a push to one repository made every other's home sync for nothing on every read. The node keeps `config.CodeMaxRepos` repositories' floors, forgetting the lowest past it, which costs a read of that repository at most the git node's one sync at the log's head. A MERGE PREVIEW (`code.merge_preview`, `opCodeMergePreview`, `CodeSeam.MergePreview`) is a git READ like a read by object id — the same walk, the reads' lane and attempt, its attempt stamped home, a node that cannot answer saying `behind` with its reason — answered by the git half's `GitServer.MergePreview` (the merge a repository's home records a change's mergeability by, `code/home`), its floor the query's own, raising none. An ENGINE WRITE (`code.git_write`, `opCodeGitWrite`, `CodeSeam.GitWrite`) walks the same git order as an `opIdempotentWrite` keyed by its push id — derived from its caller's operation, so the runner-up asked the same write after a node that ran nothing (`code.ErrNotServing`, declined and asked last) or went silent builds the same push and is answered from its row where the silent node landed it — held to the one rule every party to a write holds it to (`code.EngineWrite.Validate`) before any node is asked, and raising this node's floors only where it applied or is pending; its attempt on one git node, `Timing.GitWriteAttempt` (82 s: a share wait, three read budgets, the build's ten seconds, the upload's allowance, the proposal's own attempt and the margin, whole; every term's p99 measured beside its budget by BenchmarkEngineWrite, inside each but the share's wait, which sixty-four writes on a one-unit share reach whole and are sent on unready, and every answer's p99 3.2 s at the most), is the router's own whatever deadline its caller gives (`budgetFor`), since the git node fits every stage into it and only a wedged one costs it, and `NewRouter` refuses one no longer than the proposal's attempt and the margin. The router's attempt budgets — a read's and a write's on one data node, a git read's and an engine write's on one git node, and the admission question's whole — are ONE `Timing` (`RouterOptions.Timing`), `Production()` on every node, so a suite in another package can scale a WEDGED node's cost without reaching an unexported field (a node that is gone costs no attempt: nothing serves its subject). A shorter attempt never hears an honest node as silent — the serving node fits its waits into whatever attempt it is given, a reply's margin short of it — so `NewRouter` refuses only a field left zero by name and an attempt that cannot do its job: a read attempt no longer than `code.ReplyMargin`, which leaves the serving node no time to wait for anything or run the read, and a write attempt no longer than the floor wait, a committer's batch and its answer (`code.MinProposeAttempt`: `statelog.ReadBudget` + `code.CommitBudget` + `code.ReplyMargin`, 33 s — the domain's ONE sum of what a proposal needs, which the code host holds an engine write's upload to as well, so the two never disagree), under which a push whose committer first waited out its floor is answered busy, undecided. Production's ten-second read attempt is a linearizable read's waits at their whole budgets and the margin (2 + 5 + 2 + 1). A repository's readers are ONE PAGE A ROUTED CALL (`Usage.RepoReaders` over `usage.repo_reads`, with `usage.repo_reads_elided` on the first page; both floorless): the asker's page budget travels with the call, capped by the serving node's, every data node cuts and resumes by the one rule, a page that does not end after its cursor is refused, and a cursor or a range no read takes crosses the wire as its sentinel. A REPOSITORY LIFECYCLE REQUEST (`code.lifecycle`, `opCodeLifecycle`) is routed to any data node like a settings change and DECIDED THERE against that node's own chart and configuration (`judgesGrant`, its predicate true for every request, since every one names its actor and is judged by its grant), so a node confirmed behind refuses it `not_serving` and the asker tries another; the asker's floor is raised only by an answer that carries a record (`applied`, or `pending` at its position). `code.mark` is the one UNKEYED code write: a person's code inbox mark, asked of any data node, whose writer refuses it for anybody but the person. An ENGINE WRITE'S ATTEMPT FOLLOWS ITS KIND (`op.attemptBy`, `Timing.GitWriteAttemptFor`): the git write attempt with its build term, `code.WriteBuildDeadline`, replaced by the kind's own build budget, so a `merge_change` — landed as one merge record, its build a mergeability's thirty seconds — is given 102 s on one git node, a `rebase_change`, replaying up to a thousand commits under forty, 112 s, and every other kind its 82, read off the write each attempt of the walk carries, its home's included. |
| `rendezvous` | THE ONE ORDER A FLEET'S NODES ARE PREFERRED IN FOR A KEY — rendezvous (highest-random-weight) hashing, ADR-0008's rule applied to placement. `Order(key, nodes)` ranks every node by a 64-bit weight of the pair and nothing else exists to agree on: no table to build, publish or keep current, every node computing the same order from the same SET (a tie breaks on the node's own bytes, so the order a placement listed them in never matters), and a membership change moving only what it must — a node that leaves hands each key it ranked first to that key's own runner-up, spread across the survivors, and one that joins takes about 1/N of the keys while nothing else moves. The weight is FNV-1a over `key NUL node` FINISHED by MurmurHash3's fmix64, and the finisher is the point: plain FNV-1a ranked nodes named alike but for a last character — `node-0`…`node-4`, a StatefulSet's ordinals — by that character's low bits, so one of five nodes came first for half of all keys and every other node had ONE fixed runner-up (`node-0`'s was always `node-1`), which sent all of a failed node's load to a single neighbour. GOLDEN VECTORS pin the arithmetic, because two builds in a rolling upgrade that rank one key differently disagree about which node it prefers, and the weight is deliberately unexported, because comparing two weights anywhere else is a second order. Its caller is `estate`'s router, in TWO walks keyed on two different things. Every operation any data node answers equally is keyed on the asking node's own id, and what it takes from the order is an asker's FIRST data node and every failover target — spread evenly — and never where the asker STAYS: the router keeps asking the node that last answered until that node goes silent, so a data node that joins is asked only by askers with none yet or whose node went silent. A push — the one KEYED operation — is keyed on its REPOSITORY'S id over every live data node, the asker included where it holds the estate, and there the order is the whole decision: no node is stayed on, and its first node is the repository's committer, where every asker's pushes to it meet so one group commit holds a burst — and a committer that leaves hands each repository to that repository's own runner-up, spread over the survivors. `TestNoPackageOrdersByAHashOfItsOwn` holds it as the only implementation: any other package that ORDERS two values derived from a hash — a comparison, a `cmp`/`bytes`/`strings` compare, `min`/`max`, a sort, max or search over hash values — is rendezvous or a hash ring whatever its candidates are called, and that includes a hash SCALED by `/` or a `math` function (weighted rendezvous ranks by `-w/ln(u)`) and RAW digests in their canonical byte order (a sorted set of SHA-256 sums is a ring's points, and `bytes.Compare` over two of them is rendezvous over SHA-256), however the comparator reaches them; while a digest compared for equality or encoded into a name, a hash WRAPPED into a bucket by `%`, `&` or `math.Mod` (`search.ShardOf`), a seed or a one-sided threshold never is |
| `objstore` | The OBJECT STORE — ADR-0026 — and its vocabulary: ONE OBJECT PER UPLOAD, under a `Key` minted for that upload alone — a UUIDv7, strictly parsed, built by `KeyAt` from the store's own clock, carrying the instant it was minted and never minted twice — and an `Object` (key, the whole content's SHA-256 `Hash`, size) that a row names. A key is never derived from the content: content addressing made two files share a stored copy, and a shared copy is one a writer can be re-using at the moment the collector deletes it — so there is NO DEDUPE (a move uploads its bytes again; the seat's own write skips content the file already holds) and NO LOCK. What is divided off is the BYTES and only the bytes: that a file exists, its path, its hash and its object's key are replicated rows like everything else, so listing a project's files never leaves the node. The bytes live in ONE `Backend` the whole fleet shares (`Put`, `Get`, `Stat`, `Delete`, `List`, plus `Pending`/`Abandon` for the uploads it began and never finished, every verb certified by `objstoretest`) — a DUMB, STREAMING store of opaque names: a put reads a reader to its END — `io.EOF` itself, through `Fill`, the one rule every reader in pieces here follows, never a body cut short's `io.ErrUnexpectedEOF`, which `io.ReadFull` answers for its own short read too and so stored the half of an upload that arrived as the whole — and leaves nothing anybody can find when it fails or is abandoned, a get hands back a reader and REFUSES a context with no deadline rather than pick a bound of its own, and a listing hands back every name it holds verbatim and never one it cannot DATE (`ErrUndated`), because what a name means is this package's grammar and the collector's judgement and never a backend's. Every name the engine writes is `files/<key>` (`Key.Name`), so a shared bucket's other objects are never the engine's. It is wrapped in a `Store` that mints the key, counts and hashes every byte on the way in (the size limit and a cancelled context reaching the backend as a FAILED READ, the one failure every backend cleans up after), and on the way out checks every byte against the row and HOLDS BACK THE BYTE THAT COMPLETES THE FILE until the whole is verified — so a download of a damaged object ends short rather than whole, and `ErrCorrupt` is only a DEFINITE answer, never a read that failed first. A read is bounded by `ReadBudget` and ended by `Stall` when its backend stops moving — or has begun no stream, since the backend's open is a wait on it like any Read and a backend asking a failed request again until its deadline would otherwise hold a read its whole budget; and a WRITE the backend stops taking is ended at the same minute by the backend that sends it, the only one that can see it move (`ErrStalled` either way: the store failing, never a refusal); a page (`ReadAt`) is checked for its length only. `RecordWithin` (12 h) is the bound on the write that names a key, which is what lets the collector delete with no lock. `AssumedClockSkew` (1 h) is the most a node's clock, or the clock of the broker member leading the position register, may stand from the fleet's — the median of the data nodes' clocks; the engine's `clock_skew` raises past it — so two machines may disagree by TWICE it, the reading every rule that judges one machine's instant by another machine's clock is held to: the collector's grace against `RecordWithin`, and a retired reference's against its longest read, since the node sweeping a retired row and the broker member that stamped its retirement are each held only to the fleet's clock. `MiBPace` is the ONE floor a transfer is held to on every leg — the API's upload and download pacing, a bucket request, an object read — so no bound can be retuned out from under another. A backend that lists its names IN ORDER implements `Ranged` (`ListRange(after, through)`, certified by `objstoretest`, which refuses an uncertified one), and `Store.ListKeys` reads it as keys and CHECKS the order rather than trusting it (`ErrUnordered`), because every caller reads an object missing from a range as one the store does not hold; `Key.Compare` is the order of the canonical spelling. One that deletes many names in one request implements `BatchDeleter` (`DeleteBatch`, at most `MaxDeleteBatch` (1,000) names — S3's DeleteObjects — certified the same way), read through `Store.DeleteKeys`, which counts what the store says it removed (`DeleteBatchError` names each name it refused) and answers `ErrNoBatch` for a store that refused the request itself, a gateway without the verb, so its caller deletes each name instead. Two OPTIONAL CAPABILITIES a backend may advertise rather than verbs every backend must answer: `Presigner` (the s3 backend, once its probe proved the provider enforces the bindings), through which `Store.PresignPut` signs a client's own put of a key the store minted (`Store.Mint`), bound to length, type, SHA-256 and `If-None-Match: *`, ending inside `RecordWithin` and at most `MaxPresignTerm` (`PresignTerm` sizes it), and answering `ErrDirectUnavailable` everywhere else; and `Reserver` (the nats bucket), whose `Usage` the store reports. A put that bucket refuses because its reservation is spent is `ErrFull` — and so is one an S3-compatible bucket's provider refuses for want of room — a REFUSAL rather than a failure, answered only inside a `Full` that carries the BACKEND'S OWN remedy — the room is made where the backend keeps its bytes, which only the backend knows: every surface refusing an upload for it says so in its own vocabulary and splices in that `Remedy`, keeps the store's own words (its bucket, its keys) for the log, and never invites the retry the full store answers the same. `ReferenceTable` is how a consumer DECLARES a table that names objects, its two statements decoded once beside them, and the declarations live in `objstore/references` rather than beside either end, because the collector deletes whatever no declared table names. A Required declaration also names the LOG that writes it, the table its domain DEFERS that log's unreadable records into, and the domain's own reader of ONE RECORD (`Records`, sharing the applier's decoder, the row spelled with `JoinOwner` exactly as the walk spells it), because a backup carries what the log a restore replays names (ADR-0052); `ErrRecordUnreadable` is a newer build's record that may name an object, and `ValidateLog` refuses a Required table missing any of the three. The KEYSET WALK of a declared table (`WalkOf`, a `WalkPage` of 500 rows a read, each reference carrying its table's standing) and the K-WAY MERGE of every walk into one key order (`MergeWalks`, one page of each table held, a key two rows name handed on adjacent) are here too, once, for every pass that reads them — the audit, the backup and a store move. A declaration's STANDING is one of three, ADR-0051, with no default: `Required` (kept from collection, audited, copied, counted and asked after by a backup, copied by a store move), `Retired` (kept for its domain's grace and nothing else) and `Derived` (kept while a row names it and copied by a store move, never audited, never copied, counted or asked after by a backup — an object the engine can build again, so it owes no grace, and a backup taken again and again would pay its bytes every time to spare a rare rebuild, where a move made once would leave every node of the moved fleet to rebuild it); the obligations are one table at `Standing`'s doc, the collector asks every table, and each walk is built for its `Pass` (`PassAudit`, `PassBackup`, `PassMove`), whose `Reads` is the table's walk columns: the audit's and the backup's read `Required` alone and refuse the other two, a store move's reads `Required` and `Derived` and refuses a retired table, whose rows no pass walks (`ReferencesAfter` refuses it). The first `Derived` objects are a repository's PUBLISHED LAYERS (ADR-0061): built once by its maintainer, installed by every git node only once checked against their rows, and published again after a restore, since no backup carries one |
| `objstore/natsobj` | The DEFAULT backend: a JetStream object store bucket (`crewlet_files`, stream `OBJ_crewlet_files`) on the fleet's own broker, in NATS's own documented format so `nats object get` reads it, created through `jsprovision` at `stream.replicas` with a BYTE RESERVATION (`store.objects.nats_max_bytes`, or the engine's derived share: the smaller of a quarter of the broker's limit and what the state logs' five eighths and a 1 GiB coordination floor leave), because a bucket growing into the broker remainder the leases and ledgers live in would stop the fleet when full, where a full bucket refuses only uploads (`objstore.ErrFull`, inside an `objstore.Full` naming `FullRemedy`: move the files to a bucket, raise the reservation on an external broker — an embedded one keeps the ceiling it was created with — or let the collector remove what nothing names); a bucket holding no ceiling is refused naming the `nats stream update` that bounds it, an existing one keeps the ceiling it was granted, `Usage` feeds the bucket gauges and `objects_bucket_near_ceiling`, and `Seal` makes it read-only for good once `crewlet objects migrate --to s3` has moved the fleet off it. It advertises no presign. So the files survive exactly what the logs survive, on the same members, a node without `data` reaches it across its leaf link, and a backup carries it as one more stream snapshot. What it costs is that every member holding a copy holds every object; a company whose files outgrow a member's disk takes `s3obj`. NOTHING GOES THROUGH THE LIBRARY'S OBJECT-STORE VERBS. A PUT is the backend's own, in the library's format: pieces of `MessageBytes` (128 KiB) published in order from one buffer — nats.go copies a payload at the publish, and only a re-publish reads it again — with no publish retry (one re-sent from the library's single reused message lands another piece's bytes, out of order), holding a place in the CONNECTION's window for every message out — one window every put on the connection shares, since the connection is the one the node's leases and events ride and a window a put let K puts hold K of it: `linkWindow` across a leaf's link or to an external cluster — as many messages as a link at the floor carries in one heartbeat of the lease IN FORCE (`Config.LeaseHeartbeat`, the TTL the engine resolved over `seat.HeartbeatRatio`, which `coordination.lease_ttl_seconds` moves), at least one and at most `memberWindow`: four, half a mebibyte in fifteen seconds, at the shipped 45 s lease, one under 22.5 s, which runs a fast leaf link at about half its throughput (a lone 16 MiB put 47–83 MB/s against 120–139 with four) — so a lease renewal queued behind the node's uploads still lands with an interval of its lease to spare (a test holds that over several TTLs against `seat`'s constants; sized for the shipped heartbeat alone, a 15 s lease waited 20 s behind the window at the floor and lapsed), a lease under `config.LinkLeaseFloor` (11.25 s), whose heartbeat even one message outlasts at the floor, being warned of by `crewlet validate` — and `memberWindow` (16) on a member's own client, where no link lies between, sized by throughput (eight puts sharing it ran equal within the spread to sixteen each, and sixty-four bought nothing) — a place given first come first served, a put waiting for one ended only when the connection has had nothing acknowledged for `objstore.Stall`; ended only when the broker has acknowledged nothing of it for `objstore.Stall` while pieces are out (`objstore.ErrStalled`), no message waiting behind more than the window, and then drained and purged — metadata first where it was published, then pieces — within `purgeWait` (5 s, the broker API's own timeout) each, every failure, the caller's context ending included, reaching it as a failed read cleaned up after with a live context — because the library's put failed any message unacknowledged five seconds after it was published, so a put across a link slower than its queued bytes in five seconds failed while moving (2 MiB at 1.25× the floor: 10 s), and on a frozen TCP link filled the socket until the write deadline took the node's connection down; the metadata, with `Nats-Rollup: sub`, last, once every piece is acknowledged; no lookup first, a key being never reused; one publisher a put, cleaned up. Everything else goes through the stream, because the library's other verbs cannot be made safe: metadata is read from the stream LEADER (`jsapi.Leader`, the one implementation coordination's listings use too) since the library's info read is a direct get a lagging replica answers "not found" — every leader read here, the stream info a usage reading and a seal begin with and each page of the sweep's piece listing among them, asked again while nobody answers it, and a read whose answer is a whole piece given that piece's crossing only on a node that reaches the bucket across a leaf link (`jsapi.Leader.Carrying`), where a re-ask would put another piece on a link the first is still crossing; a get is an ordered consumer of the object's own messages read under `objstore.Stall` and ended by its context — the library's reader holds the lock its close needs, and bounds a context with no deadline by five seconds for the WHOLE download — and a ranged get walks message HEADERS to its offset; a delete is a PURGE of the metadata and then of the pieces, never the library's delete MARKER, which no later put of a never-reused name would replace; `Pending` lists the piece subjects no metadata names — through the leader's subject listing, a page at a time, never a walk of the pieces, which is linear in the objects but reads the bucket's bytes (every block holding an object's last piece, and its start reads each block's per-subject state under the stream's store lock) where the listing reads only the in-memory subject index: measured, two thousand 128 KiB objects walked in 103 ms against 6.3 ms on empty messages, the listing 2.2 ms on either; the listing's sort makes a page 4.22 s at three million objects, so a deadline-less sweep's five-second page is outgrown at about three and a half million, where every sweep after a node's first asks the page once and fails. Its listing is an ordered consumer of its own over the metadata subjects, ending on the pending count every message carries — never the library's `List`, which gathers the company's whole inventory into one slice, nor its Watch, which never finishes when the newest metadata cannot be decoded. A CONSUMER'S END IS ITS REPLICA'S, never the stream's: the broker places a consumer on any replica it counts as up, one still catching up after a restart included, whose count says nothing follows where the leader holds more — so a get whose consumer ends short of the object's size, a range walk short of its offset and a consumer made with nothing to deliver ask the LEADER what follows (`jsapi.Leader.Next`) and go on from a consumer made again (a quiet second apart after the first, failing once behind for `objstore.Stall`), and a listing reads the leader's newest metadata first and FAILS where its walk ended short of it, rather than name fewer objects: taken for the end, a get read a whole pack back as zero bytes, which the store calls damaged, and `Pending` handed the collector a finished upload's pieces to abandon. Metadata that does not decode is no object and still a NAME: a stat and a get answer it `objstore.ErrCorrupt`, a definite answer, and the listing hands on the name its subject spells, holding nothing, so the collector judges and clears it like any other. The bucket is looked up and created ONCE, through `jsprovision`'s `Place`, never through the library's create-or-update, whose UPDATE goes unanswered on a stream whose group has not elected; a bucket held at fewer copies than `stream.replicas` refuses the node. A get holds `readAhead` (4) messages in hand and a listing pulls `walkAhead` (500) metadata messages at a time. A read whose consumer the broker TOOK AWAY under it is given a replacement once two of `pullHeartbeat`'s heartbeats go missing, and the heartbeat is DERIVED — a hand at the `MiBPace` floor, fifteen seconds — because a compressed link (a leaf's, by default) delivers what the member flushed at once as ONE BLOCK, so a healthy read on a slow link hears nothing, heartbeats included, until its whole hand arrives, and one second replaced every such read before anything arrived until it failed having read nothing; the hand went from eight to four so that two heartbeats and a replacement's first hand still fit `objstore.Stall`. Nor is a read that hears nothing for `quietWait` (1 s) left to them: at its FIRST message it ASKS ITS CONSUMER and remakes one that holds no pull and has delivered nothing — the pull a clustered fleet drops at a consumer's creation, one get in 25 to one in 1,500 with the machine's load, measured — up to `firstPullRemakes` (3) times, asked rather than timed since a read remade while its hand is on the wire waits behind the hand it threw away; at every LATER message it simply waits again, because nats.go decides whether to send its next pull only as a wait begins and now and then skips it (one 1 MiB get in ten to twenty thousand on one member stalled two heartbeats). What it costs at THREE REPLICAS is measured by `objstore/natsobj/replicated`, a package of tests alone, apart because forming the three-member cluster puts a package in the solo half and this one's whole suite would go with it |
| `objstore/s3obj` | The S3 backend, over the AWS SDK because SigV4 and the regional endpoint rules are what a hand-rolled client gets wrong: any S3-compatible bucket, keys `<prefix>files/<key>`, path-style for MinIO and most gateways, static keys or the SDK's own chain, `AWS_CA_BUNDLE` added to the roots for the bucket's requests only. it is a failure the request is GIVEN UP on at once, by its KIND (`attempts{stalled}`) — the watch and the minute of failures below reading ONE stall bound — and the client speaks HTTP/1.1 alone — offered in the TLS handshake too, since a clone of a transport that has spoken HTTP/2 offers `h2` and a gateway taking it is read as a malformed answer — so a socket is one request's; and a THROTTLE slows every reader and writer of the node's `files/` prefix through ONE `gate`, a step round each attempt after the SDK's retry step, rather than failing them once a quota drains: a limit on ATTEMPTS IN FLIGHT, not a rate — cut to `throttleShare` (0.7, CUBIC's β) of the node's WIDTH — the most attempts it has had out at once since its last cut, which the cut then sets to the limit it leaves — ONCE A ROUND (a throttle to an attempt let out before the last cut cuts nothing more), grown by one a round of answers, first come first served, and LIFTED after `throttleQuiet` (a minute) without a throttle, by ONE timer the throttle that engages it sets (lifted only as an attempt entered or left, a gate whose places were all held by attempts that had stopped answering kept its waiters until their own deadlines) — because the SDK's adaptive mode limited a rate it had to measure, so a fresh client's first throttle dropped it to half a request a second (a burst of 500 heads by 32 loops at 150 ms: 9.6–9.7 s, against 2.72–2.74 through the gate and 2.43 unthrottled, `BenchmarkAThrottledBurst`), and a cut of what happened to be OUT when the throttle came back left a node throttled on a lone HEAD between two syncs ONE place (its next burst 4.99–5.00 s, against 2.72–2.73 cut from its width; Every caller but the API's files is a closed loop of its own width; a request the bucket answers with nothing but failures — server errors that are no throttle, or no answer at all — is GIVEN UP within a minute of its first (`judge.giveUpAfter`, the client's one stall bound, `objstore.Stall`, the minute a read already waits), at a BOUND OF ITS OWN drawn evenly from the minute's last twenty seconds as the failures begin, its last attempt sent at that bound — one bound for every request sent the last attempt of every write that failed together in one instant, on the minute — after nine attempts at the median and twelve at the 95th percentile at a 100 ms first byte (`TestAFailingRequestsLastAttemptsAreSpreadOverTheirBounds`, seeded), its caller told how long and the bucket's last answer, while one answered throttles is held by the gate until its deadline — because with no count of attempts a person's part asked a failing bucket again for its whole sixteen-and-a-half-minute deadline; a throttle answered to the request itself starts the span again, and S3 Express's session request is judged the same way, its waiter failing with it. A certificate the node does not trust is final at once rather than asked again until the deadline; and a request its deadline ends names the bucket's last answer beside it, as text. Every attempt is counted as `crewlet.objects.s3.attempts` and every request as `.request.attempts`, by S3 operation (a closed set of twelve, `CreateSession` among them and no `HeadBucket`, which the open's probe replaced) and outcome, and its gate as `.gate.limit` and `.gate.waiting`, set only when they change — the outcomes read by the gate's own predicates, so what the counter calls a throttle is what cut the gate, and a request that never got its turn counted `unsent` at zero attempts, because the retries an operator reads are attempts less requests plus those. A put streams without knowing its length: under `PartBytes` (8 MiB) it is one PutObject, past it a multipart upload of equal parts with up to `partsInFlight` (4) IN FLIGHT AT ONCE while the next is read and hashed, each from a buffer of its own — the upload's first, then spares from ONE allowance the node's uploads share (`spareParts`), taken only while one is free, so a fifth large upload at once goes a part at a time rather than waiting for memory — ended AT ONCE by the first part that fails (the rest cancelled, the body read no further), and abandoned, once every part sent has answered, under a context the caller's cannot end on any failure, because an upload nobody completes or aborts is billed and listed by nothing — and one a killed process left is what `Pending` (ListMultipartUploads) hands the collector. A read that may run past a part is the mirror of a put: in-order `PartBytes` RANGES — the first streamed straight through, so an object of a part or less is the one request and the memory it always was, and its answer says how many follow; every later one held by If-Match to the first one's ETag, so an object written over mid-read fails the read (412) rather than stitching two objects, and an object with no tag is refused past its first range; read ahead into buffers from the SAME spare allowance the uploads draw on, never waited for, and only AS MANY AS THE READER KEEPS BUSY — one to begin with, up to `partsInFlight` while the reader waits on the bucket (one that waited a share f of its time on a range would have kept 1/(1−f) times the connections busy; judged once half the first range is handed out and at every range's end), one given back when a reader that never waited on a range finds the next one read ahead landed whole (the ranges asked for at its old pace withdrawn once its ranges take longer than the lead they were asked with) — because a read that took four whatever its reader's pace let three downloads paced to people's links hold the node's whole allowance for hours (a read that finds none free streams its ranges, taking one as soon as one is free); every range's REQUEST sent `askAhead` (two) first bytes at the reader's pace before its answer is needed, the answer waiting on its connection rather than in a buffer, because a range asked for only once its buffer was free left a whole first byte between ranges, which held a wide read under its connections and a streaming one under a single stream — one range waiting so for each buffer, and at most twice `partsInFlight` ranges a read, the one it streams among them, so a read has at most twice `partsInFlight` requests at the bucket; handed out a piece at a time as it lands, because a range handed over only whole is minutes of silence at the floor and `objstore.Stall` ends a read that hears nothing for one; a range that fails ends every range after it at once (each range's context is the next one's parent), and the read asks for nothing more; Close cancels every range, waits for each and gives its buffers back before it returns. Every range of an empty object answers 416, read as the empty stream. `ListRange` is ListObjectsV2 from StartAfter, ending at the page that holds the range's end, and an S3 Express DIRECTORY bucket (`…--x-s3`), which lists in no order, answers `ErrUnordered` without a request. `LastModified` of an object put in parts is when its upload BEGAN, which is why `objstore.Info.Written` promises only an instant between a put's start and its end. A batch deletion is ONE quiet DeleteObjects carrying a Content-MD5 beside the SDK's checksum, because gateways implemented the MD5 the API long required; a request refused as a whole — a client error but a throttle, or 501, which is how a gateway without the verb answers — is `objstore.ErrNoBatch`, while a throttle or a server error is the bucket failing. `s3fake` is an in-process S3 the suite runs against, and it routes on the QUERY before the method, since a multipart upload is spelled with the same methods as a plain put; it holds a get's If-Match to the object's ETag as S3 does, and can be made as far away (`Latency`) and as narrow (`ConnBytesPerSecond`) as a provider, and to throttle or fail its next requests (`FailNext`), every request for a span of time (`FailFor`) or every session (`RefuseSessions`) as one does, and to STOP as one does — a kind of request taking so much of its body and then nothing, with no answer (`Hang`), its headers and nothing after (`HangAnswer`), or a body read at a pace of its own, fast at first if a case says so (`BodyBytesPerSecond`, `BodyFastBytes`) — and served offering HTTP/2 (`StartH2`); it speaks S3 Express's CreateSession, so a case opens a real directory bucket (`…--x-s3`) rather than pointing a client at one's name; `RealBackend` is the bucket `CREWLET_BENCH_S3_*` names, for a benchmark's real arm, under a prefix of its own that is cleared after it |
| `objstore/memobj`, `objstore/objstoretest` | The in-memory twin and the ONE suite every backend runs (`queuetest`'s idiom): an object reads back exactly, an empty one and large ones across the backend's own pieces and ranges included; a put whose reader fails — with `io.ErrUnexpectedEOF` or a wrapped `io.EOF` too, inside a piece or at its end — or whose context ends leaves nothing a stat or a listing can find, and fails with the reader's own error; a read with no deadline is refused and one whose context ends stops; a missing name is `ErrNotFound`, and a delete leaves nothing and is idempotent; a listing names every object once, verbatim, however slowly it is read, and stops at its visitor's error; an upload begun and never finished is `Pending` until `Abandon`ed; a put the backend stops taking (`Stalling`, where a backend can be made to stop) fails as `ErrStalled` between its stall and its clean-up and leaves nothing; and a name holding something the backend cannot read as an object (`Spoil`, where a backend can hold one) is `ErrCorrupt` to a stat and a get, listed holding nothing, and cleared by a delete — a twin that agreed only with itself would certify nothing |
| `objstore/collect` | The one fleet duty the object store needs (`object-collector`): an hourly COLLECTION that pins the estate, lists the store and sorts every name by its SPELLING — an object's key under `files/`, or somebody else's, never touched — and deletes a key's object only when it is past a day's grace by BOTH the backend's clock and the key's own minting instant and no declared table names it in an estate read COMPLETELY, with NO LOCK (ADR-0027); and SWEEPS what no listing shows, the uploads a backend began and never finished (`Pending`/`Abandon`), past the same grace — a sweep the backend refuses is reported beside the collection, never failing it. A daily AUDIT reads the references a page per estate read, on one goroutine, every required table's keyset walk (`Source.Required` — never a Retired or Derived table's, whose objects the company is not owed, though the collection keeps theirs as it keeps a Required one's;
