a store move walks `Source.Moved` instead, the Required and the Derived tables, which `Sources` sorts by `objstore.Pass.Reads` as it sorts the audit's) MERGED into one key order, and on a store that lists in order (`objstore.Ranged`, S3) asks about a RANGE at a time — cut to fill `rangeChunk` (900) names, S3's 1,000-key page less a tenth (`rangePageRoom`) for a range fuller than the ones before it, at the density the ranges already listed MEASURED (`auditor.cut`, an eighth of the way a range, `rangeDensityWeight`; the first range listed before the second is cut), so a range fits one request whatever the bucket keeps among the live rows' keys — a retired or derived row's objects, garbage — and holds at most 900 references, the ranges tiling the key space, one listing answering what a HEAD of each would, and a range whose listing fails taking its second look as ONE listing more rather than a HEAD of each — and where it keeps no order (`nats`, a directory bucket, and the rest of a walk once a range is answered out of order) holds them against the store's whole LISTING a window at a time — `auditWindow` (100 000) keys, the smallest above the last window's, at 127 bytes a key (about 13 MiB on the node running the duty) — judging every reference in a window from what it lists, so an audit of N objects lists N × ⌈N / 100 000⌉ names, a cost that grows with the square of the store past one window (on `nats` a million objects are ten listings and two to three minutes, against a read of the stream's leader an object), and asking BY NAME only about a name listed holding nothing (metadata that does not decode, a name only a delete marker holds) and, once a listing FAILS, about every reference after it, since a window's extent is its listing's answer; it keeps up to `auditConcurrency` (32) questions — a listing or a stat — out at once OUTSIDE every read while the walk reads on; a collection hands a store that deletes in batches (S3) each judged batch's unnamed keys in ONE request, `batchesInFlight` (1) at a time because a batch's keys are taken to spend the prefix's writes one each, and otherwise keeps up to `deleteConcurrency` (16) deletions in flight beside its listing — CLOSED LOOPS, a request sent only when one in flight has been answered, so a slower store is asked more slowly by itself, and a pass returns only once every request it made has ended; an object not found is asked again before it is MISSING — at the end of the pass, or sooner once `maxSuspects` (100,000) others are waiting for theirs, so a store that lost everything never has the audit hold the company's whole inventory — and one held at another size or digest, or as no object the backend can read at all (`objstore.ErrCorrupt` from a stat, a definite answer rather than a failure), is DAMAGED; ONE OBJECT IS NEVER THE AUDIT'S FAILURE — a question the store fails is set aside for its second look, and an object it fails on both is UNANSWERED once the store answers a question after it, while the audit fails only when the STORE does (`failedInARow`, 32, with none answered between, or a last run of failures whose confirming question about an object the store had answered for fails too) — each named as the FILE that holds it, and all three raise `objects_missing`. The report keeps the last audit that ran to its end (`found`) beside the last attempt, and a new duty holder `Restore`s both halves from the fleet's record, so neither a failed audit, a moved duty nor a record the coordination store did not give up clears the alarm — that last runs no pass until the record is read, since an unknown taken for "nothing recorded" wrote the findings out of it — and only from a record of THIS store, read whole, through the ONE reader the status surfaces read it by too (`engine.ObjectCollection`): the record outlives a move of the fleet's files, and another store's report is none, so the new store is collected and audited at once, and `/fleet` shows no report until then rather than the old store's under its name. Nothing here decides where an object lives or repairs one — the backend keeps its own copies. The inventory is DERIVED from the estate, never kept beside it, judged `JudgeBatch` (500) keys to a statement over the declaration's own query — exported so a declaring domain's plan test plans the question at the width it is asked, since each width is a statement the driver plans on its own |
| `objstore/migrate` | One pass of `crewlet objects migrate --to s3`: every object a Required table names AND every one a Derived table names (`collect.Source.Moved`, `objstore.PassMove`, ADR-0051) is read verified through `Store.Open` and put into the target backend under the same name, then checked there at its size (and digest, where the target keeps one), `Copies` (4) at a time — a derived object because the moved fleet would otherwise rebuild it on every node at a cost that grows with everything it was built from, where the copy costs its bytes once; a Retired table's object stays behind, since no reader that has not caught up outlives the restart onto the target. Resumable by running it again, because a key never names other bytes and the target is the record of what was copied; a file the source cannot give back whole is reported by name, never stored, and a derived object it cannot give back is counted apart (`Report.Derived`) and holds nothing up, since the engine builds it again. It moves no record: the engine's final pass, in maintenance mode, seals the nats bucket and moves the fleet's recorded backend (`coord.ObjectStores.MoveObjectBackend`) |
| `objstore/references` | The ONE LIST of tables that name objects, read by the collector's references and by the backup, and held against the replicated schema IN BOTH DIRECTIONS by a gate: a column named `object` that nobody declared is a table whose files are deleted a day after they were written — and every Required entry is held to name its log, a deferred-record table the schema has, and its reader of one record, with `engine`'s gate holding the log and the table to the registered domain's own. It also holds the one test that pins the lock-free rule's arithmetic — `RecordWithin` plus twice `objstore.AssumedClockSkew` (two nodes, each within it of the fleet's clock `clock_skew` measures them against, the median of the data nodes') under the collector's grace, and the largest object any declaring domain stores — a file of `tracker.MaxFileBytes`, a pack bundle of `code.MaxPackBytes`, a part of a published layer of `code.LayerPartBytes`, an artifact's file or asset of `artifacts.MaxTextFileBytes` — at the `MiBPace` floor under `RecordWithin` — because it is the one place every one of those constants is importable. `TestEveryDeclarationStatesItsStanding` holds each entry's standing to a reviewed row with its reason, two-sided, so a standing is a reviewed decision rather than a field its author filled in, and a Retired entry is further held to a reviewed grace and sweep while a Derived one owes neither; the package doc says why a backup leaves a Derived table's objects out and a store move copies them. The first Derived table is `code_layer_parts`, the parts of the code host's published multi-pack-index layers. And it reviews the RETIRED half: `code_packs_retired` is the first retired table, held in `retirements` to its grace (`code.RetireGrace`), its largest object (`code.MaxPackBytes`) and what sweeps it — the code-retention duty's `retire_sweep` records, which the engine's maintenance list could never name, so the gate admits a duty whose own engine test holds it — its grace above `objstore.ReadBudget` of that object plus twice `objstore.AssumedClockSkew`, since the broker member that stamped a retirement need not be the one the sweeping node's clock is measured against. A package of its own because the list belongs to neither the consumer nor the collector |
| `artifacts` | The artifacts VOCABULARY, values only, the way `iam` is the identity vocabulary: ids and the host label (the id's own bytes in lowercase Crockford base32, never a keyed digest, so a keyring rotation cannot change an artifact's origin), kinds, capabilities, the role ladder (viewer, commenter, contributor, editor, owner; a role is a RELATION, never a permission), audiences and shares, manifests and the script policy the linter derives, THE ONE LIMITS TABLE (every bound with its reason, and the Tier A plan quotas a deciding node stamps into a record), the authority stamp a datastore write carries, upload intents, view ticket claims, frames, datastore op and schema shapes, and presence frames. Imported by `authz` and `config`, so it imports no store, no broker and no clock. The ordering of roles is `authz`'s, the label codec and id derivation `artifactsdomain`'s, each in a named file of this package |
| `artifacts/manifest` | Pure: the path grammar of a version (the engine's one file-path rule), reserved paths, whole and patch publishes, carry planning and the canonical manifest encoding. CONTENT ADDRESSING LIVES AT THE MANIFEST, never in an object key: keys stay random, one per upload, which is what lets the collector delete without a lock, and a carry never crosses an artifact (a company-wide hash lookup is an existence oracle) |
| `artifacts/mediatype` | Pure: the ONE extension table an artifact may publish (served type, class, compressible, scriptable). The served type is always the table's, never a client's |
| `artifacts/slideformat` | Pure: the slide format a `slides` deck holds (Claude's, without `project/`): the `deck.json` index and one `<section>` per slide on a 1920 by 1080 stage, read with the HTML tokenizer into ONE CANONICAL TREE the publish is held to and the dashboard draws from, so one parser decides what a slide is. A CLOSED SUBSET, REFUSED RATHER THAN DROPPED: the element, attribute and inline-style tables with a value grammar per property, a drawing subset that references only its own ids (`svgsafe`'s allowlist, the one a brand logo is held to), images and faces only as files of the version, Claude's bounds, every finding naming the file, the line and the rule. Its lists are exported for the dashboard's renderer, which a gate holds equal |
| `artifacts/svgsafe` | Pure: the ONE drawing allowlist both an inline slide `<svg>` (read by `slideformat` with the HTML tokenizer) and a standalone `.svg` (a brand logo) are held to: shapes, paths, text, gradients, clips, masks, markers and filter primitives, no script, event attribute, `style`, `<style>`, `foreignObject`, `<a>`, `<image>` or animation, every value free of `javascript:`, `data:`, `<`, `expression` and `@import`, every reference to an id of the same drawing. The standalone front end is encoding/xml in STRICT mode with no entity table, refusing any DOCTYPE or ENTITY, any processing instruction but the XML declaration, `xml:base`, a non-UTF-8 encoding and every namespace but SVG's and XLink's, matching names by their exact SVG spelling, bounding elements, depth and what `<use>` expands to (a use cycle refused), and requiring a viewBox between 1:8 and 8:1. WHAT IS STORED IS ITS RE-SERIALISATION, never the uploaded bytes: attributes sorted, everything escaped, no comment, no instruction and one namespace declaration, so a parser differential between Go's reader and a browser's has nothing to hide in. Refused with the line and the fix, never silently stripped |
| `artifacts/brandformat` | Pure: the format a `brand` kit holds (`brand.json` and the logo and face files it names) and everything an adoption makes of it. The index is decoded STRICTLY (unknown keys, a key twice in any object, a wrong type and anything after the object refused, each finding at its line), every field to its rule (plain text with no control, format character, markup or link, `#rrggbb` colours, exactly one source per face, a licence attestation for an uploaded family). Assets are stored in the engine's form (`Sanitize`: an SVG through `svgsafe`, a PNG read chunk by chunk with its stream inflated row by row to exactly the rows its header promises and metadata chunks dropped, a WebP without EXIF or XMP, a WOFF2 held by its header), and an adoption re-sanitizes what it copies. Contrast is WCAG 2's on the colours the kit paints (`Fallbacks`), only text on the light ground refusing an adoption. THE ONE STYLESHEET GENERATOR (`CSS`): `--brand-*` inputs on `:root` and `@font-face` rules, nothing else, every value re-checked as it is written and every URL `/_crewlet/brand/<sha256>.<ext>`, fuzzed against the injection corpus. Also the canonical index an adoption snapshots, the served extensions, the face lists the dashboard contract is gated to, and the slide theme a deck begun in the brand copies |
| `artifacts/canvasformat` | Pure: a `design`'s `canvas.json`: where each artboard of the version sits (boards keyed by their page's path, with position and size), the order they are drawn in, and the titles and stickies beside them. A CLOSED FORMAT, REFUSED RATHER THAN REPAIRED: decoded strictly (an unknown field is refused, not dropped), every number to its bound, and held to the version so every board is an HTML page of it and every HTML page a board, every problem answered at once at its place in the file. Also the outline the gallery and the agents read and the comment anchor (an artboard point, D65). No runtime of its own: an artboard is a plain page under the html contract (D59) |
| `artifacts/dashspec` | Pure: the format a `dashboard` board holds (`dashboard.json`): filters as closed choice sets, datasets from four sources (`store`, `engine`, `file`, `calc`), widgets drawn by uilet primitives on the twelve-column grid, the ENGINE SOURCE CATALOGUE (named reads over the tracker, spend and agents with typed parameters and fixed columns), the closed calculation steps (deterministic: no clock, no locale, a total order), file datasets parsed and held to their declared columns, refresh, the outline a cover draws and the comment anchor. It reads no store and asks no domain: what answers an engine source, AS THE VIEWER, is the API's (`adr/0043`). A board outside the format is refused at publish with every finding, never dropped on read |
| `artifactsdomain` | The `artifacts` state-log domain (`CREWLET_ARTIFACTS_LOG`), strict and claiming identity, its health NOT a readiness input (a seat reads no artifact on its turn path): artifacts, versions and canonical manifests, the objects each artifact names (`artifact_objects`, refcounted, and `artifact_assets`, both declared in `objstore/references`), shares, pins and opened marks, a year of history and the company's usage counters. EVERY RECORD THAT MOVES AN OBJECT REFERENCE ARBITRATES ON THE ARTIFACT SUBJECT, and the applier writes a carried entry only when its own transaction finds the object named by THIS artifact and an uploaded key only when no other row names it, so an unnamed key is never named again and no carry crosses an artifact. RETENTION RIDES THE RECORD (a hundred unlabelled versions plus every labelled one). QUOTAS ARE STAMPED into the record and enforced by the applier from the stamp. A REFUSAL IS A HISTORY ROW, never an error, and the writer reads its own verdict back by operation id; the applier applies no rule a later build may change (the media table, the grammar and the limits are the writer's). A purge runs its dependents first, step by step. Each committed batch hands the engine what it moved (`Engine.OnArtifactsMoved`). THE FOLDER TREE IS ONE SUBJECT AND A FOLDER GRANTS NOTHING (ADR-0047): an artifact sits in at most one folder, a folder is seen through its top-level folder's owner and shares, filing an artifact rides its own subject, a removal moves what it held up a level in one record, and the listing (S3 `prefix`/`delimiter`), its total and every folder count share one visibility predicate. THE ADOPTED BRAND IS A SNAPSHOT (ADR-0048): an administrator's adoption on the one `brand` subject copies one kit version's canonical index, its generated stylesheet and every logo and face into objects its own record uploaded, so the kit's retention, trash and purge never reach it; a framed version pins the stylesheet adopted at its publish (naming the brand in its scope), and a superseded adoption's files are pruned once no retained version pins them. Attribution is a seat handle, never a display name |
| `artifactdata` | The artifact DATASTORE as its own strict state-log domain: one subject per store, writes SEQUENCED (no broker expectation; the log orders and the applier decides each write as a pure function of the record and its own rows, all or nothing, the verdict written for every record and read back by its writer). Authority that needs IAM or the chart (role, actor kind, limits) is STAMPED at the API; the applier never reads another domain. Compact integer keys, a running state digest per store and real-byte footprint metering keep later tiering from being a re-derive. A field's last writer is the one the LOG ordered last, never a clock, so two blind writes converge on every node; a guarded op (`if_version`) refuses the whole batch, and long text is written whole only guarded or appended. Presets compile to the role vocabulary, so no rule is ever evaluated by name. Seven days of history (bounded values, the digest after each row) back cell history and undo, which is a write of the caller's own conditioned on the versions the undone write left. Quotas count growth only, so a write that frees space is never refused. The cost-weighted write budget is PER NODE and answers when it holds the cost again, never a lockout. Per-viewer state is the `private` read rule on the engine's own `_state` collection |
| `artifactdata/canon` | Pure: the ONE canonical document encoding (sorted keys, numbers as decimal text) and the store state digest (a modular sum of document digests) |
| `artifactdata/query` | Pure: the datastore query grammar compiled to SQL over the field index; an unindexed filter is refused naming the fix, never answered by a scan. Every value is a bound parameter, the cursor is a keyset over the order and the document id, and a string longer than the index's 256-byte prefix is rechecked against the row so a prefix match is never answered as an equal one |
| `costlimit` | The one cost-weighted limiter: two token buckets per charge, a principal on a scope and the scope itself, paid together or not at all, PER NODE and never a fleet quota. The datastore weighs a write by the rows its apply writes; a dashboard's live sources weigh a read by the domain read it costs. One implementation so the refill, the forgetting bound and the rounded Retry-After cannot drift between the two — nor the code host's re-sync surface (`code.NewResyncs`: ONE limiter a git node, which its coding boxes — a run on its seat — its checks' boxes — an attempt on its repository's checks — and its seats' code tools — a turn on its seat — all draw on, so a seat's boxes and tools pay one seat bucket), which charges it one a read through the code log's barrier |
| `node` | Where the fleet layer becomes runnable — a broker client, a coordination backend and the seat host, composed. Deliberately thin: the one behaviour none of its parts can express alone is that WHEN A SEAT IS ACQUIRED ITS MAILBOX IS ATTACHED, and released in an order that never leaves a seat consuming work it no longer owns |
| `engine` | The ENTANGLEMENT POINT — which concrete thing satisfies which seam. THE RUNTIME IS TWO HALVES, split by what depends on a company and never by domain (`core.go`, `native.go`): the CORE — the state log for EVERY registered domain, the node gate over every identity-claiming log, the identity estate's two sides, the directory trigger over it, and on a publishing node the trim, the identity duties and this node's own usage publisher — is published ONCE AT BOOT ON EVERY NODE THAT HOLDS THE ESTATE (`HoldsEstate`: a node with the `data` role, which `ingress` and `workers` require), company or none, because the node nobody has configured is the one a Tier A token's session signs in on to CREATE its company, and the one its first person is invited to and signs in on once that company declares the human seat they will hold (ADR-0032) — so every node that validates a session reads its own identity rows, with no cache and 503 rather than 401 when it cannot — the SEAT is the one thing that waits for a company, so `SeatView` answers a node running none with the seam's own `session.ErrNoCompany`, which the directory's writer turns into `iamdomain.ErrNoCompany` — `409 no_active_revision` at `/iam`, naming the remedy — rather than a 503 no wait clears, while the request path still reads it as unknown; and it is every domain rather than that one because a join replaces the whole replicated file, a snapshot names every registered domain and the trim counts nodes per log. The usage publisher is the core's although it is no singleton — every data node publishes its own days, from its own event log, whatever its company keeps, and a node without `data` keeps no event log, so its events reach a data node through CUSTODY and are counted in that node's day — because armed with the native half a company on Jira and Confluence never replicated a day of its spend, and a node handed its first company by an apply published none until it restarted. The NATIVE half — the tracker's and the knowledge base's writers and readers, the lexical indexer, the change feeds and the embedding duty — waits for the first company that keeps them, and whoever reads it reads `NativeStarted` FIRST, so "not up yet" (503) is never confused with "not this company's" (404); on a node without `data` it starts REMOTE (`startRemote`): the same seams, every one answered by a data node. EVERY ESTATE READ AND WRITE goes through the ESTATE ROUTER (`router.go`, ADR-0025) — a seat's tools, the operator's surfaces and the API's work and page questions alike, on a data node too, whose own copy answers in-process under the same session floors — and the identity reads a node without `data` needs (who holds a login, who holds each human seat, and what a request's bearer is checked against: `MachineToken`'s row, whose verifier is set from the presented token only where the serving node's nonce-bound MAC confirms it, so `credential.CheckToken` decides there as on a data node, and `BoundSeat`'s binding, weighed by `boundRowOf` as a data node weighs its own) are estate operations like any other, floored on the identity log. Seats are admitted through ONE gate, `StateLogHydrated`: on a data node every core log established, the identity log included, AND the router serving the tracker and the knowledge base; on a node without `data` the router's word from a data node — and `seatsAdmitted` is that gate behind a mode that publishes. A copy that is WRONG rather than behind is taken OUT OF SERVICE (`judgeCopy`) and the node's seats STAY, reading the estate through another data node — each log's health, a stall on the checkpoint and a deferral's age all feed that judgement — while the identity log is no input to it: an identity fault feeds the vouch watch below and never takes the tracker's or the knowledge base's copy out of service. The NODE GATE judges an eviction or a readmission in twenty seconds and writes every identity-claiming log AT ONCE in thirty (`GateAnswerBudget`, fifty), and `GateClientWait()` — a minute — is the one wait the CLI and the dashboard are each held to exactly. The teardown ends the view triggers first — the directory trigger and the vouch watch, since neither may outlive what it serves — then the fleet duties and the usage publisher (the native half's embedding writer before the core's usage publisher, trim and identity duties, every one before the duty leases are released; and every writer of a row a node's usage day counts — the learning passes, the scheduler, the code host, whose reads of a unit's repository are such rows, the auxiliary-spend ledger's last flush and the custody keeper — before the publisher's own last flush), then the native half's feeds, index and search answerer, and the logs last; a walk over the teardown's calls holds that order. Deliberately many small files rather than one large one -- this package is flat, and the hardest passage of all, the inbox handler, is a package of its own (`agent/inbox`); the config apply is `reconcile.go` and `epoch.go` here. `mcp.go` is where `mcp_servers:` becomes running children: shared servers on the epoch, per-role children on a seat's LEASE, each claimed seat with its own bridge and its own registry — two children of one template publish the same tool names, so anything shared between seats hands one seat's identity to another. `seatservers.go` keeps those per-role children CONVERGED while the seat is held, through ONE RUNNER PER SEAT (the claim, an apply and a retry each ask it to go again rather than race it): every apply hands the seat's runner a reconcile against the new epoch and secret snapshot (a moved spec restarts, a missing child starts, a failed start retries from fifteen seconds doubling to five minutes, and a seat the published company no longer declares has every child retired by that pass rather than at its release, since placement may hold it a whole sweep interval longer) WITHOUT the apply waiting on it, and no pass starts a child unless `seat.Host.Provable` says the lease is still this node's — a child started once at the claim never saw a credential provisioning sealed a moment later, and its agent heard messages it could never answer. `toolcredentials.go` is the rule ANY tool server is STARTED by at all, in ONE function every start passes (`resolveServer`, the only source of a spec's values and of a box's): every `${VAR}` the server is started with — its own `env` or `headers`, its `url`, and a seat's own `mcp_env` block for it, merged over them key by key BEFORE anything resolves and by the key the server READS, so an http header is one header in any case — must resolve first, because a vendor's server handed an empty token refuses to start or acts as nobody — one a provisioning pass mints for the seat, read off that pass's seat plan (`seatMinter`), which is the plan its own run mints by and the only one this package reads (a walk holds it), WAITS unstarted and not failed until the seal reaches the node; one that pass's last report says it cannot mint until a person acts FAILS with that finding, judged again on every heartbeat since a seat's pass never sees an integration change; one nothing mints FAILS naming the variable and its remedies — the integration's provision command only for a seat's own credential, and the `optional_keys` declaration only for a server's own setting, never for a seat's identity or a variable the url needs; none is retried on a timer, because every change to the snapshot runs the seat's pass and an apply's reconcile of the shared servers — a shared server held back is never handed to its bridge, whose supervision would retry it — and a running server whose credential stops resolving is stopped; and the same rule withholds the server from a coding agent's box, and holds a coding run's OWN environment and setup files (`runEnvironment`, `sandbox.go`, each declaring its own `optional_keys`): `run_sandbox` is REFUSED as its call's result, `unavailable`, while an agent-mode executor — the turn itself, which a refusal would fail — starts without what is missing and says so. A RUN GIVEN REPOSITORIES (`sandboxrepos.go`, ADR-0063) has them resolved after the launch's own refusals and BEFORE its holds are sealed — a resolution takes a scratch repository's hold, which waits on the seal — through the code tools' one resolution, refused as the call's result where this node serves no code host or has no `CREWLET_BOX_GIT_URL` (`BoxURLs.Git`, read at boot with the box's other two addresses; `sandbox_git_url_unset`), and each launch's git environment (`boxGitEnv`, boxgit.go) is made once the store names the job: that launch's run token, the seat's commit identity by `codeSigner`'s one rule and the trailers' values, over the run environment, so the engine's names win. What such a run PUSHED is read back when it is collected (`runpushes.go`, the coordinator's `sandbox.RunPushes`): its launch's push rows on the repositories it was given to push to, the first page LINEARIZABLE — the collection is where "it pushed nothing" must never be a stale answer — and every later page at session at the first page's position, collapsed per ref by the run's first push of it and held to `sandbox.MaxPushedRefs` refs, the rest counted, so what a box pushes in a loop costs the collecting node no more than a record can carry; the router's code seam is asked at each collection, since a node serves the code host with its revision and the coordinator outlives every revision. The same collection names each pushed branch by the live change it heads and opens the changes a run asked for (`runchanges.go`: one `branch_changes` read a repository, linearizable then session; the open a STEP of the launch's own derived operation, `openOpID`, so a retried collection on any node opens nothing twice; as the run's seat, on the run's work item, titled by the delivered commit's subject, else its branch's name, else its id, through `code.DeliveredTitle`, the push's own rule), and a box's admission stamps its pushes with the run's work item's id (`boxRuns`, `RunProvenance.WorkID`) so a `git push -o change` of the box carries the item too. A host setting passed through by name that may be unset is DECLARED, by KEY, in the server's `optional_keys`, and a key so declared whose value does not resolve is LEFT OUT whole — never passed empty, never half a value — except a variable a pass mints for the seat, which is waited for whatever the declaration says; not spelled `${VAR:-}`, which the reference grammar deliberately does not parse. `mcpsupervise.go` is what the engine decides about the bridges' own supervision: a per-role child that DIES is restarted by its bridge only while `seat.Host.Provable` holds — the predicate the passes ask — and a pass leaves it to that bridge (`mcp.Bridge.Runs` answers yes for it) rather than starting a second copy, so the runner and the supervisor never run two loops over one child; a shared server whose start failed is retried by its bridge, and what it brings back is refiled into the CURRENT epoch's registry — the one part of a published epoch that changes — and every held seat's surface. It is also where every fleet-singleton DUTY is armed, and the reason is the same: the trim, the maintenance sweep and the EMBEDDING duty each need the company's provider, the log's publisher and the fleet's leases in one place. The alarm table is the exception that proves it — evaluated on EVERY node on its own HEARTBEAT (`statelog.AlarmInterval`, which IS `statelog.PositionHeartbeat`, the ten seconds every data node republishes its position on — one declaration, because a copy of the number per package agreed only until one was retuned) and never behind the trim's duty claim, because a reading describes one NODE and a table evaluated only where the lease sits would report the lease holder's health as the fleet's, and because the trim's quarter-hour silently raised every sixty-second threshold to fifteen minutes. What that evaluation finds firing rides the node's PRESENCE HEARTBEAT (`nodeStatus`, off the tracker the health envelope reads, from memory), and a node with no tracker or none evaluated yet publishes no alarms rather than an empty set, so a peer never reads it as quiet. The trim's tick is where the SCANS the table needs are taken — the vector coverage and each log's daily intake, the second withheld through a log's first two days because its first day is the one a company imports into — and so is the store pool's wait DELTA, which whoever reads it clears for everyone after, so it is taken on that regular quarter-hour alone, the cadence the `pool_wait` p95 is a percentile of; the heartbeat and every report read them back rather than repeating them; a gauge whose measurement became unknown is WITHDRAWN rather than left holding the last figure. Every field of the reading it evaluates has a PRODUCER that the reading's assembly reaches (`TestEveryAlarmReadingFieldHasAProducer`, a `sourcetree/decltype` walk from `retention.reading` and statelog's `ReportInputs.alarms`), because a field nothing fills reads as zero, which is exactly what a healthy node looks like: `code_maintenance_refused` and `prefetch_slow` were each silent on every running node that way. The code half (`codeReading`) reads the COMMITTER beside the git cache and the compactor, never inside their branch: a committer runs on every data node of a company hosting code, the git role or not, so `code_commit_busy` is filled wherever one decides pushes — its five fields assigned in `codeReading` itself from the concrete committer's `BusyReading`, since the producers gate follows no call whose result is the committer's own shape, and the repository named by this node's own rows (`code.Store.NameOf`), else by its id; and the CODE READER on every node that holds the code log's rows, whatever else it runs, for `code_checks_backlog` (`code.Reader.ChecksBacklog`): the queued attempts past their OWN timeouts are measured from the rows by every data node, never by a runner, so a fleet whose every git node is gone still raises it. And the HOME'S QUEUE WORKER on a git node for `code_merge_rate_over_capacity` (`codehome.Loop.AlarmReading`, read after the compactor): only the home builds a queue's trains, so only it times their rounds, and a case reaches it through `WithHomeSeams`. And the TRIM'S TICK for `code_refs_near_limit` (`refsnear.go`): `retention.evaluate` takes `code.Reader.RefsNearLimit` through the `retention.refs` field on every data node of a company hosting code, CACHED under `retention.mu` until the next tick and copied by `retention.reading` with the bound and the window it was judged at (`code.MaxRefsPerRepo`, `code.RefExpiry`) — the vector coverage's idiom, because the reading walks the repositories and counts each near one's refs while a report is assembled on every beat, request and poll — never in `Engine.codeReading`, the cache's half, which returns on a node with no cache; a failed read clears the figure and logs `code_refs_near_limit_unread` once a tick. The reading of this node's own disk (`retentioncapacity.go`) names the copy holding the largest write-ahead log and counts, per volume, the log each file's last copy measured a copy holding back, so `wal_large` can say a copy's end folds the log back in and `volume_low` keeps room for it beside the second copy. Whether the trim floor can be read is observed on the heartbeat itself (`floorwatch.go`), through the read path's own two steps — one listing, then `floorFor` per domain — so `floor_unknown` lists exactly the floors every read refuses on, one log each in its `domains`. How far this node's clock, and the clock of the broker member leading the position register, stand from the fleet's is measured on the position heartbeat too (`clockskew.go`), off the register it has just read — every data node's row, stamped by its node and stored at the member's instant, read only where the member stored it within four heartbeats of this node's own, and the fleet's clock their median — so `clock_skew` names the clock that moved and costs no read or write of its own, and a measure stands until two beats in a row could not renew it or the heartbeat stops — counted in beats by the heartbeat, never as an age the reading judges, since an age counted from a beat's start lapsed between two beats whenever a beat and the next listing outlasted it, clearing a standing alarm for one evaluation and raising it again at the next. A binding dangles by ONE rule, `bindings.go`, which is the request path's own seat table applied to a directory row, and `/iam/check` is the one place it is reported, with the repair its holder's KIND takes — a person is moved to another human seat or removed, since the directory refuses to unbind one, and a service account unbound or moved — never an alarm, because it is a fact about the company's content that every node would raise at once and none could repair (ADR-0015). The IDENTITY estate's duty is armed here too (`identityduties.go`) — the per-bucket sweep publisher — on its own lease of three of its hourly intervals, inside `coord.MaxDutyTTL`. It is armed only where the `workers` role runs, although every data node runs the identity domain — elsewhere every claim would be refused, so arming it would be a loop that never runs reported as a duty that does — and `/health` serves what a node armed as `identity_duty_seconds`, because a duty never armed looks exactly like one finding nothing. And the COMPANY KEY a blind is derived under (`companykeys.go`) is MINTED here, on first use, with a CREATE the store refuses over a key that landed first, and every minter reads back whichever did: the store is last-write-wins for a put, and two nodes that each found a key absent each read their OWN write back and derived under different keys. The fleet hold and the in-process gate stay only as the courtesy that keeps two nodes from each running the guard — a lease cannot fence a holder paused past it, so a put under the hold split the company exactly as it did with none. A mint takes a REQUIRED guard, and the identity key's refuses both an estate holding a blind (minting over a DELETED key orphans every address) and rows that have not APPLIED the whole identity log — behind it, or holding a record they retained, which moves the checkpoint without writing the rows — whose "no blind here" may be rows that were never applied; the log's END is read first (`IdentityLogEnd`) and the rows prove themselves against it in their own snapshot. A NODE THAT STOPS VOUCHING FOR ITS IDENTITY ROWS SAYS SO (`identityvouch.go`): every identity read compares the identity log's lag against `statelog.StallGrace`, and an applier halted on a record it cannot read, or frozen behind a broker, commits no batch for any listener to hear — so a watch over that same lag hands the credential listener a move naming everyone the instant it crosses the grace, once per crossing, which is the instant the request path starts answering 503 — and that move only closes the node's sockets, whose reconnects the handshake decides. The party registry has TWO rebuild triggers — a published company, and the identity applier's `Moved.Seats` (`directory.go`), with a thirty-second re-read behind it, which is the only trigger beside a published company on a node without `data`, where who holds each seat is read through the estate router (`SeatHolders`) — serialised on one lock, each building a WHOLE registry for its company and swapping it, never a diff; a directory it cannot read carries the last reading forward, and a node that has NEVER read one withholds every human seat until it does, because the reading it would otherwise carry is the chart-only one. WHOSE CREDENTIALS a committed identity batch moved (`iamdomain.Moved`, `identitymoved.go`) goes to whatever `SetOnIdentityMoved` registered — the API, which decides again the dashboard sockets a move names and closes every socket on a move naming nobody, since every data node applies the identity log and so closes its own, and every node serving the API is one — and an adoption or a reopened estate, which put a donor's rows in place with no committed batch to name a revocation the donor applied, say `Everyone` (`stateLog.estateReplaced`). The appliers' post-commit hooks are one `applyHooks` value built by `Engine.applyHooks`, so the register's own cases build appliers from what a running node hands them — a nil hook compiles and passes every case in the domain's own package. WHOSE INBOX a committed batch moved (`inbox.Movement`, `inbox.go`) goes from the appliers of the domains the register marks `Inbox` — the tracker's and the code log's — the mark is the wiring, since `stateLog.inboxFor` hands the listener to those alone — to whatever `SetOnInboxMoved` registered, the API's `inbox_changed` push; while this node's copy of such a domain is out of service, that domain's movements come from a sound peer's applier through the INBOX RELAY (`inboxrelay.go`): ONE server per node on one subject, registered once at boot, holding a stream per source under a sequence and an epoch of its own, and one follower per source that relays only a domain whose own copy is out, from a server whose copy of THAT domain serves, every switch a re-read naming the source (`SetOnInboxGap`). A server KEEPS ONLY WHAT A FOLLOWER CAN STILL ASK FOR, since no follower is served a batch recorded before the last answer it took: a source's batches for one round trip past the stream's latest answer (`inboxRelayHold`, `statelog.ReadBudget`), or past the batch while an ask waits for it, and never more than `inboxRelayMoves` (`statelog.ApplyTxRowBudget`) movements, which also holds the widest answer to one reply — so a fleet with no copy out of service keeps none, where a ring kept by count, the obvious shape, holds a thousand whole transactions of every source on every data node for nobody; a follower slower than that, or behind a wider burst, is answered a gap and re-reads. A watch over one subsystem's appliers names the domains it judges (`localEstate.outOfService`): the relay judges each source alone, and the artifact rooms withhold their pushes while this node's copy of the artifacts log or the datastore is out of service, and never for the tracker's, whose wrong copy describes nothing a room shows. The mailbox sweep's roster (`maintenance.go`) judges a seat ABSENT only on a node that has applied the revision the fleet's activation pointer names, so a node behind on an apply never stamps a seat the fleet has just added. THE ORG IS THE APPLIED EPOCH'S: `roles:` and `units:` are part of every revision, built into the running `org.Organization` once per apply, and ONE list (`converge.go`, `followCompany`) brings everything derived from a company up to the published one — the party registry, the seats' vendor identities and tool surfaces, the tracker's projects and the knowledge containers (stamped with the revision's activation instant), the mailboxes, the scheduler and the socket push — run by boot and by every apply, so a step added to one path is a step both get. Every HOLD on a seat's mailbox — a person's pause, a budget park waiting out a spent window — is keyed on the seat's ID (`seatpause.go`, `budgetpark.go`), because the inbox a hold sits on is named by the id. A person's STOP is read ONE way (`stoppedBy`) for both of a detached coding run's write edges — the code host's box admission (`codeBoxRuns`) and the session a launch opens on the tool bridge (`agentLauncher.bridgeSession`) — so its box's pushes and its bridged writes are refused, and let through again, together. And it is where every FLEET-WIDE consumer group — one any node may attach, so a message goes to whichever member takes it first — declares whether its handler needs data (`groups.go`): `joins` is the one door every attachment passes, a node without `data` joins none that do, and a gate holds the table against the tree's subscriptions in both directions, because a stateless member of a group that reads the estate answers its share of the messages from nothing and nothing fails. And it is where the two INSTRUCTION CHANNELS the engine serves beside the estate are signed (`fleetbroker.go`, `steer.go`) — a broker member removing a voter through its own system account, and the node running a turn taking a person's note as that person — each a `signedask` channel built ONCE in `New` from the fleet keyring on every node and refused rather than served unsigned, because each carries out what it is asked on the asking node's decision, and the broker's admission vouches for a connection, never a request: a member runs only what it admits, a key it does not hold is "did not run" so the next member carries the removal, and the carrying member's log names the author, kind and credential read off the signed request; `TestEveryInstructionChannelSignsInDomainsOfItsOwn` holds every channel's key domains apart from each other's and every state log's. And `codehost.go` is where a node with the `git` role builds the code host for a `code.native` company, with the runtime: the cache's directory prepared before git's HOME exists, its budget derived from the ROOM the cache can use — the volume's free space PLUS what the cache already holds (free space excludes the cache's own files, so a budget from it alone shrank at every warm restart), LESS what an embedded broker on the same volume may still store under its limit (`jetstream.Queue.VolumeClaim`: the broker's limit is a share of the same free space, so a budget beside it claimed the same bytes twice) and the host's spool allowance — half of a shared volume's room, seven eighths of one of its own, never more and with no floor, and a room of nothing REFUSES the code host by name, since any budget there is bytes the volume does not have; "the same volume" is one pool of space (device id, filesystem id, or the space left read at one instant to within the coarser block, so a btrfs subvolume or a ZFS dataset of the store's pool is shared whatever its recordsize) — the git pool, the cache, the GIT READS (`internal/code/query`, over the same cache and pool under one seat git-work share of the pool's read lane, the chart and the posture read per read) and the host over the engine's own router, chart and audit — handed THAT SAME SHARE, never a second, so the builds of its ENGINE WRITES (ADR-0062) and the git reads hold at most half the lane between them, judged by the same chart and gated by the same posture (`Roster: e.codeRoster`, `Current: e.admits`, read per write), and signed by `codeSigner` (`codesign.go`): from the CURRENT epoch, read ONCE a signature so a seat's role and the identity domain come off one epoch, the author the acting principal's seat — an agent's own, or the human seat a person's credential is bound to — by its role's name and its role's email AS WRITTEN, never `org.Role.ResolvedEmail`, which reads THIS node's environment and would let a failover on another git node build another commit of one write and write an address a company kept out of its document into history, a name or an address git would not write as given, an address that is no `<local>@<domain>` of at most 254 bytes and a `${VAR}` reference falling back to the handle and `<handle>@<code.identity_domain>`; the committer `Crewlet <engine@<code.identity_domain>>`, spelled once (`engineCommitter`) for what `Sign` writes and for `Committer`, the address a coding box's pack may hold no commit of that the repository does not already hold (`forged_committer`, read off the same epoch); a node with no epoch or no such seat signs nothing (`code.ErrNotServing`, so the walk asks another git node) — and the COMPACTOR (`code/compact`), built there unstarted and STARTED with the native half's duties (`startNativeDuties`) — past the runtime's publication, which its first pass reads this node's git half and copy through (started at the build, that pass found the node home to nothing), and behind the mode's publisher gate, since a compaction stores objects and appends its record; read per request through `CodeHost`, never captured, and stopped ONCE within one bound in the teardown BEFORE the committer, since its syncs append barriers and its pushes propose — the home's change work first, then the compactor, then the git reads, cut and waited for, then the host and its cache. The HOME'S CHANGE WORK (`code/home`) is built beside the compactor and started and stopped with it, its nudges a watch on this node's own code log, each sequence a position in the generation this node's applier last committed in (`codeNudges`). The COMMITTER judges a seat's push from the same current epoch a seat's read is judged by (`codeSeatRules`: the epoch's roster and the company's clock, the quota's day, off ONE epoch, so a revision moving a seat and the clock together is judged by both or by neither; false before the first epoch is installed, on which the committer hands a seat's push to the repository's next committer) and behind the same posture gate (`Current: e.admits`, `Reconciler.Admits` through `SetAdmits`), so a node confirmed behind the company's configuration decides no seat's push. Its sibling the CHANGE WRITER (`code.ChangeWriter`, a change's conversation on the change's own subject) is built beside it on the same terms — the same `codeSeatRules`, posture gate and retention — handed out with it through the write set, so a maintenance mode serves neither, and stopped right after it in the same bound before the estate subject stops answering. The host and the git reads RECORD every read of a `visibility: unit` repository they serve through `recordCodeRead`, which FILES it in the node's reads ledger (`codeReads`, `code/readaudit`) and returns — it used to publish there, so a fetch's response, a read's git-lane slot and every read on the page it cut waited for the broker's replicated, fsynced acknowledgement, up to the client's five seconds while the event stream had no leader — and the ledger publishes each reader's reads of a flush as one `code_fetched` event of this node's own log, which the usage publisher derives into the day's `repo_read` record; the two ledgers (`auxSpend` and `codeReads`) take their last flush at once on one five-second budget (`stopLedgers`), after the code host stops and before the usage publisher's own — NEVER through the operator audit (`auditGit`), whose envelope source is the runtime audit's and whose types the dashboard's audit log reads as people's calls, when a read is neither a call nor necessarily a person's; and `CodeReaderDeps` hands `code_repo_readers` the usage domain's readers through the router, on `CodeToolDeps`' terms. `CodeToolDeps` wires the code host's writes and the turn's hold seam over the very router seam its reads take (`Writer`, `Holder`), so a node that serves a seat's code reads serves its writes and keeps its holds on the same terms — and, where the company runs the native tracker, the tracker's read of one task (`Tasks`, the work tools' own reader), which resolves a native work item a change write names by its key. The router's GIT PLACEMENT is every live node whose presence profile has the git role, off the same watched view as the data placement (`wholeGit`, `gitNodesOf`), and this node's git half is `engineGit`, which loads the published runtime on EVERY call and answers its `gitHalf` — the query server's reads by object id, the code host's engine writes and the home's merge previews (`codehome.Previews`), built together over one cache, one pool and one share and stopped together — handed to the router and, where the profile has the git role, to `serveEstate`, so the git lanes registered at boot answer `no_backend` until an apply builds the code host and serve it after with no restart: captured at either's construction, a git node that applied its company after boot answered "no code host" to every read and write until it restarted. The router's GIT BUDGETS are what each git node's presence row says its cache gives its background work, read with the git nodes themselves off ONE listing of the same view (`wholeGit.Listing` over `presenceRoster.GitListing`, every lease decoded once and by `gitNodeOf`, the rule `LiveGitNodes` lists by; `coord.GitBackgroundKey` beside the status: a row without it a node NOT SAYING, never one with nothing to give), and a git node advertises its own on every beat — handed to the seat host as a hook beside the status (`node.Config.GitBackground`, `Engine.gitBackground`, nil on a node without the git role) rather than as a status field, because a beat whose status read overran drops the status whole — from the PUBLISHED runtime's cache (`gitBackgroundBudget`, zero where it holds none), so a repository's maintainer is weighed by the figure its admissions hold the work to. ONE READING OF TIER A'S STREAM BLOCK (`streamConfig`) and ONE FLEET-STORE CONSTRUCTOR (`openFleet`) serve the boot and `OpenFleetStore` alike — the fleet's coordination store reached by a process that is not the engine (a `crewlet` command while the engine is stopped): a solo member starts its own broker on its store directory, under that directory's lock and with no leaf listener; a node on an external broker dials it; a member of a cluster (no quorum alone, and rejoining as this node for one value is a restart's cost) and a node that holds no data (a leaf of the members') are `ErrNoFleetStoreHere`. Only the fleet store: not the queue, which would provision the engine's streams, nor the lease bucket or the object store, whose settings the engine decides. A node serving the code host ANSWERS THE FLEET'S CODE STATUS on `crewlet.code.status` (`codestatus.go`) with `host.Server.Status`, registered once the host starts and withdrawn first inside the host's one bounded stop, so no peer is told of a cache that is going; UNSIGNED, since a node answers it about itself and carries nothing out for the asker (internal/signedask's rule), and versioned, a question at a version this build does not speak answered by nobody. ANY node ASKS it (`CodeStatus`, for `code_status`): a scatter over the git nodes the presence leases list live, inside the fleet read budget (`eventfan.FleetReadBudget`), lasting until every git node the roster NAMES has answered — never a count of replies, because a git node the roster does not hold answers too (a draining one gives its presence back first and its host serves until it stops) — reported with the one coverage shape every fanned answer carries — a git node that did not answer, or answered at a version this build does not read, is named rather than counted healthy, and a node with no broker asks its own host. The health tools take `HealthToolDeps` on both surfaces, and a seat's runner is handed the builtins its seat is refused outright (`RunnerFor` takes the context and asks `builtin.Withheld` over the runner's own registry, as the seat's principal, against the epoch it pins), so what a seat is offered and what it is admitted are one decision — and the code writes too where the seat's code tier writes no repository (`builtin.CodeWritesWithheld` over that epoch's role, `code.Tier(role.CodeAccess)` as `codeRoster` reads it): every call of one would be refused by every repository's grant, which the authority table, admitting everybody to a code write so each repository's grant decides it, cannot say ahead of the calls. A data node's ALARM TRANSITIONS leave through an OUTBOX (`alarmwakes.go`, armed with the retention loops and stopped with them): the tracker hands each one on as it is made, and the outbox publishes it as the inbound delivery `alarmwake` routes (source `alarms`), retried until the broker takes it — two seconds doubling to the alarm interval — and in order, bounded at 1024 with the oldest not in flight dropped and counted (`crewlet.alarm.transitions.dropped`, the taken ones on `crewlet.alarm.transitions{change}`); a transition still queued when the node stops is not recovered, since the next start's first evaluation says what is still firing. The alarms' parser rides `nativeParsers` on every node, whatever halves it runs, built per epoch from the chart's granted seats. The code host's (`code.NewParser`, `code.Prompt`) rides it where the company hosts its repositories (`halves.code`), its wakes relayed by the code log's feed the register's code entry declares (`crewlet-code-feed`), and `code.Prompt` is one of `workItemNamers`, so a code wake charges its turn to the work item the change implements (ADR-0067). WHO A SEAT'S TURN IS ACTING FOR IS COMPOSED ONCE (`turnRequesters`, requesters.go) for every family of tools that decides the requester ceiling — the artifacts tools and the code tools, wired beside the chart that decides them (`ArtifactToolDeps`, `CodeToolDeps`, each read per call) — an agent seat as its own principal and a person's seat as the person the routed identity directory says holds it, at their stage — that one seat's holder (`Identity.SeatHolder`), never the whole directory, because it is composed on every call of such a tool — so one requester is one principal whichever tool reads or writes for it. WHO WOKE IT is read off the trigger by one resolution (`subjectOf`, prefetch.go) for the requester, the senders and every counterparty profile alike: a vendor's sender as an id in its own namespace, and a FIRST-PARTY notice's writer by the kind its record holds (`types.NotificationWriter`, `writerSubject`) — a seat's change, or a person's as their seat, that seat by its handle; the engine's own, nobody; a credential acting as no seat, a removed seat or an unknown kind, the platform identity a stranger keeps — because the tracker's and the knowledge base's actor is spelled as a handle, a login or a node's name alike, and read as a vendor id every colleague's comment woke a stranger's turn. The requester is the first voice besides the seat's OWN (`requesterOf`): the outcome of its own act asks it nothing, and a colleague who speaks after it is who the turn is for. THE LIVE TURNS' CODE HOLDS (codeholds.go, M3-decisions M3-O61, M3-O64) — the scratch repositories and the seats' branches a turn named, by keys this package never decodes: a turn's `turnctx.Holds` lives in this process while it runs — every running turn in one registry (`runningTurns`), entered by the dispatch and a resume for the turn's whole run — and on its RUN ROW once it launches (`sandbox.PendingRun.CodeHolds`): both launch paths write the set on the row they open and attach the row's writer through `Opened` (the seal), and a resumed turn starts from the row's set (`rowHolds`), so a parked run holds what its turn named until its row ends; a coding box's push adds the seats' branches it names to the same row (`boxRuns.Hold`), because a box pushes as its run and never as a turn, and a row that is gone or ending holds nothing new and is no error, since a run that has ended depends on nothing. `Engine.codeHolds` is the code-retention pass's `Retention.Holds`: every run row, whatever its status (`ListRuns`) — listed BEFORE and AGAIN AFTER the ask, because a turn's first launch moves its holds from its memory onto a new row while the ask is on its way, and a row written after the first listing by a turn whose frame returned before the ask reached its node was in neither answer — and every seat-lease holder's running turns, asked on `crewlet.code.holds` (an unsigned ephemeral read scatter inside `eventfan.FleetReadBudget`, lasting until every holder the leases NAME has answered — never a count of replies, because every node answers it, one holding no seat among them, and its answer would fill the count while a holder's went unread) — the seat holders, never the presence listing, because a draining node gives its presence back before its turns finish — and a store, a lease read or a holder that cannot be read is an error, so a page expires nothing on an unknown. The box credentials are built at `New` on EVERY node over the fleet keyring, whatever its roles: a coding box's run tokens (`BoxTokens`) and a check's box's tokens (`CheckTokens`), in one key domain — the node that launches a run or claims a check mints, whichever git node the box dials verifies, and the API verifies either on a node whose code host is not built (`api.CodeSurface.VerifyBox`) — and the code host admits both by them (`host.Boxes`). |

## The agent runtime

| Package | What it is for |
|---|---|
| `agent/turn` | The turn loop — an executor and a reviewer — the guards around them, and the one question the whole engine turns on: did this turn actually reach anybody? WHO IS WAITING is derived at dispatch from the trigger's own type and WHAT RAN is the tool loop's own record, so neither half is something a model can get wrong. It replaced a reconciliation of the planner's PREDICTED tools against reality, which is where both recorded delivery incidents came from |
| `agent/runner` | Drives the phases against real models and real tools — the wiring between the prompt builder, the tool registry, the provider chain and the turn loop's contract, none of which knows the others. Plumbing, plus the one rule plumbing cannot express: what happens when the model never gives a structured answer at all. What a valid answer IS lives here too, in the decoders; the SHAPE is `agent/structured`. The executor has TWO runtimes (`agentrun.go`): the native tool loop, and somebody else's agentic loop behind one seam — same prompt, same surface, same submission, same rescue, and only the rounds happen elsewhere — and the surface's own METER (`BridgedSpend`), because the CLI calls the seat's tools after the segment that launched it has charged what it spent, so what those calls cost (the auxiliary rewrites, the delegated workers) is counted apart, written to the run's row with every bridged call and paid by the segment that resumes. A seat's WITHHELD builtins (`Config.Withheld`: the tools the authority table refuses that seat outright, computed by the engine once per runner from the epoch it pins) leave the registry's snapshot through ONE method every phase reads (`seatSnapshot`) — the executor in either runtime and on a resume, the reviewer, the onboarding turn and the workers a phase hands its tools to — so a seat is never shown a tool whose every call it would be refused. The DISCOVERY PAIR (`list_mcp_server_tools`, `activate_tool`) never passes through the registry, so `DiscoveryTools` hands it out as ENTRIES carrying their own hints — read-only, idempotent, closed-world — which every phase surface and a worker's (`subagent.Config.Discovery`) add as built: activating changes only which of the seat's tools the phase is offered, and a pair a caller wrapped bare reached the bridge unannotated, where a person's stop refused a stopped run's discovery as a write it could not classify |
| `agent/structured` | How a phase gives a typed answer: it CALLS A TOOL rather than writing prose the engine parses, so the ordinary tool loop carries the answer out and nothing needs a side channel. Three rules travel with the shape — a rejected submission goes back to the model (the one failure a model reliably fixes), the last write wins (a second submission is a correction), and an absent submission is not a value (every rescue path turns on telling "decided this" from "nothing decided anything"). The decoder belongs to the caller, and `Remarshal` — the one mapping from arguments to a typed payload, which `delegate` decodes through too — REFUSES a field the payload does not have, at any depth and by name: decoded the obvious way it was DROPPED, so a reviewer's misspelt `completed_work` never told the next round what had already landed, and a delegated task's `afer` ran at once on input it was told to wait for |
| `agent/phase` | The per-phase vocabulary: which phase is running, which model it runs on, and what a turn concluded. `execute` names the phase although it now decides as well as acts: deciding is part of the seat doing its own work, which is what every dashboard and rollup that reads the value means by it |
| `agent/toolloop` | The model↔tool round-trip — and the loop RE-ASKS A PHASE THAT ENDS A ROUND WITHOUT ITS SUBMISSION, naming the call that finishes it, because a phase that finishes by a call declares so (`TerminateAfter`) and a round of prose there is a report written where nobody reads it — and its CLOCK: every round's provider call and every tool call is timed here, where the call is made, and every completion's prompt-cache share is kept, because a reader reconstructing either from event arrival subtracts two publishers' clocks and the cache counts reached nothing past a span attribute — and the `Suspend` primitive a detached coding run returns through Every call is told its round's MODEL beside its round (`CallModel`, set by ONE setter, `WithCallModel`: here from the round's own model, the completion's report else the provider's identity — the precedence `Round.Model` records — and by the MCP bridge for a call a coding CLI made), so a seat's code write names on its push the model that made it. |
| `agent/steer` | A person's note to a RUNNING turn: the box one turn holds and the wire a note crosses. A note enters the conversation at the TOP OF A ROUND, IMMEDIATELY AFTER THE FENCE, as a USER message — the one point where every call the previous round made has its result, so a note can never split a call from its answer, and never the system prompt a provider caches on. The executor and the reviewer read notes; a worker, the onboarding pass and the judge do not; an agent-mode executor answers `unsupported`, because its rounds are the CLI's. A read note is CARRIED into every later phase of the turn (a parked run's state included), or the reviewer grades against the task the person corrected. The note is scattered on the queue's ephemeral verb, SIGNED and bound to one request on `signedask`'s `seat.steer` channel — the running node offers it as the person it names on the asking node's decision, so a note the fleet did not sign reaches no turn and an answer the asker cannot verify is no answer — and only the node running the turn answers — it remembers the last 256 turns it ended so a note that raced the end is `closed` rather than silence — and a retry of one request is one note, because the note's id IS the call's operation — the request's `Idempotency-Key` as `internal/api/opkey` scoped it to its principal — so an MCP call, which names none, is a new note every time. What became of it is the running node's `agent_turn_steered` (`delivered` at its round, `expired` when the box closed unread). WHO MAY STEER is decided on the turn's SEAT — its lead, or `admin` — and the node a request reached cannot know that seat until the running node says: `admin` admits at once and a caller who leads nobody is refused at once, while anybody else's call first sends a PROBE that offers nothing (one nobody answers is `unavailable`) and is decided again on the seat it names before the note is offered; the sender travels as `iam.ActorFor`'s author, kind and credential, never as a second bound-seat field beside them that could name somebody else |
| `agent/execstate` | The wire format for a suspended executor loop. A detached sandbox run stops the phase mid-loop with its tool call UNANSWERED and is re-entered later — after a restart, possibly on another node — so the conversation is serialized into the pending-run row rather than parked in a goroutine. The sandbox coordinator carries it back without ever decoding it. Evolution is additive WITHIN a version and a reshape takes a new number, never a reused one. Once a TAG has shipped a version, the build that replaces it carries a reader for it for as long as rows of it can be parked: nothing rewrites a parked row, and the layer holding the blob cannot decode it. A version this build does not read — the unreleased 1 included, which no tag ever shipped — is refused loudly and the row left for a node that can |
| `agent/extension` | The round-cap extension judge: when a phase runs out of tool rounds, a cheap model says whether it is progressing or thrashing, and the engine grants more or falls through to the rescue path. THE ARITHMETIC IS SEPARATED FROM THE JUDGE — how many rounds a decision is worth is pure policy, impossible to exercise through a live model, so it is a value type tested directly |
| `agent/subagent` | `delegate`: the short-lived WORKERS an executor hands work to. One tool loop with a persona (a `workers:` template or an inline prompt), a slice of the parent's own tools, a declared answer shape — the task's own `output` or its template's, the first held to the second's load-time rule (`config.ValidateWorkerOutput`) before anything runs — and hard caps on rounds, wall-clock and tokens. A worker is a LEAF — it delegates nothing further and contacts no colleague — which is the only thing bounding the depth of the construct, so there is no depth counter anywhere. One call is a GRAPH: `after` makes a task wait for others and receive their submitted fields, run in topological waves under one barrier each, because a wave boundary is checkable where a per-task readiness queue's deadlocks are not. Three rules are enforced in code because a prompt is only a request: the grant is a security boundary (a config template widens nothing — every name still passes the same filter), one budget slice covers a whole call however many tasks it has, and an absent submission is `no_result` with the worker's prose rather than an answer the engine invented |
| `agent/inbox` | What wakes a seat, and what it must not be woken by twice. `ledgered` is the completion side |
| `agent/ledger` | The prior-work ledgers: `iteration` (within a turn), `conversation` (across turns in one thread), `budgets`. Structured, never a transcript replay — the thread has moved by the next turn. A PAYLOAD past its budget — an argument value, a failed call's error, a round's produced text — is REWRITTEN by the seat's auxiliary model, never cut: the ledger only NAMES the pieces (`IterationPieces`, `SessionPieces`) and `ledgerfit` fits them, omitting an argument by size and digest where no rewrite can be had and carrying a draft whole. The cross-turn block keeps its newest entries whole and CONDENSES the older ones rather than dropping them |
| `agent/prefetch` | The turn-start context assembly: relevant knowledge, memory, the CHAT THREAD the turn was woken in, the blocks a prompt gets. FROZEN before the runner exists, which is what stops a self_iterate loop moving the system prompt underneath a turn. Nothing re-fetches mid-turn any more — the executor asks, with `search_knowledge`, over this same seam. The thread block is what a prompt telling an agent to go and read one cost: three MCP rounds before a word, and an agent that skipped them answering a pointer. It is bounded by WHOLE MESSAGES against an item cap and a byte ceiling anchored at a third of the conversation ledger's, because both are frozen into one system prompt and re-sent every round; it reports what it dropped; and its two failure sentences differ, because "nothing was said earlier" and "this node could not read it" send a seat to opposite places — the second covers a maintenance-mode node, an instance down at boot and a refused seat token, none of which is a chat API error |
| `agent/prompts` | The prompt text, and one builder per phase — and EVERY BUILDER RETURNS ITS OUTLINE BESIDE ITS TEXT (`outline.go`): the parts it appended, each keyed, titled, measured and marked headed or not, because only the frame that appended a part knows where content quoting its own headings begins and ends. The engine STATES ONLY WHAT THE MODEL CANNOT KNOW, EACH RULE ONCE: the seat's identity and chart, the company's own words, the turn's prefetch and the phase's contract — a rule about a tool or a submission field lives in that tool's description or that field's schema, which reach the model with the tool on every round, and no tool the phase already holds is listed again (`## Tools to discover` names only what it can still reach). Prompt text is behaviour, changed deliberately with its tests and docs. Two prompts where there were three: the frame that DECIDES is the frame that acts, so the identity scaffold is sent once rather than twice and thrown away in between. The executor's two code sections, `code_history` (read on the server, never from a clone, and a change's diff, its findings and what changed since a review with `code_get_change`) and `code_writes` (a small change committed on the server, anything to build or test in a checkout), are each gated on EVERY tool its claims rest on (`CodeHistoryTools`, `CodeWriteTools`), which internal/agent/builtin's tests hold to the code tools it registers; `code_history` adds that a repository `run_sandbox` clones is blobless — so its history is for building, not for answering questions — only where the executor is offered the sandbox too, whose `repos` comes with the code tools; `code_search` (`CodeSearchTools`), between `code_history` and `code_writes`, says to search before reading whole trees; `code_merging` (`CodeMergeTools`), after `code_writes`, says a merge that is not ready, or is queued for its checks, is not waited on — the turn ends, and a `ready_to_merge` or `merged` wake brings the seat back — gated on the merge tool alike. |
| `agent/skills` | Prompt fragments admitted from the knowledge base and injected per phase, plus the load-before-use guard. The registry is keyed by the PAGE a skill was read from, not by its key: every change names a page, and a page whose key was edited no longer says what it used to hold, so a key-addressed registry could never remove the old key. Pages as the identity are also what make a one-page update end exactly where a walk of the same pages would |
| `agent/skillsync` | The one loop per node that keeps the registry current: a walk when an apply moves the skills source (and the previous source retired at once), a read of the one page a webhook named, a `tool_skill_page_changed` broadcast so every node hears a webhook only one node consumed, a periodic walk behind all of it, and a bounded retry behind a walk that failed. ONE GOROUTINE DOES EVERY READ, which is what lets it need no version comparison: a later read always sees later state. Every change is READ BACK, a deletion included, because the backend and never the report says whether a page is gone. And a walk or a read that finds what the registry was built from changes nothing and logs nothing above debug, or the periodic walk would repeat every registry and audit warning on every node each interval |
| `agent/builtin` | The tools the engine itself ships, and the ONE AUTHORIZER that gates both catalogues (a seat's registry and the operator's), built from `authz.Decide` over the running chart and wrapped round every tool ONCE, at registration: the action IS the tool's name, so there is exactly one place the check could be forgotten. A NIL AUTHORIZER REFUSES, the opposite of a nil skill gate and deliberately so: a missing skill gate is a company that locked nothing, a missing authority decision is a surface that shipped without wiring one. What each tool is ABOUT is a table of ARGUMENT NAMES rather than closures, because a name can be walked against the tool's own schema and a closure can only be called with arguments somebody thought of; a personal verb that takes no handle is about the caller. The few checks that need a stored row first (re-routing a task, a project's lead-only facets, archiving it, setting somebody's priorities) ask the SAME seam from inside the tool under a dotted action, since which project a task is filed under is not in the arguments. A person verb that takes a name (`get_person`, `work_inbox`, `set_priorities`, and the HTTP surface's `MarkInboxFor`/`SetPinsFor`) resolves it to a RECORD first (`WorkDeps.personRecord`, over `iam.OwnerOf` and `WorkDeps.Holders`) and is decided on THAT, so it is `inTool`, but the directory is asked only once the verb has been decided on the record nobody has resolved yet (`WorkDeps.mayLook`, `authz.Object.Unresolved`), so a caller who leads nobody is refused before any lookup, in the very words a seat they do not lead gets, even when this node cannot read the directory; a login nobody holds is then decided on the name as typed before it is refused as naming nobody, and the directory's own words (which name the seat a login is bound to) go to the log and never into a refusal. Together that is what keeps the directory from being a roster. The same gate REFUSES AN ARGUMENT A TOOL DOES NOT DECLARE, naming it and what the tool reads, before any authority is asked (`ErrUndeclaredArgument`): a builtin reads its schema's properties and nothing else, so an undeclared one was DROPPED and the call answered as though it asked for less: the HTTP routes refused it and nothing else did, so a seat, a worker, a sandbox's bridge and the operator's assistant each saved `save_work_view`'s retired `owner` as a shared tab. The gate derives the principal FROM THE TURN when the context carries none, and never over an answer already there; and its wrappers are POINTERS, because a struct holding a func is uncomparable and the engine's epoch-identity `==` over a seat's tools panicked on it. ONE OPERATION RULE FOR EVERY FAMILY OF WRITE TOOLS (`operations`, operations.go): the tracker's, the artifacts' and the datastore's writes offer `op_id` on the operator's MCP alone — minted for the call it names, taken back with that call only, refused before a write with any other — and refuse it wherever a turn or a request's own key already names the operation, in one set of sentences under one description, with `OperatorTools` the one place that says the surface holds its operations; only what a turn or a key DERIVES stays each family's own (`opIDFor`, `artifactCallNamespace`), each namespace fixed for the life of the format, because a per-family copy of the rule is where a skipped check makes a second write under an `op_id` promised to make nothing twice. THE ARTIFACTS TOOLS (artifacts*.go) are one per authority row, and each reads the stored artifact and asks `authz.DecideWithin` itself: for the caller, then for whoever the turn is acting for (the REQUESTER CEILING, composed by `ceilingOf`, the code tools' too), sparing only edits to an agent's own work; every byte goes through internal/artifactupload, a coding run's box is listed and streamed through the box itself and never handed a URL, no tool takes `force` or shares an agent's work with the whole company, and a stale publish is answered with the live version, fenced. A FOLDER GRANTS NOTHING, so an agent files (`move_artifact`: editor on the artifact and contributor on the destination, both through the ceiling) and makes subfolders (`create_artifact_folder`, five records a segment) but never reorganises a tree; a folder reference resolves only among folders the agent AND the requester see, and a stranger's turn sees none. THE ADOPTED BRAND (`get_company_brand`) is everybody's under every ceiling, its voice fenced under the `company` trust, and a logo or face reaches a version only as a `from_brand` slot the pipeline copies. THE DATASTORE TOOLS (artifactdata*.go) take that same half for the head, the decision and the stamp and add only the datastore and a turn guardrail (1,000 ops a segment, a retry not charged twice): a row is answered only when the requester could read it too, and a write is stamped with the lower of the agent's role and the requester's. Both sets put a seat in the artifact's PRESENCE room, viewing on a read and editing on a write, once the call was allowed. THE CODE TOOLS (codetools.go) are ONE LIST (`CodeTools`) that a seat's registry and the operator catalogue both take whole — twelve proven reads, each named for its authority row (`ClassEverybody`: the repository's GRANT decides, on the node that answers, from its own chart) — offered where the node's running runtime serves the code host (`engine.CodeToolDeps`, `OperatorCode`'s terms);
