the caller is named by ONE conversion (`CodeAskerOf`), which the REST reads take too, so one person is one asker on every surface, and no argument names who reads; and inside a turn somebody woke every call is decided AGAIN for them, the REQUESTER CEILING the artifacts tools apply, composed for both families in ONE place (`ceilingOf`, ceiling.go, over the one `Requesters`), where the seat's OWN voice among a turn's senders is no stranger's — the outcome of its own act, a workflow its push failed, is its own work — and any other sender with no known requester is: the tool's row through `authz.DecideWithin` first — a stranger, or a person who may not act now, reads nothing, refused with the ceiling's reason before the code host is asked, and a requester this node cannot compose is undecidable, never a turn nobody woke — and a colleague SEAT rides on every question the call asks (`CodeDeps.read`, `resolveRef` and `gitRead` stamp it as `CeilingSeat`) for the answering node to judge beside the seat's grant, its refusal (`code.ErrAboveCeiling`) answered forbidden with the ceiling's reason over the domain's sentence; a person bounds nothing (they maintain every repository), and the tools are offered only with the ceiling's halves wired (`CodeDeps.serves`: the code host, `Requesters` and `Chart`), so no surface reads for whoever messaged a seat whatever the seat itself may; rows (a repository, its refs, what a ref points at) are read at the seat's level, a repository resolved ONCE A TURN (`CodeDeps.repo`, on the turn's lookups, forgotten when a read answers the name with another repository or the id gone; `CodeDeps.repoRead` lets the call's own read be that resolution where its answer carries the repository's detail, so `code_get_repo`'s first page of branches resolves it and its first call is one barrier, not two — that page alone asking for the rules' merge gates, which code_get_repo answers whole beside the detail and no other resolution reads, `code_list_repos` answering each rule as a push is held to it; and A REPOSITORY AT THE DOMAIN'S BOUNDS IS ANSWERED — its rules with their gates are half a mebibyte, eight tool answers — so `code_get_repo` cuts its rules whole at the answer, keeping room for a branch, and pages the rest through `protections_next`/`protections_cursor`, and `code_list_repos` lists a repository whose rules alone are past an answer without them (`protections_left_out`) and goes on past it, never ending the listing there; `TestEveryRepositoryAtTheDomainsBoundsIsAnswered` holds both) and the refs a call names by ONE `code.resolve_ref` (`CodeDeps.resolveRef`, the one ref resolution: a comparison's two sides cost one barrier and are read at one moment), while a read by object id carries only the caller's floor as its git node's SYNC level — its own writes to that repository and where each ref it names last moved, never where the resolve read it, which made a current copy sync for nothing on every read by a ref; a file's lines, a patch, a blame's lines and every commit's subject a history or a blame names (`repo_commit`) are fenced at trust `external`, naming no writer — and the git node that cuts a history's or a blame's page counts each of those fences and every string's escape into the page, so a page of a hundred fenced subjects is one answer. THE CHANGE READS (codechanges.go) — `code_list_changes`, and `code_get_change` a change a PART at a time (summary, merge_readiness, files, diff, findings, reviews, comments) — are two of the twelve, and every change tool names the access its call needs on the change's repository in ONE TABLE (`changeToolNeeds`, held to `ChangeToolNames` and `ChangeWrites` both ways): a read's is read, the one access the answering node judges the requester ceiling at, and a write's the least access its decide may admit it at, the relation beyond it the deciding node's; a call reads the change's rows at the seat's level ONCE and every later read of the call at session floored at the first's position, and its reads by object id — a revision's files and patch from its recorded base, the interdiff since an earlier revision, ONE re-anchoring that places a page's findings in the revision asked about — at the floor of the records that moved the revisions' heads and their targets (a finding's placement at the HEAD revision's too, the latest any was raised on), never at a row read's checkpoint — each of those decided by the code domain's ONE rule (`code.RevisionDiff`, `code.ObjectFloor`, `code.PlaceFindings`, `RevisionRow.FilesGap`), which the API's change question reads by too, so a seat and a screen are shown one diff and one placement; a change's text NAMES ITS WRITER, whom its rows record — `change_description` under its owner (`provenance.TrustForPartial` once the change moved past its open), `change_review` under the reviewer and the raiser, `change_comment` under the author — and every page is cut WHOLE-ROW at one tool answer, fences and all (`fitLabelled`, a fresh labelled read a probe), resuming after the last row kept by that row's OWN cursor (`code.ReadQuery.ChangesCursor`, each part's row's `Cursor`), never a spelling of the reader's cursors written again here — and every page goes out COMPACT, because the git node cut it in compact JSON and indented it would arrive past the bound it was cut to fit; a page the fence lengthens past one answer is refused naming a narrower read, never cut; and the code tools' definitions, which every round of a seat of a company hosting code carries uncached, are held to a catalogue budget as the text backend renders them, the grammar every revision argument takes written ONCE, in `code_resolve`'s description. THE CODE WRITES (codewrite.go) join the same list where the code host's writes are wired (`CodeDeps.Writer`, `CodeWrites`): ten, each named for its authority row (`ClassEverybody`: the repository's GRANT decides, where the change is decided — the git node and the repository's committer for the five ENGINE WRITES, each landed as one push, and the data node for the five LIFECYCLE requests); each names ONE operation by the rule every write family takes (`operations.bindActor`, `opIDFor`), its object BOUNDED — the repository's id, the word `new` for a create — so no ref or name carries an id past `statelog.MaxCallerOpIDBytes`; a seat's push names its run (`code.RunProvenance`: its turn, its work item, the model the call was made on, `toolloop.CallModel`), a person writes as their human seat through their credential, and a token bound to nobody writes nothing, refused before anything is routed (`code.UnboundDetail`); inside a seat's turn somebody woke each write is decided again for them (`CodeDeps.writeCaller`, the REQUESTER CEILING the reads take, M3-decisions U40 a): the tool's row through `authz.DecideWithin` first, a requester this node cannot compose or decide answered unavailable, and one the row refuses — a stranger, a person who may not act now — becoming a ceiling of NOBODY rather than a refusal, because the seat's own work stays theirs to steer and only the deciding node reads whether a write is that; a colleague seat rides on the write (`code.WriteCeiling` on the engine write, the lifecycle request, and a change's proposal and conversation write — held there at the access `changeToolNeeds` names for its tool, which `code.ChangeProposal.CeilingNeeds` and `code.ConversationWrite.CeilingNeeds` are held to) and on every read the call makes; a person bounds nothing; and the ceiling is judged where the write is decided, never on this node; a seat's write is built at its session floored at its own writes to the repository, its turn's read of it and where every ref the turn resolved on it last moved (`refsRead`, noted on the turn's lookups by the one ref resolution, `refsOf`), so a write onto a head `code_resolve` read after it moved, or naming a commit by a ref, is judged on a copy that holds it in one sync rather than judged again at the log's head, a person's at the log's head; `code_protect_branch` changes a rule a push control at a time and its merge gate a field at a time, each list an item at a time (the controls `code.ProtectControlFields` names and the fields `code.GateChangeFields` names, decoded by `code.ProtectChange`'s and `code.GateChange`'s own spellings, resolved by the deciding data node against the rule its snapshot holds), sending nothing the call leaves out, and no lifecycle answer carries a repository's rules whole — sixty-four gated rules are past one answer, refused only after the write landed — a protect's carrying the one rule it set, whole as the code host answers it beside the row (`code.LifecycleResult.Rule`); `code_create_repo` forms every create at `search: grep`, the code domain's default for what a tool makes, and `code_update_repo`'s `search` is one settings change like `visibility`, the answer and the read tools' rows saying the mode a repository has; each delivers to the code host's surface (`code.Source`), none is ever a worker's (`OpenWorld: Yes`), and every refusal is answered in a class every write surface already renders (`pushClass`; a conflict's marked text fenced `repo_file`), an outcome nobody can account for — a git node gone silent, a lost acknowledgement — an unknown the same call made again finishes. THE CHANGE WRITES (codechangewrite.go, `ChangeWrites`, the last of `CodeWrites`) are a change's lifecycle — `code_open_change`, `code_update_change`, `code_close_change` — each ONE `code.ChangeProposal` the repository's committer decides from its own chart (`CodeWriter.Change`), named by the same rule (verb `code-change`, object the repository's id): an open's change is derived from its operation, and its ask of its `reviewers` is a SECOND write, a `statelog.StepOpID` step of the open's operation (`stepTwo`, ONE byte, since the operator's longest step already holds 125 of the id's 128) decided at or past the open's own record, whose failure leaves the change open and says so on the answer (`reviewers_not_asked`), never as the open's; every other write names the change by its number, reads it ONCE at the seat's level for its id and is decided at or past that read, holding the caller's `expected_version`; an open carries the turn's work item unless the call names one — a native key resolved by the tracker's own read (`CodeDeps.Tasks`), any tracker's `<backend>:<id>`, or `none` — every label fitted (`code.FitProvenance`); and every refusal is answered in an existing class (`changeClass`: forbidden, stale_version, exists, invalid, conflict, not_found, unavailable), `changeToolNeeds` naming each one's least access. Its CONVERSATION's writes (codechangeconverse.go) — `code_request_review`, `code_review_change`, `code_comment_on_change`, `code_resolve_finding` — are each ONE `code.ConversationWrite` the change writer decides (`CodeWriter.Converse`), after the call's ONE read of the change at the seat's level (`conversing`, over `changeRead`) and at or past it: a review is ONE record, its findings and carried findings with it, a seat's naming its turn and the call's model; a finding and an anchored comment are anchored where their lines were read — the revision's head on the new side, its recorded base on the old (`changeRead.recorded` refusing a base not yet recorded), each blob from ONE read of the revision's diff kept to their files at the revision's floor (`conversing.anchor`), on a file the revision changes or refused; a reply marking its thread makes the mark a `stepTwo` step decided at or past the reply's record, its failure said beside the reply (`thread_not_set`); a review and a comment are annotated NOT idempotent (additive, as `comment_on_work_item`) and a finding's answer DESTRUCTIVE (a dismissal clears a blocker nothing sets back); every refusal in an existing class (`conversationClass`; `superseded`, the home's own, none). And `code_apply_suggestions` (codechangesuggest.go) is ONE engine write (`code.WriteApplySuggestions`, `CodeWriter.GitWrite`) of the findings the call names — read a page of the conversation at a time from the call's read of the change, each standing, suggesting and on the new side or refused before anything is written — onto the change's source branch at the caller's `expected_head`, the CALLER's commit, which makes them an author of the change; once it landed, its findings are answered resolved with its commit as a `stepTwo` step decided at or past the push's record, its failure said beside the commit (`findings_not_resolved`), an outdated apply `stale_version` and an overlapping one `invalid`. THE SCRATCH HOLD's one seam (`HoldRepo`, codehold.go): every resolution of a call's `repo` (`CodeDeps.repoRead`, on a memo miss) takes the turn's hold on a scratch repository before the call's first request about it — RECORDED on its subject (`code.LifecycleHold`, one per run and repository, never per work key, so a redelivered turn records its own — minted where the run's own derived ids are, `runIdentity`'s instant: its rebase on an attempt the engine rebased, since a record minted at a start every node's ledger has swept is answered `unknown` for good and refuses every call about the repository) where it is idle `code.HoldRecordAfter` — and a create holds the id its decide will derive before it is sent; the turn's lookups keep only resolutions whose hold was taken, so `code_status`'s hot list, which names no repository a call chose, reads and keeps nothing (`TestOnlyTheSeamResolvesARepositoryForATurn`). THE REF HOLD's one seam beside it (`HoldRef`, codehold.go): every seat's branch a turn names passes through it before the request that reads or writes through it — the one ref read (`CodeDeps.refsOf`, the only `code.RefQuery` in the package, `TestOnlyTheSeamResolvesARefForATurn`) holds every seat's branch it answers, under every rev, target and ref a tool resolves and every ref a launch resolves; a write's branch (`CodeDeps.branch`) is held as a read is, its head resolved first, because a write that moves nothing orders nothing, and a branch it creates is held with no read; the change tools hold what a change depends on through the same seam — an open's source and target, a retarget's target, and the source a reopen reopens at and a suggestions apply commits onto (`TestAChangeWriteHoldsTheSeatBranchItDependsOn`); and a test classifies every argument of every code tool, so no new one escapes it (`TestEveryCodeToolHoldsTheSeatRefsItNamesBeforeItsRequest`). A branch unset `code.RefHoldRecordAfter` is RECORDED, decided at the resolve's checkpoint; one deleted since answers `not_found`, naming the commit to restore it at. A turn's hold on a key is CONFIRMED once (`turnctx.Holds.Confirm`, M3-decisions M3-O65), so a write resolves its branch for the hold at most once a turn and never where no hold is taken, and an idle scratch repository is recorded once a turn. A CODING RUN'S REPOSITORIES ARE CODE READS TOO: `run_sandbox` takes `repos` only where the code tools are offered (`CodeDeps.serves`), parsed strictly before the launcher is asked, and the launcher resolves them through `CodeDeps.ResolveRunRepos` — the code tools' one resolution, so each repository is read at the seat's level under the requester ceiling (`callerOn`, on `authz.ActionCodeRead`) and held by the same seam before its first request, and every ref a grant needs (its branch, a base named as a ref, the default branch, the branch an `open_change` proposes into) is read in ONE `code.RefQuery` (`refsOf`, which `resolveRef` reads through) — and a refusal is the call's own result (`RefusedLaunch`), naming the repository, never a launch failure. A `change` it names is read once through the change writes' own read (`changeOf`, linearizable, closed and merged refused) and decides the branch and the base; an `open_change` is resolved to its target at launch (`openTarget`, the title held to `code.TitleProblem`) and refused where the run could not push its branch, so an ask the collection could never open costs no box. Beside the code reads and on their terms, the operator-only `code_repo_readers` (`ReadersDeps`, `engine.CodeReaderDeps`) answers who read a `visibility: unit` repository with ONE ANSWER the registry's question of the same name gives its REST route and the socket too (`ReadersOf`, `ReadersRefusal`): the repository read by name at the seat's level, then its readers a page at a time over a range of company days, a range or a cursor no read takes refused before anything is read, the page cut whole-row as it is sent; no seat's registry ever holds it. THE HEALTH TOOLS (healthtools.go) are ONE LIST (`HealthTools`) both surfaces take — `fleet_health`, every live node as its own heartbeat states it with the alarms its own table has firing, and `code_status`, every git node's account of its cache and the fleet's hottest repositories (`engine.CodeStatus`'s scatter, merged by the cache's one heat rule, `cache.Hottest`), or one repository on each — proven reads that JUDGE NOTHING: what is wrong is the alarm table's to say (ADR-0015), so neither tool ranks a node or names a level, and a node that evaluated no table says so rather than reading as healthy. They are admin's, and a SEAT reads them only where its role carries the health grant; `Withheld` asks `Admits` of every builtin a seat's registry holds ahead of the turn, and the runner leaves each one refused off every surface (`runner.Config.Withheld`), so what a seat is offered and what it is admitted are one decision — and only a REFUSAL withholds, since a node that cannot decide offers the tool and lets the call decide. Beside it the engine leaves off `CodeWritesWithheld(tier)`: every code write from a seat whose `role.code.access` caps it below write (`code.Tier.Cap`: read_only, unset, review), at which no repository's grant admits one — a push needs write or full_access's own namespace, a lifecycle request maintain — so a read_only seat, the default, neither carries their definitions on every round nor is told to commit with them; the TIER and not the authority table, since a role grant there is an administrator's and a unit's lead sets its seats' tier. The grant is the deployment's view, never a repository's: a seat's `code_status` judges every hot repository by the seat's own grant to it, through the code tools' one resolution, and says how many it left out — and those are CODE READS, which the requester ceiling holds: they go through the code tools' one composition (`CodeDeps.callerOn`), so a colleague who woke the turn rides on every one and a stranger's turn reads nothing, with whoever woke it decided on the code host's read verb (`authz.ActionCodeRead`) rather than on the health row, which the ceiling does not hold any more than it holds `fleet_health`; and `HealthTools` serves `code_status` on the code reads' own terms (`CodeDeps.serves`), never over a code host wired without the ceiling's halves. `code_mark_inbox` is the operator catalogue's alone and a person's own: a person's gesture over their code notices (`CodeWriter.Mark`), read by the one gesture parser every domain's mark tool shares (`gestureOf`). `code_rebase_change` (codechangerebase.go) is ONE engine write (`code.WriteRebaseChange`, `CodeWriter.GitWrite`) of the change its call names, read in the call's one read of it — its source at the head its caller read (`expected_head`), under an operation of its own verb (`code-rebase`) and made for whoever woke the turn (`EngineWrite.Ceiling`, which the git node holds the force-push to as every branch write's) — answered as every engine write is, its conflict fenced through `conflictedWrite`, the one rendering a revert's and a cherry-pick's take too; authz row `{ClassEverybody, RecencyAny}`, `changeToolNeeds` read, never handed to a worker. `code_merge_change` (codechangemerge.go) is ONE engine write (`code.WriteMergeChange`) of the change at the head its caller read, answered MERGED, QUEUED at its place, or refused in the class of its own `not_ready` naming EVERY unmet precondition and whom each waits on — never the first alone, so one answer says everything between a change and its merge — its `delete_branch` unsaid deleting a seat's branch unless a gate over the target keeps it (`code.GateRules.KeepsSource`, read as the git node reads the gates, `seatSourceDeleted`), and its `close_task` closing the change's native work item as a `stepTwo` step through the tracker's one-actor writer (`CodeDeps.Closer`), decided first as `update_work_item`'s own gate decides it (the tracker app's row, `authz.ActionWorkUpdate`), so a person without the tracker merges and closes nothing; a close that fails or is refused said as a residue beside the merge ("merged; ENG-42 not closed: …"), never as the merge's refusal; `code_dequeue_change` reads the target's queue a page at a time to the change's own live entry and sends ONE `code.QueueProposal` (`CodeWriter.Queue`) as the caller with no run, since an entry is its enqueuer's and never a turn's, carrying the turn's ceiling for the committer to hold it to (`code.QueueProposal.Ceiling`), as the merge's engine write carries it for the git node; and `code_revert_change` (codechangerevert.go) is ONE engine write (`code.WriteRevertChange`) reverting a merged change on the caller's own new branch (`seats/<handle>/revert-<n>`, a person's `revert-<n>`), held before it is sent, whose push opens the revert's own change — a branch already there `exists` — at a floor raised to where the change was read merged, and, asked to `merge`, that change merged as a `stepTwo` step from the commit the revert made, a merge it could not make said as a residue. All three `changeToolNeeds` read and never handed to a worker. THE CHECK TOOLS (codechecks.go) name a commit by `change` (its head, for its target) or `sha` with `checked_for` — never `commit`/`target`, which the hold gates read as refs a call must hold, while a check's key is a whole id and a branch nothing is read through: `code_search` (`codesearch.go`) is a READ listed after the twelve and before the writes, served where `CodeDeps.Searcher` (`CodeSearcher`, the engine's own `CodeSearch`) is wired: `query` in codeindex's one grammar, the filters beside it joined by `codeindex.Query.Beside` (the `code_search` question's rule too), asked AS the caller with the requester ceiling's seat; in a seat's turn every repository it names resolved through `CodeDeps.repo` and a ref in each through `refsOf` (both holding) and the search floored at their `RefsFloor`, a ref that may be a seat's branch refused in a search naming no repository; each hit fenced `repo_file`, the answer cut whole hits at `ToolAnswerBytes` (`fitLabelled`) with a cursor keyed by the last hit sent and its search's fingerprint — never a count, which a second search's new line would shift; `code_get_check_failures` is a READ beside the code reads (`CodeToolNames`), one attempt's failures paged `failures:<i>` (`fitLabelled`) and its kept log `log:<offset>` 32 KiB a page (`checkLogPage`) through `CodeDeps.Logs` (`CheckLogReader`, the fleet's `objstore.Store.ReadAt`; nil answers every field but the log), each page cut back to a whole character so no fence holds half of one; `code_get_change`'s `checks` part a page of at most 12 (`changeChecksPage`), cut WHOLE-ROW at one tool answer — twelve at their widest, a 4 KiB summary and a 2 KiB link each, are past one — and resumed after its last line by the listing's own cursor (`code.ReadQuery.ChecksCursor`); every summary, tail, failure message and log page fenced `check_log` at `external` naming no writer. `code_rerun_checks` and `code_report_check` are WRITES (`CheckWrites`, `CodeWriter.Checks`, the estate's `code.checks`): a rerun names each check with the attempt its caller read as latest and asks for the next (`code.CheckRerun`, ≤ 16 a call, `maxRerunsPerCall`, so each is a one-hex-digit `StepOpID` of the call's op), `all_failed` naming only what the caller may rerun (a seat: errored or cancelled); a report is a PERSON'S (authz `humanOnly`, refused in the tool again), its attempt read off the commit's rows — the latest while it runs, the next once it ended unless it is that ending sent again — for the repository's default branch where `checked_for` is left out; a `CheckRefusal` maps onto existing classes (`checkRefused`: no_such_check/repo_deleted not_found, rerun_genuine/attempts_exhausted/not_permitted forbidden, not_finished conflict, busy/unavailable/not_applied unavailable, the rest invalid); authz rows `{ClassEverybody, RecencyAny}`, the report's `humanOnly`; never handed to a worker. `code_purge_unreachable` (codepurge.go, in `CodeWrites` as `PurgeWrites`) is a PERSON'S request that a repository's maintainer expunge every object no ref reaches, with no grace: authz `{ClassEverybody, humanOnly, RecencyStepUp}`, the grant held to maintain in the tool, a token bound to nobody refused `code.UnboundDetail`; its request id is the call's operation (`opIDFor`), so the same call again is the same request the committer's ledger collapses (`CodeWriter.Expunge`, the estate's `code.expunge`); answered `{request, position, maintainer, holds{git_nodes, backups_before, store_until}}`, the maintainer and the git nodes read through `CodeDeps.Placement` (the engine's router, by `cache.PlacementCharge`), a placement it cannot read SAID (`unplaced`) beside a request recorded all the same; destructive and idempotent; never offered to a seat. `code_import_repo` (codeimport.go, the last of `CodeWrites` as `ImportWrites`) is a PERSON'S create of a project filled from a repository at another host — a lifecycle create carrying `LifecycleRequest.Import`, or with `mirror` the settings' `MirrorFrom` and no import beside it (a pull mirror imports from its own source) — which the repository's home fetches (code/mirror): authz `{ClassEverybody, humanOnly, RecencyAny}` (U49 a: an import dials another host, so never an agent's, and the deciding data node refuses a seat's create carrying either again), a token bound to nobody refused `code.UnboundDetail`; `credential` is a variable's NAME in UPPER CASE, made the `${NAME}` the record carries — upper case because a forge's token (`ghp_…`) is a valid reference name, and taken as one it would be recorded on the settings; the repository's id derived from the call's operation as `code_create_repo`'s is, so the same call again is the same create; answered as a create (`codeRepoWrite.answer`), the repository `importing`; idempotent, not destructive; never offered to a seat. |
| `agent/colleague` | Resolving a free-text query to a seat — the lookup before an A2A ask, a chat mention or a page mention. A model types what it remembers, so the answer is either one seat or an honest list of who it might be, NEVER a guess. Four tiers, earlier ones short-circuiting later, so a query that is exactly somebody's handle is never fuzzy-matched against everybody else |
| `agent/turnctx` | What a turn IS, carried as an explicit argument rather than ambient state. A turn has at least five values that want to travel ambiently, and a goroutine SHARES whatever context it captured — forever, including after the turn that created it finished — so what would elsewhere merely obscure a dependency is a live data race here. A turn's LOOKUPS (`Lookups`) hold what it resolved by name once — a code tool's repository — and facts those lookups established that a later call builds on (how far the refs it resolved on a repository had moved, taken with `LoadOrStore` so callers racing to be first share one value), keyed like a context by its consumer's own type, shared by every Turn derived from it and empty again on a resumed segment; they authorize nothing, since the node answering each later read judges the asker again. A turn's HOLDS (`Holds`) are what the code host could delete from under it on nobody's request — the scratch repositories it named and the seats' branches it read, wrote or launched on, by opaque keys — taken before each request, a call's keys in ONE write to the turn's run row (`Take` takes them together, so a comparison of two seats' branches is one compare-and-set, not two) and a launch's resolution's in the set the launch writes on its row anyway (`ForLaunch`: owed to the row until that write, so a relaunch makes no hold write of its own), kept in memory and on the turn's run row once it has one; beside the set they mark which keys THIS PROCESS has CONFIRMED (`Confirm`, M3-decisions M3-O65) — in memory only, never on the row, because the row carries keys taken unconfirmed too, so a resumed turn confirms again. A seat's PRINCIPAL is derived by ONE function from the organization and the seat (`SeatPrincipal`), which `Principal` calls for a turn, so the engine decides what a seat is offered as the very principal its calls are decided as. A turn's HOLDS (`Holds`, M3-O61) are the code repositories it has named, taken BEFORE each request about one goes out and never released while the turn is unended, so the code host's scratch expiry spares them: at most `MaxHolds` (1024) a turn, written first to the turn's run row where it has one (`Attach`'s writer) and only then held — save a launch's own, which its row write carries before its box exists, and which any other call naming one writes first — and a `Seal` around a launch makes a hold taken meanwhile wait for the row, so no hold is ever only in memory while the turn lives on a row. |
| `tools` | The registry, holding four kinds of tool under one contract and recording WHICH at registration — the only frame that knows, and the last that can say. A FIRST-PARTY refusal also carries its CLASS (`tools.Refusal`, defined in `mcp`) beside its sentence, AS ITS CAUSE — `Classify` states it beneath the error that decided it, `RefusalOf` reads it back and `UnknownOf` asks whether a write may have landed, with no class field beside the cause to tell one caller two stories — because the sentence is prompt text tuned for a model and a person's surface cannot parse it; an MCP server's failure stays unclassified, since the engine will not guess a class from prose it did not write. `TestEveryFirstPartyRefusalIsClassified` walks the source for a failed result that forgot — the surface's own recorded `Call` and the loop's `ToolResult` included, because the surface refuses an unknown, unoffered or guarded call before any tool runs |
| `mcp` | MCP client and child supervision. A stdio server is a process TREE, not a process — and its environment is `hostbox`'s allowlist, the engine user's HOME and XDG directories, and what its `env:` and the seat's `mcp_env` declare, NEVER the engine's own: a server that reads anything else declares it, with a `${VAR}` reference to pass the host's value through, and lists the key under the server's `optional_keys` where a host may set none. A server whose `command` is `docker` or `podman` IS the runtime's CLI and gets `hostbox.ContainerRuntime`, the runtime's own settings beside the host user's locations, because a rootless runtime without its `DOCKER_HOST` dials the system socket. The package doc says why the obvious `os.Environ()` is wrong. Every server a bridge runs is SUPERVISED until it is stopped: the engine makes the child's one Wait itself rather than the SDK's transport, and watches the transport's read too, so a death — or a session that ended while its process lives on — is an event and not a broken pipe found by the next call. The server is restarted on ONE schedule (`RestartWait`: doubling and jittered from one seat heartbeat to the undead alarm interval, at once after a healthy run), the same one a failed start is retried on whoever retries it, its tools staying listed and refusing `unavailable` meanwhile through the SAME Tool objects, which reach their server rather than one session. Whether this node may start a per-role child on its own is the owner's call (`Supervision.MayStart`), because the child is a seat's identity; a shared server's failed start is retried by its bridge, since nothing else would ask again, and a seat's by the seat's runner, so the bridge keeps nothing of it |
| `a2a` | Agent-to-agent asking: one ask, one answer, then closed. THE CHANNEL IS AN AUTHORIZATION RECORD, NOT A TRANSPORT — nothing queues here, and both the brief and the reply travel over the durable seat inbox, so a colleague owned by another node is an ordinary target. The in-process bus this replaced woke the target on whichever node owned ITS seat, where the queue was empty |
| `tracker` | The native work tracker as `statelog`'s FIRST DOMAIN — its records, its subjects and the arithmetic that has no database in it. Every change is one record published to the subject of the object it changes, so THE SUBJECT IS THE ARBITRATION UNIT rather than a routing label: two writers racing on one task contend at the broker and exactly one wins, and two writers on different tasks never contend at all. The record decodes in TWO PASSES — an envelope every build reads and a version-gated payload — which is what lets a node file a newer peer's record under its subject, probe for it, drop it through a gate and reprocess it later; a record that failed to decode at all yields no id, no kind and no subject to file it under. Its `Scope` is the complete set of objects an apply may write, stated by the WRITER, and the paths NEST — an object's path is written under its container — because that is the only shape under which a record deferred on a project blocks a write to a task in it while a record deferred on one task does not block its neighbour. `rank.go` is the manual order as a VALUE TYPE: a byte-comparable key with a magnitude head that borrows and carries, so repeated head insertion grows the key by about a character per sixty-two placements rather than exhausting a thirty-symbol alphabet, and the two mint sets — a create's pure integer at or above the origin, and every `rankorder` mint below it or carrying a fraction — are disjoint BY THE SHAPE OF THE VALUE, so the proof mentions no counter, no staleness and no in-flight create. A DEPENDENCY is the one relation with two ends and therefore the one gesture that is a SEQUENCE — `waiting_on` AUTHORED on the dependent, `Dependents` MIRRORED on the blocker — and the asymmetry decides everything: the counterparties are read before the first append so a full, tombstoned or absent blocker refuses the edge; the authored commit lands; the mirror is BEST EFFORT, because the edge is durable without it and refusing the whole gesture over it would lose what somebody asked for. The residue is a ONE-SIDED edge, which the applier DERIVES rather than the record carries — whether a blocker lists this task is a fact about the other end's rows, so a writer stating it would be stating what it read in another transaction on another subject, which is why the flag had no producer and the partial index the schema ships for the repair duty was over a column that was zero on every row. `one_sided_final` is the opposite kind of fact and stays on the record: the duty's DECISION that this edge will never be mirrored. Every collection a record carries whole takes a GESTURE resolved inside the decide — `Watch`, `Relate`, `Depend`, `Checklist` — for one reason each time: a caller cannot form a set it read in another transaction, and a cap governs the set the write LANDS on. And the routing precedence is ORDER IN ONE FUNCTION: `Candidates` offers the eighteen reasons to `internal/routing`'s builder, whose add-once rule is what makes the order the precedence; `Reasons` writes the same order down a second time, for the surfaces that print and filter by reason, and a test that walks every kind with a different handle in every routing field holds the two together, so a moved arm and a moved entry both fail it. `Route` is that package's post-filter with the tracker's one reason that reaches the actor (`unblocked`) bound in — the same predicate the applier's notice rows leave the actor out by. What THIS COMMIT did to you outranks the role you hold, which is what `mention`, `asked`, `answered` and `blocking` sit above `assignee` for — behind it `blocking` could name nobody at all, since the only handle it ever adds is the blocker's own assignee. `coerce.go` is the CUSTOM-FIELD table and it is PURE over values, for the reason `textindex`'s arithmetic is: a rule exercised only through a database is a rule nobody re-reads. It runs at the WRITE because that is the only place a value can be REFUSED — the applier deliberately salvages, since refusing a malformed value there would stop that task's every later change on every data node — and it NORMALISES what it accepts, so the record carries one canonical form and every data node writes identical rows without re-deciding. Nothing is rounded to fit: a field exact to two places refuses `3.14159` rather than storing a number nobody typed. The one seam it declares is the ROSTER, because a people field's value belongs to the EPOCH rather than to a row; a relationship's task is deliberately not a seam, being a row this package reads inside the decide's own transaction. Its AUTHORITY VALUES — `ProjectAuthority`, `TagAuthority`, `PersonAuthority` — state the TABLE'S DECISION and never its inputs: they used to carry a lead flag and an "is an operator" flag that every caller combined for itself, which was a second copy of the rule that consulted no permission, so an administrator was refused here while every HTTP route allowed. The caller asks `internal/authz` and passes what came back; this package keeps only the half a chart cannot know — which FACET each answer unlocks — and the one rule of its own, which is about the gesture rather than anybody's authority: an AGENT never re-orders a colleague's queue, even one it leads, because that is a hand-off in disguise that bypasses the guarded take and the reassignment budget. A person's MARKS over its notices — read, unread, snoozed, the seen-through position and the primary reasons — are `inbox.Marks` embedded in its `Person` record, so the record's JSON keeps every key it had, moved by `inbox.Apply` inside `MarkInbox`'s decide and read by one `inbox.Classifier` that the inbox scan's filters and the page's marks share; a gesture the inbox rules refuse on what it says is re-marked `ErrInvalid` HERE, its sentence unchanged, so every surface that classes this package's refusals classes it, and a list at its ceiling stays `inbox.ErrFull`, whose remedy is `read_through`. Its applier also says WHOSE INBOX a committed batch moved: one `inbox.Movement` per recipient per batch, naming `tracker` as its source — identifiers and a count, never content — announced POST-COMMIT, because the transaction body can re-run and a movement announced inside it could describe a write that rolled back, and noted only when the notice row was actually written, so a redelivery the history guard already absorbed announces nothing. The count is a HINT, keyed on (record, recipient). Its query grammar is every native read's freshness grammar too (`ParseFreshness`), and `min_position` is the one key that REPEATS (`RepeatedKeys`, read through `Params.List` and `ListOf`, so a socket frame's array and a query string's repeats name the same floors): one floor per log an answer is read from, every one carried to the read, whose reader refuses a floor on a log it does not read |
| `inbox` | A PERSON'S INBOX AS EVERY NATIVE DOMAIN KEEPS ONE: the marks a person lays over one domain's notices (`Marks`: read, unread and snoozed `Entry` lists, the seen-through `Position` triple and the primary reasons, generic over the domain's reason type), the one `Gesture` that moves them, `CheckGesture` and `Apply`, the `Classifier` a page of notices is marked by, `SnoozeScope`, and the `Movement` an applier announces after a committed batch with the `Batch` that collects it. ONE SET OF RULES, KEPT BY EACH DOMAIN ON ITS OWN LOG: a second copy would drift exactly where an inbox cannot afford it — a mark one copy prunes and the other keeps — and one record for every domain would put positions on several logs in one number space nothing can compare, and a gesture across two logs in two decides that cannot be one. A GESTURE, NEVER THE LISTS: a person says "this one is done", "put that off", "read through here", and the domain's writer applies it to the record its DECIDE reads, so two screens marking two notices both land; the position moves first and the marks after, so "read through here but keep this one unread" means what it says, and the lists hold only the exceptions the position does not answer, pruned on every write rather than capped by discarding (`MaxEntries`, 256 a list, refused `ErrFull`). THE SCAN AND THE PAGE ARE ONE RULE: a domain filters its own scan by the Classifier's sets and marks the page by the same Classifier, because a filter applied to a page after reading it short-paged it; an unread mark outranks the position, the position covers only its own stream, and a snooze is asleep until its instant everywhere at once. A MOVEMENT IS IDENTIFIERS AND A COUNT — source, handle, unread delta, subject, reason, five fields a test holds — never content, because it rides a socket frame to whoever watches the seat and content there would be a read path around the inbox question's authority. Its refusals are its own sentinels (`ErrInvalid`, `ErrFull`) with the sentence a person reads (`Sentence`); a domain's writer classes the invalid ones as its own. THE HORIZON IS READ ONE WAY: `Horizon(epoch)` reads the company's `inbox.retention_days` under the one `RetentionKey`, with `RetentionDefaultDays` (config's default, a test holds the two equal) where the epoch states none, so every domain's applier that declines a notice past it and every sweep that deletes one hold one horizon. A LEAF above the state log's position: every domain that keeps an inbox imports it, and nothing here reaches back into one. `Compose` READS EVERY DOMAIN'S PAGE AND MERGES THEIR HEADS by the authored instant, then source, then record — never a sort of the union — so what it takes of each source is a prefix of that source's page and the composed cursor carries ONE position per source. THE CODE HOST KEEPS ITS OWN: a person's code marks are `code_person_marks`, written by `marks` records on their own `person.<handle>` subject of the code log, never on the tracker's person record — a position counts on one stream. |
| `changefeed` | How a committed record becomes a WAKE: a durable, fleet-wide pull consumer over one ESTATE — a state machine's log. The estate is a SEAM rather than a document family (a coordination type that left with the last projector), because a log delivery has no family, no key class and no change record, and manufacturing a family for one would have started a projector over a bucket that does not exist: a `Source` names it as a string, an `Opener` opens one consumer over it, and a `Record` is one delivery from it. The principle is that a wake is derived by something that OUTLIVES the writer, never published by the writer's goroutine as a courtesy — the chat path already swallows a failed publish and the webhook path is safe only because the vendor retries, so the durable record is the retry. A group rather than a duty, because a lease flap on a duty holder would stall the whole company's notifications for stateless work. TWO dedupe layers, each covering the other's gap: a claim collapses a redelivered message and FAILS OPEN (a coordination store that cannot be reached must not silently stop notifications), and a deterministic per-recipient wake id lets the inbox and the completion ledger collapse what the open claim let through. A first-party wake also carries the COMMITTING RECORD'S POSITION (`trigger_position`), and the turn it starts observes it before its first read, so a woken seat never reads the change that woke it as not there yet — whichever holder answers |
| `routing` | WHO A COMMITTED RECORD CONCERNS, AND WHICH OF THEM IT WAKES — the one rule every native domain routes by, lifted out of the tracker so the next domain to route a change routes it by the same rule rather than a copy: a `Builder` that keeps ONE REASON PER HANDLE, the first offered, so the ORDER a domain offers its reasons in IS its precedence, decided in that domain's own candidate function — and a domain that also declares the reasons as a list in that order for the surfaces that print and filter by them (the tracker's `Reasons`) holds the list to the function with a test that walks every kind with a different handle in every field, so a moved arm and a moved entry both fail; the ORDERED FALLBACK (`Fallback`, a chain whose rank is a link's place in it, empty links included), kept only when nobody ordinary survives and then only its lowest-ranked survivor — a chain rather than one lead because the actor drop would otherwise empty it for a lead filing into their own team, and only one link because waking the whole chain is how a fallback gets filtered out; `Route`'s four steps, in order (inbox-only out, the actor out except under the domain's `wakesActor`, departed handles out, then the fallback), asking the roster ONLY ABOUT A HANDLE IT COULD WAKE — never the actor, never a chain while somebody ordinary survives, and a chain link by link in rank order until one is still employed — so a handle a domain counts as a dropped recipient is one, and a snapshot of hundreds costs a lookup per would-be recipient rather than per name; and `Delivery`, the five things a reader can say about who one record reached, with `DeliveryOf` the one place its three EMPTY states are told apart. TWO FUNCTIONS, never one: where a domain's APPLIER writes a notice row per candidate, its candidate set is pure over its record, so the applier computes it inside its transaction and every data node writes the same rows, and only `Route` needs what a record cannot carry — the roster as it stands now. Who acted IS on the record, which is why `Candidate.Hears` is the one step of Route an applier takes too, so the notice rows and the wake leave the actor out by ONE predicate. GENERIC over the domain's reason type (`Candidate[R ~string]`) rather than a string field, so the reasons STAY THE DOMAIN'S closed set at the offer and at every read — the tracker passes `Reason.WakesActor` as it is, and a plain string would accept any reason at the offer and need a conversion at each read; the knowledge base, with plain-string reasons and no notice rows (its one fallback link comes from the chart its parser holds), is the degenerate case. EVERY OFFER IS HELD TO ADD-ONCE, a notice about another subject than the record's own included: a record reaches one handle ONCE in both places that keep what it reached — a notice row keyed on record and recipient, a wake whose id is derived from the two — so a second notice to one person on one record would collapse into the first and never be delivered, and a domain with news about another subject writes it on that subject's own record, as the tracker's unblocked repair tells a dependent's assignee in a commit on the dependent. Standard library only: an applier runs it and every domain imports it, so it opens no store, reads no clock and holds no roster |
| `alarmwake` | A node's ALARM TRANSITIONS as wakes (ADR-0065): the notify spine's parser and prompt for the inbound source `alarms`. The delivery has ONE SHAPE, written by `Encode` (the engine's outbox) and read by `Decode` (this parser), so the two ends cannot disagree about it; a delivery naming no id, node, alarm or change is a parse failure the spine records, and a change this build does not know is read and repeated, a newer node's word for what its alarm did. Routed through `routing` to every AGENT seat whose role carries `health_tools` — `orgchart.GrantedSeats`, the rule the health tools admit by, so who is woken and who may read are one set — under one reason and no actor, a seat the company no longer employs dropped; each wake's id is DERIVED from the transition's own id and the seat (`WakeID`) and dated by the transition, so a republish collapses in the inbox and the completion ledger and a granted seat is woken once per transition. PER EPOCH, built by `engine.nativeParsers` on every node whatever halves it runs. The prompt is a POINTER: the alarm, the node, the log, the measurement and the table's own meaning and remedy (`statelog.Describe`), then `fleet_health` before the seat acts, since a later transition may already have followed; it addresses nobody, and ONE ALARM IS ONE CONVERSATION wherever it stands, so a flap, or one fleet-wide condition every data node raises at once, is one thread and one look rather than a turn per node. |
| `pages` | The engine's own KNOWLEDGE BASE — containers, pages, revisions and the change record — and `statelog`'s THIRD domain, its second STRICT one, which is what tells a per-domain checkpoint from a per-node one. There is no projection and no coordination family behind it any more: a write is one record on `CREWLET_PAGES_LOG`, an applier writes every data node's rows, and a read reports the POSITION it answered at rather than a hydration boolean. Adopting the log removed all three of the two-key sequences the bucket forced: a create is ONE record whose apply writes the title, the head, the first revision and the history entry together, so there is no orphan claim and no grace rule for stepping over one. THE SUBJECT IS THE ADDRESS — a create arbitrates on the TITLE rather than on the new page's uuid, because two writers must contend for a name and two uuids never would — and the title travels as a bounded TOKEN, since a subject is a broker path and a title is prose carrying spaces, dots and wildcards; the applier recomputes it from the payload and REFUSES a record that took one address and claimed another. Plus the three things a wiki has that a tracker does not. A TITLE IS AN ADDRESS, so it is unique per container and it is what a create ARBITRATES ON. A BODY HAS A HISTORY, so revision N is the body at version N, including the newest — the other reading, that a revision holds what was replaced, collides with itself on the first save and leaves the current body reachable only through the head; a save must state the version it edited, unlike a work item's optional If-Match, because there is no per-field merge that makes overwriting prose safe. And a page is SEARCHED rather than filtered, so `Searcher` fulfils the `knowledge` seam and the native backend is a drop-in for Confluence. A RENAME IS ITS OWN OPERATION rather than a field of a save, because one record has one subject and a record carrying both an address change and a content change could arbitrate only one of them; it stamps the head's `scoped_through` and never its `version`, so the page's own expectation is never poisoned. A COMMENT DOES NOT SUBSCRIBE ITS COMMENTER here, which is the opposite of the tracker's participants rule and deliberate: a page a hundred people have remarked on would otherwise wake a hundred seats when somebody fixes a heading. Its parser routes by `internal/routing` as that package's DEGENERATE CASE — `mention` over `watcher`, a one-link chain to the container's lead offered only for a page created, trashed or changing status (`LeadWorthy`), and no reason that wakes the actor — so a departed watcher, a lead who is no longer a seat and the page's own author are dropped by the same four steps as the tracker's, rather than by a second copy of them kept in step by hand. The applier is also what NOTICES a tool-skill page moving — natively there is no page webhook and the change feed deliberately drops those changes, so the apply, which already derives the skill flag, is the only thing that sees both an arrival and a departure. THE RESERVED CONTAINERS ARE THE STORE'S RULE, not a tool's: it was one check on `write_page`, so a seat that could not create a page in the skills container could still save over one, rename one and comment on one, and every surface added later would have inherited the hole silently — now every write path asks, reading the keys live because they are Tier B. The two containers are reserved for DIFFERENT REASONS and refuse different gestures, which is `Reserved`'s shape: the SKILLS container is machinery excluded from search and routing, so an AGENT is refused every write there — create, save, rename and every comment path; the ORG ROOT is the company's canon and is searched and routed like any other, so an agent is refused only ADDING a page. Folded into one list, every agent write into the root was refused with a sentence claiming its pages were unreadable. It is a rule about the ACTOR and not about capability: a seat cannot see that a page there is machinery, and a person publishing there on purpose is what those containers are for. The CAPABILITY half is the tool layer's, because the store exempts every person and holds no permissions: a person's create, save, rename, trash, restore or purge of a page in the SKILLS container is asked `pages.skill.write` (`admin`, never a seat) once the container is known — a tool skill is injected into every seat's turn, so the `knowledge` app alone had been a way to rewrite the prompt the whole company runs under. A PAGE IS CREATED ONLY IN A DECLARED SPACE (`Reserved.Declared`: every unit's and seat's `space:`, `knowledge.root_space` and the skills container, from `engine.reservedContainers` over `chartContainers`), refused for a person and an agent alike by `refuseUndeclared` — a separate check with neither of `refuseReserved`'s early returns, so a store with no `Reserved` seam declares nothing and creates nothing; only `Create` asks it, a page's container never moves after. A REMARK IS ITS AUTHOR'S: an edit is refused to anybody else in the decide's own snapshot, and a removal — which had no check at all — takes the author or a caller the table admits as a moderator, passed as `CommentAuthority` because this package holds no chart and no grants. And `ContainerKey` is the one canonical form of a container key, because eleven copies had drifted and six of them did not trim |
| `iamdomain` | The company's own IDENTITY ESTATE — people, the addresses and logins they are known by, the credentials they hold, the invitations that enrolled them and the sessions they are signed in with — as `statelog`'s FOURTH domain and its third STRICT one. `iam` stays the VALUES LEAF; this is the durable half and it imports that leaf rather than restating it. What is particular to it is that ARBITRATION IS THE CONSTRAINT: the replicated estate forbids a UNIQUE index outside a primary key — a violation inside an apply transaction aborts it deterministically on EVERY node, which here is every login in the company — so there is no uniqueness check anywhere in the schema and nothing to add one to. What keeps two people off one address is ONE SUBJECT FOR THE DIRECTORY (`iam.directory`): every record that sets or frees an address, a login or a seat — an enrolment (an administrator's `Create` or an invitation's `Redeem`, two verbs over one `OpEnrol` record and one decide), an invitation's issue and its cancellation, an identity change (`SetIdentity`, the login and the seat as post-state), a removal — publishes there, decided in a snapshot of EVERY row, so a value somebody else holds is refused naming them (`ErrTaken`, an open invitation by its id) and the broker accepts the record only if no other directory record landed since. It was a subject PER CLAIM, so an enrolment was a sequence whose half-finished residue was a reservation every reader stepped around, a move was a claim and a release, and a duty reported duplicates nothing could refuse. THE DIRECTORY OWNS A ROW'S EXISTENCE AND ITS THREE UNIQUE COLUMNS — an enrolment creates the row whole, a removal deletes it, and a record on the person's own subject (`iam.person.<id>`) only ever UPDATES a row that exists — so the two subjects meet on one row and never on one column, and one `version` guards both. A DIRECTORY DECISION ASKS THE WHOLE DEFERRAL INDEX (`wholeDirectory`), because the framework's probe covers the buckets a record declares — the people it writes, never the people it compares against — so a node holding any record it cannot decode refuses `deferred` and another node decides; a removal frees values and does not ask. TWO ROWS HOLDING ONE VALUE — a retained record's residue, or a restore's — answer a lookup UNKNOWN rather than either row. A PERSON HOLDS ONE HUMAN SEAT FOR AS LONG AS THEY ARE HERE (ADR-0032, the package doc's anchor): a create names it (`Creation.Seat`, `ErrSeatRequired`), every invitation names it and its redemption binds it, an identity change MOVES it and never clears it — `seat: ""` on a person is `ErrSeatRequired`, decided in the record's own snapshot from the row's kind, before the no-op arm — and only moving or removing them frees it; a suspended or retired person keeps theirs, and a SERVICE ACCOUNT's seat stays optional. EVERY BINDING, of either kind, is checked as a HUMAN seat of the running org (`Writer.humanSeat`) — a create and an edit used to ask only that the chart held the handle, so a person bound to an AGENT's seat was refused `403 seat_unavailable` on every request they made — and a node running no company has none to offer (`ErrNoCompany`, from the seam's `session.ErrNoCompany`, rather than the `ErrUnavailable` no wait would clear). The WRITER's decides enforce it and the APPLIER never does: an applier writes whatever a record says, because refusing a record there stops the log on every node. AN ADMINISTRATOR'S CREATE IS DERIVED (`CreatedPersonID`, from its operation key) AND NEVER REWRITES: one reaching an existing person is a reused key (`ErrOperationReused`), and its own retry is answered from the ledger; a redemption's person is minted per attempt, and what keeps the link single-use is the link its record spends. A CREATED PERSON IS ACTIVE AT ONCE AND HANDED A FIRST PASSWORD LINK in the create's own record — a `reset` credential whose id and secret are DERIVED from the person under the company's key (`Blinder.PasswordLinkID`, `Blinder.PasswordLinkSecret`), a week long (`credential.EnrolmentLinkLifetime`), spent through the ordinary reset path — because the retry an unknown answer asks for is answered from the ledger without its decide running, so only a derivation hands it the link its first attempt issued: it reads the link back by that id (`firstLinkAs`) and answers it with its stored expiry while it opens, `LinkClosed` once it is spent, revoked or aged out, and unavailable on a node that has not applied it. A service account gets none — it proves itself with a token. And THE RECORD IS FORMED INSIDE THE DECIDE, the counters its credential is stamped with included (`stampIssued` over `countersOf`): formed before it, as every enrolment used to be, a carried credential was stamped at zero, and the first company-wide invalidation — the last step of every restore — ended every first link any create would issue from then on. The SCOPE IS FLAT AND BUCKETED (64, by FNV-1a over the id nothing renames, this domain's own and unrelated to `search`'s) and that is forced rather than chosen: a directory record's subject names nobody and the person it is about is inside a payload the deferring node could not decode, so a per-person path would be computable by every node except the one that needs it — and a person's id in a deferral row is personal data in the one place this estate most carefully keeps clear of it. THE ROOT SCOPE IS REFUSED to every kind but the FIVE that can never be deferred, because a deferral at the root freezes every suspension, revocation and login at once — three of the five install a gate, and the other two are the framework's own records every build reads, whose scope resolves to the sentinel that intersects nothing: the barrier, and the TRIMMED record (ADR-0050) that removes the arbitration anchors this log writes for every directory, person and session subject, which would otherwise keep an anchor for every sign-in's lineage on every data node for good. TWO COUNTERS END SESSIONS and they are deliberately not one word: a person's REVOCATION EPOCH ends every session THEY hold, and the fleet-wide SESSION GENERATION (`iam.invalidation`, one subject for the whole company) ends everybody's — which is what a restore needs, because a revocation performed after an artefact was taken is rolled back with it and the generation is the only number that can be pushed forward without knowing who was affected. It is also the FIRST strict domain whose `GatesSeatAdmission` is FALSE: no turn reads it, a seat's authority is the chart's, so taking a node's tracker or knowledge-base copy out of service because a human cannot sign in would be an outage caused by the wrong subsystem — what a stall gates is the REQUEST path, per request, with a 503 and never a 401. NAME AND EMAIL ARE SEALED BY THE WRITER before publication, under the FLEET KEYRING every node already holds — one envelope format and one rotation story rather than a second key hierarchy — which is what keeps the identity claim a byte comparison — and so is a SECOND FACTOR'S SEED, the one credential kept as a secret rather than a verifier, sealed by the enrolment and bound to the credential it was enrolled as (`Sealer.SealCredential`), because in the clear it was a second factor every node, snapshot and backup held; every value carries ASSOCIATED DATA naming whose it is and which of their values (`AADFor`, `AADForCredential`, `AADForInvitation`), because under one keyring a value pasted into another row would otherwise open there; a LOGIN is deliberately in the clear, because blinding a value the dashboard prints on every row costs an operator their own audit trail for nothing. The APPLIER never decrypts: every sealed value is written through as bytes and a removal erases by SHAPE without opening one, because an applier that opened a value would write rows that depended on which keyring keys its node holds — two nodes a rotation has not yet reached would write different rows from one record. A REMOVAL ERASES: its apply deletes the person's rows and CLEARS every sealed value of theirs that rows not their own still hold — an invitation addressed to them, the trail rows whose records carried their name, address or seed (`apply_erase.go`), found by the address the removal releases, the only one a person ever holds — with the record declaring that address's bucket beside the person's, as a seat bind declares its leavers' buckets, so a node that retained a record in one of them holds the removal behind it — recognising a sealed value by its shape, since every envelope here is somebody's, so afterwards no row on any node holds a value of theirs that opens; the log's copy waits for the trim and a backup for its own deletion. A ROTATION moves a person's values the only way anything here changes, a RECORD per person holding one under a key other than the active one (`Writer.Reseal`, counted by `Reader.SealedKeys`), because a node that re-sealed its own rows would hold different bytes from its peers and a replay from the floor would write the old ciphertext back; an outstanding invitation's address is counted and waited out rather than moved. The blind-key mint guard proves its "no blind here" only on rows that have APPLIED the whole log (`CoversLog`) — and APPLIED is not CONSUMED: the applier's checkpoint moves past a record it RETAINS (a newer build's, one signed under a keyring key the node was not restarted with), and a node holding one reads that record's rows as rows nobody wrote. It runs on EVERY NODE THAT HOLDS THE ESTATE, whatever its other roles and company or none, so every node that validates a session reads its own identity rows; a node WITHOUT `data` — a seats-only satellite, which routes deliveries by who holds each seat — applies none of it and asks a data node instead, through the ESTATE ROUTER's identity reads (a login's binding row for `iam.Holders`, `SeatHolders` for the party registry), floored on the identity log so a data node behind answers unknown rather than an old binding. THE ADMINISTRATIVE PERMISSION IS `admin`, checked at the RECORD rather than only at a route because a record can be published by a CLI, a duty, a migration and a test, none of which passes through one — once two grants, one asked of the record and another held by the NODE's own writer, so every invitation redemption was refused on a real deployment while the surface's own suite, built on a stub writer, stayed green; the node's writer now holds `[admin]` itself. AND NEVER THROUGH A MACHINE TOKEN: `mayAdminister` refuses every administrative record — a create, a redemption, an identity change, a status change, a removal, an invitation and its cancellation, an edit, ending everybody's sessions — whose `OperatorID` is of the `pat:` class with `ErrTokenRefused` (which wraps `ErrRefused`) BEFORE it reads or publishes, whatever the token carries, because a token MAY carry admin and deciding who may do anything needs a person at a keyboard; a Tier A token's `token:<id>` is no machine token and is admitted, and the eviction and sweep gestures (`mayOperate`) take admin with no such lock. A MACHINE TOKEN IS MINTED ON ITS OWNER'S SUBJECT, reading the owner in the snapshot the credential is formed in (`MintToken`), because what it may carry is a fact about the owner NOW — a subset of `Expand` of their permissions, admin included, defaulting to everything they hold, and nothing the minting party does not hold — and a caller handing in a finished credential would be stating a read from another transaction. A PERSON'S TOKEN IS MINTED BY THAT PERSON ALONE, decided in the same snapshot on the minting party's ID — the WRITER's own (`Writer.Principal`, derived with the rest of the party from the principal `Writer.As` is handed whole), never a field of the mint a caller fills in and never a login, which a rename moves between people — and never through a machine token, which would renew itself for ever with nobody present: whoever mints a token is shown its value and it acts as its owner, so an administrator minting on a person's account could hold a credential acting as anybody — admin now mints for SERVICE ACCOUNTS only, the one kind with nobody behind it to mint for itself; `Reader.MachineToken` answers the row, the owner, their epoch and the fleet generation in ONE snapshot for the guard. AN ENROLMENT AND AN INVITATION CONFER ONLY WHAT THEIR WRITER HOLDS, exactly as an edit does — an enrolment is a permission change from nothing, and it was held to nothing, so an administrator of people alone could create a colleague holding every permission — judged through `iam.Expand` (an unknown name already on a row is kept, one added is refused) — and the one enrolment the NODE's own writer performs, a REDEMPTION, NAMES its authority instead, checked in the enrolment record's own snapshot: the INVITATION (nothing outside `Expand` of what it carried, no address or seat it did not carry, and no link already spent or aged out). There is NO EXEMPTION FOR THE FIRST PERSON: a company with nobody in it still holds the Tier A token every serving node requires, and that token's invitation is held to its grants exactly as an administrator's is — and names a human seat exactly as theirs does, so a company's first person waits for a revision that declares one. AN ENROLMENT NEEDS TO BE FINDABLE rather than addressed: a PERSON needs an email (it is the interactive login key) and a MACHINE needs a login (it has no login page, so requiring an address would mean inventing one) — the flat address requirement meant a company could not declare a service account at all. A login is never cleared, only changed — a person's seat neither: an identity change stating none is refused, because a principal with no login is recorded as nobody and a Tier A token's machine row is how it is bound to a seat. And EVERY PRINCIPAL NEEDS A LOGIN, a person too although their address finds them: it is the name they sign in with, the name `iam.OwnerOf` resolves when a lead names a report, the `by <login>` in every trail reason, and what `Principal.Validate` requires — a person enrolled by address alone was written as `anonymous` beside every change and failed `Principal.Validate` on every request — so an invitation's view PROPOSES one from the address (`iam.LoginFromAddress`) for the person to keep or change, and an administrator's create that names none takes that same proposal at `/iam` and answers the login it took, never one typed by nobody and shown to nobody. THE BLINDER IS A SOURCE (`Blinds`), resolved at the write and never held from construction, because its key is minted by whichever node first needs it — held from boot it was nil on every fresh deployment and every address write was refused for a key nothing minted. The retention SWEEP'S PUBLISHER is here beside its applier (`sweeppublish.go`): the only clock read on the whole retention path, turned into POSITIONS once per tick, and a bucket is swept only when something is a day past its horizon, which bounds it at 64 records a day with no memory across a lease move. A sweep collects what lapsed AND what was spent — redeemed invitations and revoked or expired credentials, the last from the person's DOCUMENT as well as the rows, since the next credential change would republish them — and every record NAMES THE CREDENTIAL its party acted through (`operator_id`), a removal and an invalidation included, and none where the node's own writer acted. Every record is written at version 1, the field table being empty: a record above a peer's decode ceiling is deferred there, so the ceiling is what a build READS and never what it writes. Whether a record this node RETAINED is about somebody is asked of the WHOLE deferral index for their bucket in the rows' own snapshot (`deferredFor`), because the runner's earliest deferral carried no scope and was about one bucket, so the session table's and a token binding's "this node cannot vouch" answer never fired. AN INVITATION is a directory record held against every person and every open invitation to its ADDRESS AND ITS SEAT, since its own id would never contend and two administrators would each get a link that creates the same person, or two people on one seat; that id is its issue's operation key blinded under the company's key (`Blinder.InvitationID`), so a retry finds and answers its own invitation, the key on other terms is `ErrOperationReused`, and nobody holding a key can compute a link; the decide reads `iam_invites` as well as `iam_people`, because an address or a seat an open invitation holds is held — an administrator's create of it, a bind to it and another invitation naming it are refused naming the invitation (`heldByInvitation`). THE LINK IS THE ID AND A SECRET, derived from the id under the same key (`Blinder.InvitationSecret`) so a retry hands back the same link, of which the invitation keeps only the SHA-256 (`Invitation.Verifier`) and the redemption's record checks it again in its own snapshot (`Redemption.InvitationSecret`): the id alone was the credential, and it is the row's key in every snapshot, backup and access log — an invitation with no verifier admits nobody. AN INVITATION NAMES ITS SEAT, always (`InviteMint.Seat`, `ErrSeatRequired` — a human seat no person and no open invitation holds, recorded by its handle in `iam_invites.seat_id`, replicated migration 0042, which the apply writes and the applier's `statelog.Deriver` recomputes whenever a checkpoint's rule set differs — a reanchored cursor, or rows adopted from another build's rules), and the redemption binds it in the record that creates the person — refused there only by a PERSON holding it, so two open invitations a restore or a retained record left on one seat cannot deadlock each other — while a redemption that names no seat at all is its caller's fault (`ErrInvalid`), since every invitation holds one; and an invitation with no expiry is open nowhere, where three readers used to disagree. An invitation nobody redeemed is CANCELLED on the directory too (`CancelInvitation`, `OpCancel`), and its apply DELETES the row rather than marking it, because the row is what holds the address and the seat and an id the estate does not hold is exactly what a dead link must look like — with no tombstone, since the log applies the issue first and a redelivery below the checkpoint is never applied; a redeemed one is refused (`InvitationRedeemed`) because it created somebody. A NEW PASSWORD IS ONE RECORD (`SetPassword`, `OpPassword`): the person's document with the password replaced and every live RESET LINK revoked, and their revocation epoch moved, on their own subject — as two records a change that landed its password and not its epoch left every session a thief held working. The writer requires no grant for it: the surface that calls it is the authority (the person's current password, or a reset link) and states it as `PasswordSet.Check`, judged in the record's own snapshot. A RESET LINK is a `reset` credential an administrator puts on a person through an ordinary person write — the SHA-256 of its secret, a day long, the one before it revoked, and revoked by any edit that ADDS a permission to its person — over `iam.Expand` on both sides, so storing `[admin]` as `[admin, tracker, knowledge]` adds nothing — (`RevokeResetLinks`, in `UpdatePerson`'s own decide), because the link was judged against its issuer at the issue and a grant gained since was judged against nobody holding it — and `Reader.ResetByID` answers it with its person's login and stage in one read, and whether it sets their FIRST password (`ResetRow.First`), DERIVED from their holding no password credential rather than stored on the link, so a reset issued to somebody whose first link lapsed unspent reads as the first password it sets. EVERY WRITE ANSWERS ITS WHOLE `statelog.Result` and never a bare position, because a position cannot tell `applied` and `pending` from `unknown`: a zero one beside a nil error read as success, so a second factor whose spend may not have landed opened a session. THE STAGE HAS ONE WRITER and every reader takes it from the row's COLUMN: the status apply moves the column and the document together, and a read-modify-write decide forms the next document from the column, because the document's copy went stale on the first suspension — sessions kept validating, and the next grant edit re-published `active` and reinstated the person. The applier's post-commit hook says what a batch MOVED (`Moved`): whether a seat's standing moved (a bind or a move, a service account's unbind, a stage, a removal), and WHOSE CREDENTIALS — the people whose rows it moved with the LOGIN each holds (a Tier A token is bound through the row under its own `token:<id>`, whose id it never learns), the sessions it ended, and only where no list can say whose, everyone: the fleet generation, and a record the node RETAINED rather than applied, which the framework says through `statelog.RetentionHook` because a retained record never reaches Apply and its person is inside a payload the node cannot read. An identity change names its person with the login they held BEFORE it as well as the one they hold after, because a record naming nobody ended every socket on the node for an ordinary unbind — an open dashboard socket is decided at its handshake and ended by the record that ended its credential, and a move naming everyone closes them all; `SeatHolders` answers who holds each seat at what stage in one snapshot, a removal read off its tombstone, which speaks for the BINDING it released and no other: the first bind of the seat after the removal stamps `seat_rebound_by`, so a successor's later move off the seat (or a service account's unbind) hands the seat to the chart rather than back to the leaver — which is what lets a suspension withdraw a seat's contact identities with no configuration change; `SeatHolder` answers the same for ONE seat as two seeks, the binding by the seat's lookup index and the removal by `iam_removed.seat_id` (migration 0094) — a DERIVED column, the seat the removal's identifiers released, written by its apply, re-derived by `Rederive` under derivation version 2 and indexed with its stamp, which the bind's stamp and its writer's scope seek too — so a turn composing the person it acts for pays the same however many people the company has hired or removed. A SEAT IS BOUND BY ITS HANDLE, which is immutable (ADR-0013): `seat_id` — a person's, and an invitation's — a removal's tombstone and the stamp on it name it; every binding checks the handle against the running org the writer is handed (`WriterDeps.Seats`) — ADVISORY, since a revision applied elsewhere first is one this node has not seen, and the residue is the dangling binding `iam check` reports; the other end is advisory too, a `/config` write that takes a human seat away (a removal or a kind flip) refused `409 seat_held` naming whoever holds it at any stage short of removal, or the open invitation that holds it (by id and expiry, never its address), after one read of the directory at the node's clock (`Reader.SeatClaims`, through `configapi.Holders`), which an offline import never makes — and every reader finds the seat by that handle. `SeatClaims` is the ONE read of who and what holds the human seats — every binding with its holder's KIND, and every open invitation's seat, in one snapshot — and `ClaimsBySeat` folds it: two rows on one seat are unknown (`ErrUnavailable`), never a pick, while two open invitations on one seat, which only a build before invitations held their seats leaves, keep the newest |
| `iam/credential` | WHAT SOMEBODY PROVES THEMSELVES WITH: argon2id passwords, RFC 6238 second factors, recovery codes, machine tokens, one-time password reset links (`reset.go`, `ResetLinkLifetime`, a day long because one travels out of band to somebody locked out today) — and the same credential as the FIRST PASSWORD LINK an administrator's create hands the person it creates, which lives `EnrolmentLinkLifetime`, A WEEK, beside it: the invitation's lifetime, since both bring in somebody who does not work here yet and may not read the link before their first day, and ONE constant for both, here, where it was the invitation surface's own (`iamapi.InviteWindow`, gone) — and the throttle that keeps a sign-in endpoint from being a ROSTER. Everything stored is a VERIFIER and never a secret, because the identity estate is replicated, snapshotted, backed up and donated to joining peers. THE RULE IS NOT "hash secrets with argon2id", it is SPEND COST WHERE AN ATTACKER HAS A SHORTCUT — a password came from a space small enough to grind, so it gets 64 MiB and t=3; a recovery code, a machine token and a reset link's secret are values this engine minted — `crypto/rand`, or for a first password link a MAC under the company's key, so a create's retry hands back the same link — so there is no dictionary and SHA-256 is the whole of it, and a token is presented on every request a pipeline makes. `p=1` is the parameter most often set wrong: parallelism makes ONE verification finish sooner and does nothing to an attacker already running every core they own, while costing the ability to serve concurrent sign-ins at all. `VerifyCap` is DERIVED as max(1, NumCPU/p) because each verification holds 64 MiB — uncapped, an unauthenticated caller turns the endpoint into an out-of-memory kill — and it bounds EVERY derivation through one path, a verification at the stored verifier's own cost as much as a hash: `Verify` used to call argon2 directly, so the cap bounded setting a password and nothing a stranger could send.
