TWO READS SPAN A CHANGE'S RECORDS RATHER THAN ITS ROWS: `change_routing` (`routedread.go`) is whom one change's records reached — the other axis of the rows a person's code inbox reads, `code_notifications_change_idx` walked backward a WHOLE record a page, since every notice of one record shares its position — held to the grant as a `change` read is, and its `Delivery` (internal/routing's `DeliveryOf`) tells a change whose records reached NOBODY from one whose notices were SWEPT by dating its opening against `RetainedFrom`, the serving node's clock less `SeatNoticeHorizon`: the shorter of the two horizons, since a person's inbox is kept at least thirty days (`config.MinInboxRetentionDays`), so every notice of a record since then is still kept; and `reviews` is one reviewer's reviews newest first across repositories (`code_reviews_reviewer_idx`, replicated 0090), each naming its change (`ChangeName`), its repositories judged once a page. REVIEWS AND FINDINGS ARE THE CONVERSATION'S TOO (replicated 0073; S40 in `conversation.go`): a `review` (`Review`) — a verdict, a summary and up to `MaxFindingsPerReview` (64) findings in ONE record, its id and each finding's DERIVED from the operation id (`ReviewID`, `FindingID`) — by a principal at review or above that did not write the change (rules D21, D22), CARRYING its reviewer's own standing findings and SUPERSEDING every other one they raised on an earlier revision (a review of the same revision supersedes nothing), a reviewer keeping at most `MaxStandingFindings` (1 024) standing so what one record supersedes stays bounded (`too_many_findings`); a `finding_resolve` (`FindingResolve`) — resolved, won't fix or disputed by an author (`not_author`), dismissed by its raiser, a maintainer or a person (`not_raiser`), on a finding still standing (`finding_gone`) — so a BLOCKER is cleared by its dismissal or its raiser's later review alone, an author's answer leaving it standing for its raiser to judge. `code_reviews`, `code_findings` (NARROW, since every answer and every supersession rewrites a finding's state) and `code_finding_texts` (its title, body and suggestion, a row of its own); `code_conversations` counts reviews, open findings, standing blockers and `changes_requested` — the reviewers whose STANDING VERDICT, the latest of their reviews that approves or asks for changes, kept on their `code_participants` row so an apply reads it in one seek, asks for changes (a review that comments judges nothing); a write past `MaxConversationBytes` (a record less `MaxRoutingBytes` and 4 KiB) is refused, split into two reviews of the same revision; a purge cuts a heavy conversation by its comments, then its findings' texts, then its reviews. A REVISION'S FACTS ARE THE CONVERSATION'S TOO (replicated 0074; `revise.go`): a `revise` (`Revise`) — what the repository's HOME read of a change's head revision against its target's tip (the merge base, the trees of the base and the head, the `--verbatim` patch-id or that none was taken, the touched paths or that it touches all, and the touched Go files' CLASSES — `RevisionClasses`, each classed path named by its PLACE in the touched list, never by its name again, so the facts stay one page beside a 48 KiB list, and `Complete` only where every touched path was judged: past `MaxClassedBytes` of Go (16 MiB of both versions, a tenth of a read's ten seconds at `BenchmarkClassifyGo`'s worst 58 ms a MiB), past the read's budget and wherever the revision touches all they are incomplete, the safe side for a class that only relaxes) and the path owners whose globs those paths match, MATCHED ON THE HOME (`MatchOwners`, beside the `OwnersDigest` of the owners matched against, since no decide pays 64 owners' globs against a thousand paths on every round) — is the home's own write through the change writer, naming NO asker; it is decided only of the change's head revision and head against its current target and past the facts held of that revision against that branch, and only where the snapshot's owners digest the same, `superseded` otherwise (the home's own refusal, recorded nowhere, answered to nobody else); a head its target holds is no facts (a base equal to its head refused), since its change is merged and its home lands it; the decide resolves each matched owner — a unit to its effective lead, a seat to itself — and asks those who may review (`code_reviewers` reason `path_owner`, naming no asker), skips the rest by why (`SkipReason`: no lead, no seat, below review, an author, past `MaxReviewersPerChange`), leaves an ask the change holds as it is, and STAMPS whom else the facts concern — on a revision's first facts the reviewers whose standing verdict asks for changes on an earlier one (`ReReview`), on the change's first, where nobody who may review it is asked, its owner's lead (`LeadFallback`) — so the apply judges nothing again; `code_revision_facts` holds one row a revision, replaced by a retarget's or a later tip's facts, its touched paths NUL-packed and its wide columns counted in `text_bytes`, and a `change` read's revisions carry them. THE HOME'S WORK LIST IS THE ROWS (`changesdue.go`): `changes_due`, the engine's own read, names the ACTIVE repositories holding a LIVE change whose head revision holds no facts or no mergeability against its current target, that target a live branch, or whose mergeability its moved target asks again — walking `code_changes_open_idx` grouped on its leading column, each change checked against its facts' key, its mergeability's, its target's row and its repository's state — and, of one repository (`ChangesDueQuery`, REQUIRED, with the instant `RecheckBefore` a recheck is due by: its home's clock less its refresh), those changes by source branch with the target's tip and the position that moved it there and what each owes (`ChangeDue.FactsDue`, `MergeDue`), beside the repository's path owners read in the same snapshot, and on its first page the RECHECKS — changes whose mergeability of their head revision is behind the target's tip or `unknown`, recorded at or before that instant — the OLDEST MERGE FIRST and at most `ChangesDueQuery.Rechecks` (the listing's one sort, over a repository's live changes behind a moved target, which no index orders by when their merge was recorded without one written by two subjects' records; declared in plan_test's `sortingStatements`); a recorded revise takes its change's facts off and a mergeability its merge, a push of its head or a retarget puts both back. A CHANGE'S MERGEABILITY (`mergeability.go`, op `mergeability`, one of `ConversationOps`, base format under the domain's stated condition) is the home's own write on the change's subject, through the change writer like a revise, naming no asker and judged by no chart: whether the head revision merges into the target's tip — `clean`, `conflict` naming the first `MaxConflictPaths` (100) paths within `MaxConflictBytes` (16 KiB of JSON), each one a record carries (`ConflictPathCarried`: UTF-8 naming a tree entry) and the rest counted (`ConflictsHeld`, the one list the home's merge and a preview hold), or `unknown`, a merge past its deadline — decided of the change's head revision against its current target (`superseded` otherwise) and MONOTONE in the target's tip: an earlier tip than the row holds is `superseded`, and so is the same tip once the row is decided, since a merge of two commits is decided once; only an `unknown` row's tip is taken again, its instant moving so its next try waits the refresh. `code_mergeability` (replicated 0077, CREATED AGAIN by 0078 so the readiness it stores sits before its wide conflicted paths, which the due listing reads past on every pass) holds one row a change, its conflicted paths NUL-packed and weighed in `text_bytes`, its broker instant `at` what the rechecks are paced by; a `change` read carries it (`ChangeDetail.Mergeability`), and a purge removes it with the change. MERGE READINESS (`readiness.go`, M4's subset, M5's `JudgeGate` wrapping it) is ONE PURE JUDGMENT, `ReadinessOf(ReadinessRows, Roster) Readiness`, of the rows ONE snapshot holds (`readinessRowsIn`: a page of changes read a kind of row at a time, each statement driven from the list of their ids) against ONE chart: a `Precondition` a `PreconditionKind` — `draft`, `facts`, `approval` (ONE COUNTED APPROVAL: an eligible reviewer's STANDING verdict, `code_participants.verdict`, on the head revision or an earlier one the head carries unchanged, by its CODE (the two revisions' recorded base and head trees equal: `no_code_change`, a revision carry-forward kind compared from the rows in the judging snapshot, which carries past an omitted patch id and across a revision of other code between the two) or by its DIFF (its recorded `patch_id` the head's, neither empty nor omitted — X02), the detail naming which; computing while the head's facts are unrecorded, never carried from a revision whose facts never were; NEVER AN AUTHOR's, a person always eligible and a seat by `GrantOf` at review; a later request for changes withdraws only that reviewer's approval), `blocker` (each standing blocker, the oldest `MaxReadinessBlockers` (16) by `code_findings_blockers_idx`, partial on the standing blockers, named with WHO CLEARS IT, `ClearedBy{Raiser, Maintainers, Persons}` — the maintainer the ONE seat relation's first row relates by maintain where its tier allows), `mergeable` (the head revision's recorded merge, clean "as of" its tip) — each met, unmet or computing, an unmet one naming whom it `WaitsOn`; `First` the first unmet precondition's sentence, else the first computing one's; `WaitsPerson` its first unmet precondition waiting on persons alone. It counts no tracker decision (`TestTheGateReadsNoDecision`). THE MERGE GATE WIDENS IT (gatejudge.go): `JudgeGate(GateFacts, GateRules) Readiness`, pure, the ONE gate function every reader and decide of a merge calls (ADR-0008) — `GateRulesOf` combines every rule matching the target, the strictest of each term (`merge_by` with `code.merge_by_floor` on a protected branch, `approvals` one where any gate asks one and `approvals_unless` only by a class every such gate names, every gate's human paths and checks — a gate requiring checks holding `.crewlet/**` (`code.ChecksDirGlob`) as a human path unless it sets `agents_edit_checks`, added in the combination and never stored (U44) — `owners_approve` where any sets it, the smallest queue depth and batch of the gates requiring checks); M4's `approval` is a precondition only where the gate asks one, and the gate adds `human_approval` (a PERSON'S counted approval where an unwaived human path holds the head revision), `owners_approval` (ONE counted approval from somebody on EVERY touched owner's lead chain, or a person, over the head revision's RECORDED match — no glob is matched in a judgment — `computing` while the snapshot's owner rows digest otherwise), `check` (every required check passed or skipped on the head commit CHECKED FOR THE CHANGE'S OWN TARGET by its latest attempt — `gateFactsIn` reads the attempts by (repository, commit, target, check), never another branch's — wherever the gate requires checks) and `merge_authority` (judged for `GateFacts.Merger`, the reader or a decide's principal; none on a stored readiness), every approval counted by M4's one rule (`approvalTally`); `gateFactsIn` reads a page's facts a kind of row at a time and only what each target's gate reaches, and `LoadGateFacts` one change's for a decide. A QUEUE TARGET (a gate requiring a check, `GateRules.QueueTarget`) lands its merges through its MERGE QUEUE (queue.go, replicated 0082: `code_queue` an entry an enqueue, `code_candidates`, `code_landings`, `code_green`): an `enqueue` (`Enqueue`, its entry id `QueueEntryID` of its operation id) and a `dequeue` (`Dequeue`), records on the REPOSITORY's subject decided by its committer alone (`Committer.ProposeQueue`, `QueueProposal`, `QueueResult`, `MergeRefusal`; `queued.queueWrite`, busy kind `merge_queue`, `crewlet.code.queue.proposals{op,outcome}`) — an enqueue BY THE GATE, its principal the merger, every precondition but `mergeable`, which the candidate proves (`gate_unmet` naming each unmet one), beside a live, ready change at the head and target its asker read (`stale`, `head_moved`), not queued (`already_queued`), behind fewer than `MaxQueueEntries` (1 024) waiting (`queue_full`); a dequeue by its enqueuer, the change's owner, a maintainer or a person. A change's close, draft, retarget and landing end its live entry in their own applies (`leavesQueue`, dequeued with the reason), and a push that moves a queued change EJECTS it `head_moved` and dequeues a merged or closed one, counted in the change book (`changeBook.queued`, `queuedMoved`); the readiness's `queue` precondition is COMPUTING with the entry's place while a queue holds the change. Reads `queue` (live entries in queue order with their places, a named repository's trains and, on its first page, the entries its queues ejected in the last `QueueEjectionsShown` — a day, at most `MaxEjectionsShown` — with their reasons, checks and paths, from `code_queue_ended_idx`), `queue_due` (the home's: active repositories holding one, or one repository's queue work a target a page, `QueueDue`), `landings` (a target's above its green) and `green`. The QUEUE'S OWN RECORDS (queuerecords.go, decide_queue.go, apply_queuerecords.go), each asked by the repository's home naming no asker and decided by the committer alone through the same `QueueProposal`: a `candidate` (`Candidate`) records a TRAIN — up to `MaxQueueDepth` candidates, `MaxCandidateEntries` (64) entries, ids derived from its operation id (`QueueTrainID`, `QueueCandidateID`), its commits ONE pack of kind `candidate`, never a ref — abandoning the target's earlier train; an `eject` (`Eject`, `EjectReasons`) puts one target's live entries out naming the failed checks or the conflicted paths, abandons their train and is announced `code_queue_ejected` once by the landing node — as is the entry a PUSH ejects `head_moved` by moving a queued change's head, in the push's own record, which its committer announces once that record applied (`batchMoves`, `changeBook.queueEnds`, the one model the decide names it by and the apply ends it by); a `land` (`Land`, `QueueLandID`) lands up to `MaxLandEntries` (64) — a train's tested PREFIX whose tip passed every required check (`not_green`) — its decide PROVING EVERY ENTRY'S GATE AGAIN with its ENQUEUER as the merger (`QueueResult.Refused` names each it would not land), counting the live changes its pushes move as their apply writes them (`record_full`), its scope each entry's change while 14 fit `MaxScopePaths` and the domain past it; its apply merges each change FIRST (`merged_via` queue, by its enqueuer), lands ONE PUSH ROW `via queue` AN ENTRY (`LandPushID`, naming the enqueuer — the queue never acts on its own account) through `landPushes`, and writes the landing rows in chain order, each green, moving the target's green row to its tip; a `green` (`Green`) moves a target's green row onto a landing record whose required checks passed — at the landing's own position — and a `broken` (`Broken`) marks a landing above it broken, the green row only ever advancing on green; a dequeue of a tested entry abandons its train too. A person's push onto a protected branch carries the LANDINGS its decide stamped (`PushEntry.Landings`, `pushGates`), written as landing rows of kind `push` by its apply (`writePushLandings`) — two rows each, its landing and the branch's green row, which the decide counts (`entryRows`) and the batcher's `rowBound` does not, since only the snapshot's rules say which branches a rule protects; a push whose landings take even a record of its own past `MaxRecordRows` is refused `too_many_changes`, naming them. A protected target's TIP WORK is read from the rows too (`tipwork.go`, ADR-0068): a `checks_due` read's first page answers each target holding a landing neither green nor broken (`code_landings_unjudged_idx`, partial on exactly those, so the home's backstop never walks the green landings a target keeps) with its green, its required checks and its window of landings above the green (`MaxTipLandings` 256, JUDGMENT: eight bisect rounds), each with the latest attempt of each required check on its commit FOR THE TARGET, whatever its purpose — a check is a measurement of a commit; a `broken` record's apply is announced once as `code_target_broken` by the node whose committer landed it. The queue's history — ended entries and landings below a green row — is kept `QueueHistory` (30 d): each candidate, eject and land removes up to `queueHorizonRows` (128) past it, and `Retention.Pass`'s queue step (`Store.QueueSweep`, a `queue_sweep` on the repository's subject, listed past `QueueSweepGrace` by a walk of the repositories) sweeps an idle repository's. A change MERGES BY ONE RECORD ITS GATE DECIDES (merge.go, apply_merge.go, ADR-0066, replicated 0086: `code_changes.merged_by`, `merged_by_kind`, `merged_operator_id`, `override_reason`): a `merge` (`Merge`, its push id `WritePushID` of its operation id) built on the home onto the tip it read of the target, decided by the repository's committer alone (`Committer.ProposeMerge`, `MergeProposal`, `MergeResult`; `queued.merge`, busy kind `merge`, `crewlet.code.merge.outcomes{outcome}`) from ONE snapshot — the change open at the head it took (`stale`, `head_moved`), the target AT THE BASE IT WAS BUILT ONTO read off the snapshot's refs and never the proposal (`target_moved`), its gate met by the record's principal but for `mergeable`, which the build proved (`gate_unmet`), or a PERSON'S `override` (`not_person` for anybody else's), a queue target merging only through its queue but for that override (`queue_target`), the source's deletion held to a push's (`source_refused`: protections, and the merger's grant outside the seats' namespace) and its pack to a push's rules (`upload_expired`, `repo_size`, `compaction_behind`) — and applied as ONE record (`Applier.applyMerge`): the change MERGED FIRST (`merged_via direct`, who merged it, the override), so the push it then lands through the ONE push apply (`Applier.landPushes`, `via merge`, its pack `kind merge`, the horizons given what the merge's own rows leave) finds it merged rather than merging or closing it again; its live entry dequeued `merged`; the protected target's landing row (`writeLandings`, `LandingKind` `direct`) and, where its gate requires no check, its green row (`advanceGreen`) — the two helpers the queue's land reuses — the decide stamping `Protected` and `Green` so the apply matches no pattern; and `change_merged` announced once by the node that landed it (`CommitterOptions.Events`), never for a collapsed retry. WHAT A SEAT'S DAY COUNTS of the code host is ANNOUNCED the same way (`announce.go`): `change_opened` by the committer whose record opened a change — a change tool's open, or a push's options from the batch's own row, never a push the batch found landed before it — and `review_submitted` by the change writer whose record landed a review, each after its answer is handed back (the writer's change's next write waits on none of it), never for a collapsed retry, naming every seat it counts on by the agent id `CommitterOptions.AgentIDs`/`ChangeWriterOptions.AgentIDs` resolve off the live epoch — REQUIRED beside an event stream, since an announcement naming no seat is counted on no seat's day; the usage domain counts them. No push proposal names `via merge` (`proposedVias`), and a seat's push never moves a protected branch. Every read of changes JUDGES IT AGAIN in its own transaction — the `change` read and each row of a `changes` listing (`ChangeSummary.Readiness`), so a read of changes takes the reader's chart whoever asks and a chartless reader refuses it `ErrNotServing` — and every mergeability record's decide judges it over its own snapshot with the record's merge (`ReadinessRows.With`), the writer STAMPING `Ready`, `WaitsPerson` and the versions it judged at (`code_changes.version`, `code_conversations.readiness_version`, which an ask, a review, an answer to findings and a revise move — `movesReadiness` — and a mergeability never, or its own refresh would be due for ever), so `JudgesChart` holds a mergeability too. A REFRESH (`Mergeability.Refresh`) records the readiness again over the merge the row holds — the writer stamping that merge from the row — when `changes_due`'s `refreshDue` finds either version moved past the stored answer, or an attempt at the head for its target queued or ended past the stored `checks_position` (THE CHECK TERM, replicated 0089: `Mergeability.ChecksPosition`, writer-stamped, the latest record that queued or ended an attempt at the head for its target, and the guard's third input; the record's scope names `ChecksPath`) (`ChangeDue.RefreshDue`); it rewrites the answer, its versions and its checks position alone, keeping the merge's instant, and is `superseded` where the row holds no merge of the head revision or nothing it reads moved. `needs=person` walks `code_mergeability_waits_idx`, partial on the stored `waits_person`, and keeps the rows whose readiness the read judges still waits on one. WHOM A CHANGE RECORD CONCERNS (`concerns.go`, `pushconcerns.go`) is a SNAPSHOT ON THE RECORD, `ChangeRouting`: who acted (every record's envelope names the deciding node, so the routing names the payload's principal, a push entry's pusher, the enqueuer of an entry a queue landed, the pusher of the head a check measured, nobody for the home's records) and the handles each `code.Reason` reaches — fifteen, a closed set IN PRECEDENCE ORDER (`Reasons()`, the offer order itself, the gate's `checks_failed`, `target_broken` and `dequeued` among them; `Addressed()` the nine asks and `re_review`; `DefaultPrimaryReasons` another list) — each role filled only for the reasons its op routes by and bounded (`MaxMentions` 32, `MaxRoutedParticipants` 64, the record's `MaxRoutingBytes`), stamped by ONE function, `routingFor`, from the committer's decides (lifecycle, a push's merges and opens, `PushEntry.Routing`, held to the record's bytes by `routingRoom`: carried past them, kept as far as fits by an entry leading its record; and the gate's records — a merge, a dequeue, a broken landing, and an ejection's and a landing's one routing a change, kept within the record's room by `keptRoutings`, each charged `routingNoticeRows`), the change writer's (every op) and the checks writer's (a failed ending of a live change's current head: its authors and the head's pusher); `Concerns`/`Candidates` are pure over it through `internal/routing`'s add-once precedence across a record's routings, and nobody hears of a change they made but under `checks_failed`, the one reason news to its actor (`Reason.WakesActor`, `Prompt.WakesActor` for a check's ending) — a failing ending of a change's CURRENT head, the engine's run or a person's CI's report onto the same rows (whose row names no change, so it reaches the live change into its target at that commit), routed by ONE function keyed on the change and its head rather than the row's queue purpose (`routeFailedHead`, `headChangeIn`), its actor the head's pusher or the reporting person, and its owner's day counting it where it is told. A mention is held to the deciding chart (a write naming one `JudgesChart`, a push asking for a change `judgedByChart`) and a comment's or a review's mentions join the participants. `ready_to_merge` is announced ONLY by a mergeability record whose stored `ready` goes false→true — to the authors its gate lets merge (`mergingAuthors`, `PreconditionMergeAuthority` judged for each), else the owning unit's effective lead, and never by a check record (a passed head check reaches it through the home's readiness refresh) — and `conflicted` only by the one that turns the merge into a conflict: readiness reads two subjects, and a record on either judging it would be write skew. A push that moves no change concerns nobody. WHOM A RECORD REACHED is a row per recipient (`apply_notices.go`, `noticesweep.go`, `code_notifications`, replicated 0081, DIVERGENT): the applier writes every concern that hears, once a (record, recipient), after the record's own rows and only where it wrote some, a person's within the epoch's `inbox.retention_days` (`inbox.Horizon`) and a seat's within `SeatNoticeHorizon` (`store.EventHistory`, 30 days) — none past its horizon against the BATCH's instant, so a replay rebuilds no notice the sweep would delete — and every decide counts them with its record's rows (`heardCount`, `noticeRows`, a push's in `routingRoom`); `NoticeJobs` sweeps each kind per node (`maintenance.NodeLocal`); a purge part removes the notices of the changes it takes whole and the final purge the rest, UNCOUNTED, since a part's count must agree on every node and this table does not; `NewApplier(moved)` drains the batch's notes into movements, source `code`, after each commit. A MERGE PREVIEW's question and answer (`MergePreviewQuery`, `MergePreviewAnswer`) are declared here and answered by a git node (`code/home`): two whole commit ids, the asker and the seat it reads for, the asker's floor. A LANDING (`ChangeLanded`, op `change_landed`, one of `ChangeOps`) is the home's own write of a change whose target came to hold its head by a push past it, which no push's apply can see: through the committer, naming no asker and no version, judged by no chart (`ChangeProposal.judgesChart`, so a node with none decides it), decided against the change's head revision and head, its target and that target's tip as the home read them (`stale` otherwise), a merged change answered unchanged and a closed one refused, applied as `merged` by `push` at that tip. A `change` read pages its reviews in the order given (`code_reviews_change_idx`) and its findings by id, each with its text (`code_finding_texts`' key, which leads with the change); `needs=fix` walks an owner's changes in ONE live state (`code_changes_owner_idx`, open unless it names draft), keeping those whose counters ask for a fix — a request for changes standing, or a finding no author answered — and `needs=me` is `needs=review` and then `needs=fix` for the asker, a change of both listed once. ITS LIFECYCLE IS THE COMMITTER'S (`Committer.ProposeChange`, the estate's `code.change`): an open, an edit, a draft's state, a retarget, a close and a reopen are each a `ChangeProposal` decided ALONE in the repository's one queue, never batched with its pushes, as a record of its own op (`ChangeOps`, base format under the domain's stated condition), under the asker's operation id — an open's change id DERIVED from it (`OpenChangeID`), so an open asked again is the same change, never a second — at the asker's `Seen`, from one snapshot: an open by whoever may push its source branch (`Grant.RefRule` of a fast-forward), from a live branch to another, their heads differing, the source heading no live change (`change_exists` naming it: ONE LIVE CHANGE A BRANCH, the invariant a push's revisions rest on, which a reopen holds too); every other write by the change's owner, a principal that maintains the repository, or a person, at the version it names (`stale` otherwise); a write asking for what the change already says answered UNCHANGED from its row with no record; every answer read from the change's row (`ChangeResult`: its number the apply's, its version only where its row's last write is this record); a seat's write judged from the deciding epoch's chart, so a node with none, or behind its configuration, decides none (and the estate declares the operation `judgesGrant` for a seat); `crewlet.code.change.proposals` by op and outcome. ITS REVISIONS ARE ITS PUSHES' (`revisions.go`): the push record's apply moves every live change its ok branch commands reach — a source moved writes revision n + 1 and makes its pusher an author, a target set to exactly a change's head merges it by `push` (at most `MaxPushMerges`, 64, a record, lowest numbers first), a source deleted closes it (`SourceDeleted`), each entry in the record's order with its head moves before its merges before its closes, every change ONE version a record — pure over the record and the rows it reads in three list-driven statements, the changes and the authors its pushers already are of them (`changeBook`), and WRITTEN A STATEMENT A CHUNK through `store.InsertRows` like the refs beside them, never a statement a change — a record moves up to some 1 300, so an UPDATE each held the lane past `statelog.ApplyTxTimeBudget` — a moved change an upsert by its key whose VALUES are its whole row and which SETS ONLY WHAT THE RECORD MOVED of it (`pushWrite`: a head move its head and revision, rewriting `code_changes_target_idx` alone, a merge and a close their state; the driver rewrites a row's entry in every index whose key or predicate holds a column a write names, whatever the value, so a whole-row write kept up to seven of the table's eight secondary indexes on every write, and every lifecycle write too sets only what its op moves — `changeWrite`, `lifecycleWrite`, each held to the indexes its program rewrites by `TestAChangeWriteRewritesOnlyTheIndexesItMoves`); the push decide moves the SAME model by each entry it admits — TRIED ON THE BOOK ITSELF, journaled, and taken back where the record cannot hold it (`bookTrial`; a copy of the book an entry cost every change the batch reached, on every entry and every round), so an entry costs what its own commands reach — counting exactly the rows the apply will write about changes beside the entries' own (`entryRows`), so a record never passes `MaxRecordRows` for them: a push whose changes cannot fit even a record of its own is refused `too_many_changes` (an answer about a push, never recorded), and one that fits only alone is CARRIED, with every push behind it — decided by no round of its batch, it leads the committer's next batch, never answered busy for rows only a snapshot can count; the record's format does not move (the condition and the row it would take at `revisions.go`'s head). A PUSH OPENS ONE IN ITS OWN RECORD (`pushchange.go`): `git push -o change` — the `change` namespace (`ParseChangeOptions`), every other option a forge's and read by nobody — asks for a change on the push's ONE branch (`ChangeSource`), and the push decide opens it on the state the entry leaves (the source moved and heading no live change, a live target other than it at another commit: an open's rules), STAMPING it on the entry with its target resolved (`PushEntry.Change`, base format under the stated condition, the `versionedFields` row it would take named there), which the apply opens after the entry's own moves (`changeBook.open`: numbered from `next_change`, the push its first revision, its pusher its owner, and the work item the push's run names by its identity — a coding box's, `RunProvenance.WorkID`, a field of the record and of no row — its item) — and otherwise NAMES the live change the source heads, which the push revised, or answers why it opened none (`ChangeAnswer`, `PushChangeRefusals`), NEVER a refusal of the push; an applied answer reads the change it opened by the id the push derives (`PushChangeID`), the one fact the rows hold whichever round decided the record, and a pending push's outcome reads it beside the push's row (`OutcomeQuery.Change`). Its state is a `ChangeState` (draft, open, closed, merged; `Live` the first two) and a merged one names how in a `MergedVia` (`push`). The two indexes partial on the live states are matched by the pinned planner only on their predicate AS WRITTEN — the states in their order (`liveChanges`): reversed they sought `code_changes_state_idx`, and `state = 'open'` neither — so every statement over them repeats that one constant. A change's BODY is A ROW OF ITS OWN (`code_change_bodies`, replicated 0066), written only where it has one: up to `MaxChangeBodyBytes` (32 KiB), the one value of a change that outgrows a page, and a row is read whole, overflow pages and all, so on `code_changes` it more than doubled the read and the rewrite every head move makes of the row (74 → 166 µs and 108 → 226 µs, measured at the review that moved it) — and the change's `body_bytes` weighs it, so nothing that walks the changes reads a body to learn its width. The bodies, revisions and authors carry NO repo_id, reached by their change's key, so a purge part takes a repository's changes WHOLE in number order, each one's dependents before its row — set-based over the changes that fit (one walk of `code_changes_number_idx` counting each change's dependents to the part's bound, four deletes over their ids) and cutting only the change it stops at for its rows, oldest revision first, its body and row left for a later part: a thousand one-revision changes in one part cost 62 ms where a change at a time cost 850 ms, under the race detector — and the bounded count and the final purge reach the dependents through the same index. A part weighs the bodies in ONE BYTE BUDGET WITH ITS PUSH ROWS, `PurgePartBytes`: after the pushes it takes whole changes only while the part weighs under it (at most one change's body past it, and a walk the bytes stopped cuts nothing but a conversation's comments), and the bounded count sums the bodies' `body_bytes` with the pushes' `row_bytes`, so the final purge forms only once both fit — weighed by their rows alone, bodies took a part some 32 MB past the bound on top of push rows just short of it, 112 ms against 64 ms without bodies (the review's run); weighed, the most a part takes of push rows and bodies, both bounds at once, held the lane 53 ms, 750 bodied changes alone 50 ms and the final purge 43 ms (`BenchmarkApplyAPurgePart`, three iterations of one count on two E-cores, where a part of narrow rows took 44 ms). A SNAPSHOT SERVES RETIREMENTS in a `retired` phase after its refs and pushes, bounded by its own position (a retirement after it is the next delta's): a delta every one above its floor, and a full answer those above the caller's `RetiredSince` only when it can serve every one (a position on its generation, at or past the mark, and at most `MaxFullRetirements` of them — ONE PAGE, what a cache holds for a round against the commit-graph every git node drops and rewrites from the whole history when a full answer serves none, and never the full answer's own rows, which say nothing of that cost and which compaction keeps few, so a bound of them served nothing after any compaction that retired more packs than a repository of few refs has rows), its `RetiredFrom` zero otherwise — so a git node reading a repository whole after a restart can still tell a pack file a compaction retired from one no row accounts for — and page 1 weighs the two answers LIKE WITH LIKE, each with the retirements it would serve: asked from the position the caller holds, a full answer standing in for its delta would serve the very retirements the delta carries, which cancel, and only those the full answer cannot serve weigh against the delta alone, since counted on its side alone they replaced a delta by a larger full answer that sweeps the cache's directory where the delta was applied in place. EVERY READ IS HELD TO ONE FRESHNESS RULE (`checkFreshness`), a point read's and a listing's alike: any level, a principal's read naming none read stale, `session` only beside a position on the code log — the floor a screen reads its own write back at, so the list it just changed never comes from a copy behind the change — and refused bare, since it would wait for the zero position and label this node's prefix `session`; a floor on another log is the request's own mistake (`statelog.ErrForeignPosition`). A REPOSITORY'S PUSHES ARE READ IN LOG ORDER: `code_pushes_repo_idx` is (repo_id, position, push_id, at, row_bytes) (replicated 0056, REORDERED rather than joined by a second index, since every reader of its first shape reads it), because a record's instant is the broker's and steps back across a stream leader's change — the history newest first by position (read IN BRIEF for a screen that polls it, `ReadQuery.Brief`: a push of more than `BriefCommands` commands cut to its refused ones first and then the first it sent, the rest counted by outcome in `PushRow.Omitted`, its introduced ids counted and not listed, each row charged the row it READ so a brief page reads what a whole one does and sends a fraction; A REVISION READ BY OBJECT ID IS DECIDED HERE, ONCE (`revisionread.go`), pure over a `change` read's rows, for every surface that shows a change's code — the code tools and the API's change question — so a seat and a person are never shown two diffs of one revision nor one finding on two lines: `RevisionRow.FilesGap` says why a revision's files cannot be read from its facts (`pending` on a live change, its home measuring it within a minute; `unrecorded` on one that ended first; `unrelated`, no base, where its head shares no history with its target), `RevisionDiff` is the diff from the base its facts record — never the target's tip now — or the interdiff of two revisions, `ObjectFloor` the floor of the records that moved each revision's head and its target, never a row read's checkpoint, and `PlaceFindings` places a page of findings without a read where the rows say where they are and in ONE re-anchoring of the revision otherwise, folded in by `PlaceAnchors`, which refuses an answer that is not the anchors asked; and `RefsFloor` is the same floor for revisions named by refs, the record that last moved each ref, which a code tool's read and a question's read by object id ask at alike. A COMMIT'S CHANGES are the `commit` read (`commitread.go`): the repository in detail and, in its snapshot, each change whose merge the commit is (`code_changes_merge_idx`) and each revision it heads (`code_revisions_head_idx`, kept to the repository by its change's key), at most `MaxCommitChanges` (50) of each with `More` past them — a commit's page, by its WHOLE id, the commit itself and the push behind it being its git node's to answer. A read by object id may be asked UNSTAMPED (`GitQuery.Unstamped`, an estate request's field, refused on a kind that answers no page: `GitReadKind.Unstampable`): the page alone, cut against a room no stamp moves — what a question answers immutable. or narrowed to ONE push, `ReadQuery.Push`, read whole and only from the history of the repository the asker was judged on), the `introduced` read (the engine's own: no asker, its level named, ascending over (After, Through] or descending below Before), a coding run's pushes (`run_pushes`, the engine's own as `introduced` is, read when it collects the run: the rows naming the run's launch among the pushes of the repositories it was given, above its launch position, each row the seek reaches looked up to test its run, and each page held to what it READS rather than to what it answers — at most `MaxSnapshotItems` of those pushes and `MaxPageBytes` of what their rows weigh, weighed off the index's `row_bytes` before any row is looked up, so a page of none of the run's ends at a cursor and a long run or a wide push beside it costs more pages, never a page more — NO INDEX OF ITS OWN, which would cost every box push an entry on every data node for the history's 181 days to serve one read a run; `BenchmarkRunPushes` prices the scan), the live change each of a collected run's branches heads (`branch_changes`, the engine's own too: one repository by id and up to `MaxRefsPerQuery` whole branches, answered in their order through the statement a push's decide reads its branches' changes by, `liveBySourceSQL`, one seek of `code_changes_open_idx` a branch) and a delta snapshot's `pushes` phase (every push in (Since, its position], each once across its pages), from which a git node's index of which push introduced each commit is built — those two sending each push CONDENSED to the refs it landed and holding a page to the commands it READ as well as to the rows it sends (`scanIntroduced`), because a push row stores every command its client sent, the refused ones too, and counted by what it sent, a page of pushes that each landed one ref beside hundreds refused decoded gigabytes of commands to send four mebibytes; a seat's `refs` read lists a repository's live refs under a prefix over the live refs' partial index, never the key, its first page carrying the detail, and `ResolveRef` names EVERY ref a call needs in one transaction at one checkpoint — up to `MaxRefsPerQuery`, each a branch before a tag and answered on its own, a name no live ref holds answered missing beside the rest (`RefAnswer.Err` is the refusal of a caller that needs them all) — held to the asker's grant, each with where it last MOVED (`ResolvedRef.Moved`, its row's own position) beside the checkpoint they were read at: what a read of the commit it names is floored at, since a linearizable read's checkpoint is past its own barrier and every copy current to the repository's pushes sits below it. A CHANGE IS READ AS ITS REPOSITORY'S ROWS ARE (`changeread.go`): a listing (`ReadChanges`) names a repository, a work item (`<backend>:<id>`) or a state — refused naming none, since no index holds every change — and walks the ONE index its filters lead (`walkOf`: a work item's changes, an owner's in a state, a repository's in a state on `code_changes_repo_state_idx`, a repository's by number, the company's in a state), newest first, testing every filter that index does not seek on the rows the page read, so a page is the rows it READ and a cursor the last of them (`code_changes_work_idx` recreated ordered by the opening record's position and id, replicated 0069, since 0058's could only sort a recurring item's every change) — and EVERY ROW A READ OF CHANGES SERVES NAMES THE CURSOR THAT RESUMES AFTER IT (`ReadQuery.ChangesCursor`, spelled by the walk the listing's filters choose and, for needs=me, the half the page came from, through the one `halfOfMe`; `RevisionRow`, `ReviewerRow`, `CommentRow`, `ReviewRow` and `FindingRow`'s `Cursor`, which the reader sets each page's own cursor by; and a `checks` listing's `ReadQuery.ChecksCursor`, spelled by the one walk its question takes, `checksWalk`, which the reader pages by), so a reader whose answer is smaller than the estate's reply — a code tool's, one tool answer — keeps a page's first rows and resumes after the last it kept, never re-deriving a walk's cursor; one change (`ReadChange`) is read by its number (web!42), its row, description and authors on a first page beside its revisions newest first; both HELD TO THE GRANT on the change's repository — a listing naming a repository and a read of one refused whole where the asker or its ceiling may not read it (a number in it never answered `ErrChangeNotFound`), a listing naming none leaving out every repository either may not read and a deleted one's changes. EVERY READ OF THE ROWS SEEKS A NAMED INDEX, held by a gate rather than a review: `TestEveryCodeReadSeeksANamedIndex` walks the package's non-test source (typed through `internal/sourcetree/decltype`) for every text that reads a `code_*` table — a constant, an inline literal, a statement built of constants, and the WHERE an UPDATE or a DELETE finds its rows by — and holds each to a statement `plan_test.go` registers by its exact text, whose plan must reach every table of the replicated estate by a `SEARCH` on an index the schema declares or the primary key (the only scans `json_each`, a statement's own list, a subquery's result, and a table `scanOK` bounds: `code_evictions`, the trim's listing, one row a node an operator evicted); a function that assembles a statement at run time registers its shapes (`planShapes`, objstore's builders over `PackReferences`, `RetiredPackReferences`, `LayerReferences` and `CheckLogReferences` among them, each at every width its callers build it at, from their own constants — the collector's batch of `collect.JudgeBatch` keys, a walk's page and a backup's batch of `objstore.WalkPage`, a backup's page of deferred records of `statelog.ReprocessPage`, and the one key a short last batch narrows a question to — since each width is a statement the driver plans on its own), and an index whose reader is a later commit's is held to the read its migration claims (`claimedReads`), judged as that statement will be, so a new read lands with its plan or fails the build — all but the state log's own statements over the domain's ledger and deferred records (`code_ops`, `code_log_deferred`, `code_log_deferred_scope`), which `statelog` assembles for every domain alike, this gate does not see and no test plans. A read takes the code log's checkpoint INSIDE its own transaction, so a snapshot's position, its full-or-delta decision and its stream identity describe the rows they came with, and a snapshot spanning pages is held to one monotone history — a `tail` phase, a high-water floor that a later page is read at on whichever node answers, and `ErrSnapshotMoved` when the history moves under it — a reanchor, a tombstone a delta needed swept, or a retirement the snapshot serves swept. A data node serves all of it to the estate router through ONE adapter, `Served` — the engine's and the multi-node suite's alike — whose committer and change writer are nil on a node that does not publish (a maintenance mode), so a proposal or a conversation's write there is `ErrNotServing` and the router hands it to the repository's runner-up, and whose zero value runs nothing rather than panicking on the goroutine that serves the estate. Only a company that sets `code.native` gets a committer and a lifecycle store on its data nodes — Tier B, part of the native runtime like `tracker.backend` (started at the activation that sets it on a node running no native backend yet; a switch under a runtime already running says `native_backend_needs_restart`) and enough on its own to start the state log — while `code.limits` is the EPOCH'S, read at every decision through `LimitsOf`, a conversion of config's own `CodeLimits.Resolved` — `code.merge_by_floor` with it, as `Limits.MergeByFloor`, the least any protected merge needs — so `crewlet validate` and the decide resolve one document one way, and CARRIED from epoch to epoch past a revision that turns the host off, which must drop them, so a committer still running until the restart decides with the last hosting revision's limits rather than the defaults; the engine stops the committer BEFORE the estate subject stops answering, because the withdrawal cancels the answers in flight before it waits for them and a proposal still queued would be answered with that cancellation — a final failure for a push its batch may yet land — where a stopped committer answers it `not serving` and its asker takes it to the runner-up — and a push's wait takes an answer already given over its caller's context ending, because the withdrawal's cancellation reaches every handed-back push's caller beside its refusal, and a select between the two picked the cancellation half the time. The code log's census counts its GIT CLIENTS (`statelog.Census.Clients` = `GitReadsPerDay`: the barriers, at a measured `BarrierRecordBytes` of 176 — the 136 an unsigned barrier cost and the forty bytes of the signed frame every record is appended in (ADR-0031) — that fill a tenth of the ceiling the log's stream HOLDS over the trim's whole min_age, 87 154 a day at the 1 GiB floor and the default seven days), because, beyond the seats' own (a ref resolved for a seat, a read by object id's one re-sync at the head, the read of what a collected coding run pushed, which the seats' term counts), its linearizable reads are a person's or their CI's, which no seat count predicts — a term of the LOG, counted whatever a node's runtime hosts, because the barriers it is held to are every node's over a day, which outlives a revision that turns `code.native` off and spans the rolling restart after one that turns it on; the ceiling read off the broker in the report's own stream read, never Tier A's `stream.code_log_max_bytes`, which sizes only a log not yet created, so a resize (`crewlet retention set-capacity`) moves the census and an edit of the field silences nothing. No seat is gated on it (`GatesSeatAdmission` is false). ITS WAKE FEED is `FeedGroup` (`crewlet-code-feed`, declared by `Domain.FeedGroup`, so the log's trim waits on it; feed.go, wake.go, prompt.go): the code log's own fleet-wide consumer derives every wake about a change from the committed record (ADR-0012), the `Translator` relaying a record only where its routing tells a SEAT something (no routing, a newer build's record, only its actor or only persons told: acknowledged, so a replay of the log from before changes existed wakes nobody), and the `Parser` waking, through `routing.Route` over `Concerns` with each routing's own actor step, the seats the roster still employs, a PERSON dropped after the roster's filter (their inbox reads the notice row; a person who hears still suppresses the lead's fallback copy), each wake's id `changefeed.WakeID(RecordID(op id, position), seat)` — `RecordID` the one identity the notice row and the claim name too — its writer the routing's actor by the kind the routing records (the actor named among `Persons` when a person), the home's records nobody's; `Prompt` keys the conversation on `change:<id>`, is always a pointer (`RequiresRecon`), names the tools that answer, and names the change's work item (`WorkItem`), so the woken turn is charged to the item the change implements — A CHANGE IMPLEMENTS ONE WORK ITEM AND CARRIES NO SPEND (ADR-0067, `TestChangesHaveNoSpendColumns` over every table the domain declares). The READ-BY-OID wire lives here too — `GitQuery` and its eight kinds' answers (the fifth, `landed_in`, whether a commit is in a branch's history at the tip and position its asker resolved, and the push that landed it — `LandedBy` nil, with the range, wherever the answering node's index does not hold the landing; the sixth to the eighth a change's revisions', below — an interdiff answered as a diff is), every answer naming the push that introduced each commit it names (`PushedBy`: who made it, how its pack was built — `PushVia`, `git` or `engine` for a push the engine built itself, STAMPED by its builder, carried by its proposal and its record's entry and stored on its row, whose column's default is no way at all so a row naming none is refused by every reader rather than read as one — and for a seat's push the run behind it, `RunProvenance`: the turn, the work item and the model — and for its coding box's push, the only seat's push git receives, the run that pushed, by its launch's UUID, and its coding agent, both or neither and never on a push the engine built, whose run is the turn that called the tool — which the SERVER stamps from the call or the run's row and never reads from a commit, each field at most `MaxProvenanceBytes` and FITTED to it by `FitProvenance` at whichever site stamps one, never refused, since a work key is another system's label; a push row's `actor` is the credential for a person and the seat's handle for a seat, and its `row_bytes` counts the run beside the commands and the introduced ids) and what that node's index holds (`Coverage`), every revision an object id whole or abbreviated (`ValidRev`: 7 to 40 lowercase hex, never a ref or a revision expression) and every path clean and relative (`GitPath`, with `WirePath`'s exact bytes beside a path that is not UTF-8), `MaxGitAnswerBytes`, `ErrGitUnready` (a refusal about the node that answers, which another git node may answer — `not_held` among its reasons: a node holding no copy, asked by a read that is not stamped `GitQuery.Home`, the one attempt of a walk that may fill a copy, ADR-0059; and each reason says whether the walk may let the next node fill instead, `UnreadyReason.PassesFill`: behind its configuration and `node_failed`, a failure of its own, pass it, syncing and no chart keep it, share keeps it too — the share is the node's, not one repository's, so a busy home handing its fill on would answer a fleet busy everywhere with downloads — and an unknown reason keeps it) and the four read sentinels — with the node's SEAT GIT-WORK SHARE (`GitShare`: `ShareOf` the read lane's half, at least one, waited `GitShareWait`, two seconds, measured) and the instant a served read's answer is due (`AnswerBy`: a reply's margin before its asker's deadline, its cause `ErrAnswerDue`, which `AnswerDue` tells from a caller gone), because the git node that answers a read and every asker import this package and nothing of each other; ONE share a node, so every kind of seat git work it runs — a seat's or a person's read's git (`SeatWorkRead`), an engine write's build (`SeatWorkWrite`) and the engine's own reads, a home's facts and checks file and a check's reads (`SeatWorkEngine`, `GitQuery.SeatWork`, counted apart so the share's waits tell them from the seats') — draws on the same half and no two can between them take the lane a person's push scans in. A change's CLASS is judged here too, by `ClassifyGo` — a pure lexer over two versions of a Go file, go/scanner itself: `whitespace_only` where their tokens AND comments match, the semicolons the lexer inserts at a newline counted among the tokens because a newline that ends a statement is one, and `comment_only` where the tokens match once the comments are set aside. A class only relaxes, so A DIRECTIVE IS CODE, and so is the layout around one: no class where a `//go:`, `//line`, `//export`, `+build` or generator `+marker` line, a linter's `nolint`, `lint:`, `#nosec` or name:value directive (read past the blanks before it, as revive reads `// revive:disable`) or a header's generated-code marker (under which golangci-lint lints none of the file), an example's `Output:` (its whole body) or a cgo preamble (every comment before an import of "C") changed, moved or was re-spaced, and the build constraints are compared as go/build reads them from the header, where a blank line decides a `+build` block. Each is read as its tool reads it: go generate's commands as go generate does, a line at a time whatever lexeme a line sits in (a `//go:generate` line in a block comment or a raw string is a command), and a directive's text with the carriage returns the compiler reads and the lexer drops. A linter's directive holds its file's LINES, since its reach is lines (a nolint's group and the statement under it, the first line of the node a `lint:ignore` is attached to, revive's next line or every line to its enable): in a file holding one, a code token moved onto or off a line, or a comment grown by a line between a directive and the statement go/ast attaches it to, gets no class, through the whole file because a reach may run to its end. `ChangeClass` is the closed set — `no_code_change`, a revision's carried class, then `PathClasses`, narrowest first — and a version that is empty, past `MaxClassedBlobBytes` (1 MiB, past every hand-written file of Go's own tree), lexically broken, or alone in carrying a byte-order mark gets no class; the walk holds one gap of a version's comments at a time, as offsets into it, so what a classification costs follows a file's bytes, not how many comments it holds (`BenchmarkClassifyGo`). THE READS OF A CHANGE'S REVISIONS (`RevisionFactsQuery`, `ReanchorQuery`, `InterdiffQuery`, answered on git nodes by `code/query`) name every commit by the WHOLE id its rows record — an interdiff compares two bases as ids, which an abbreviation would make unequal for one commit. A revision's touched list is bounded by `MaxTouchedPaths` (1 000) and `MaxTouchedBytes` — 48 KiB OF JSON, measured as the encoder writes it (`TouchedCost`), so a facts answer is always one page (`MaxGitAnswerBytes`, 56 KiB) beside its stamps — past either of which a revision TOUCHES ALL, with no list. `Reanchor` is the ONE rule that places lines anchored in a blob, by the zero-context hunks of its diff (`Hunk`, `ParseHunk`): moved by a hunk wholly above, outdated by one over its lines or an insertion between them, never matched by text — for the re-anchoring read, and for whatever splices a finding's suggestion. A LOG REANCHOR'S GENERATION RECORD names its author as every other log's does (`generationActor`, read in either vocabulary the facts carry): the name BARE, its kind in a column of its own and the credential beside it — a person an operator under the human seat they hold, as the code log's eviction gate names one, a seat an agent, the engine the system, nobody named the node — where it recorded `operator:<name>` with the name in the credential column, one person read as two beside the other logs' rows and the credential lost. THE REPOSITORY LIFECYCLE AS TOOLS (`lifecycle.go`): a person's, a unit lead's and a seat's request to create, rename, change, archive, unarchive, delete, restore or protect a repository a TOOL made (`ManagedTools`; one `code.repos` declares refuses every tool `config_managed`, `ErrConfigManaged` — never `ErrNotConfigManaged`, the reconcile's opposite fact) is ONE `LifecycleRequest`, decided by `Store.Lifecycle` on any data node (`code.lifecycle`) in ONE snapshot of the name's or the repository's subject, carrying a CHANGE the decide applies to the rows it reads and never a whole set read elsewhere, a protect request's rule held at the request's own validation to what a record may carry (`Protection.invalid`, the one rule `RepoSettings.Validate` reads for every stored rule, its merge gate's included), so it is refused naming the field its caller sent, whoever asks, before any decide, an outcome the node could not learn answered `OutcomeUnknown` — an outcome its caller makes again as the same operation, never an error, which crossed the estate's wire as words no caller could tell from the node's own fault; one request is one write (the ledger by its op id, and a create's repository id DERIVED from its operation, `RepoIDOf`, so a retry past the ledger's horizon finds its own repository). WHO MAY is one judgement (`lifecycleRefusal`) over the deciding node's chart (`StoreOptions.SeatRules`, the committer's): a bound person maintains every tool-made repository and creates projects for any owner the chart holds, a seat what its grant maintains — the owning unit's effective lead at full_access a project, its own scratch repository — and a settings change is judged as the repository stands AND as it would leave it, and a seat leaves NO PROJECT OWNED BY A SEAT, its own included, by a create or a settings change, though `GrantOf` gives an owning seat maintain (`seatProjectRefusal`: a seat's own repositories are its scratch repositories, capped and expiring, a seat-owned project neither and bounded only by the company-wide `repo_limit`, so only a person makes one); and A PROTECTION BINDS EVERY SEAT, its unit's lead included (ADR-0058): a seat adds a rule or tightens one and never removes or loosens one, whatever its grant, which is a person's — `loosenings` weighs the change against the rule the decide's own snapshot holds for the pattern, control by control — a merge gate field by field (`MergeGate.LoosenedBy`: `merge_by` or `approvals` lowered, an `approvals_unless` class added, a human path dropped or its `unless` widened, a required check dropped, `owners_approve` off, `agents_edit_checks` on, a value this build does not know failing closed either side, `landing.depth`, `landing.batch` and `delete_source` neutral), so a seat adds or raises a gate and a person lowers or removes one, and where a gate sets `owners_approve` a seat takes no glob from a path owner either (`gatingOwnersTaken`) — and `TestEveryProtectionControlIsJudged` and `TestEveryGateFieldIsJudged` hold it to every field of `Protection` and of `MergeGate`, so a control a later field adds is judged alike; a protect request names its rule as a CHANGE (`ProtectChange`, each push control it names replacing that control and the rest kept, `ProtectControlFields` what a tool takes) and its gate as one (`GateChange`, each scalar it names replacing that field, each list changed item by item through `ListChange` and `HumanPathsChange`, the rest kept, `GateChangeFields` what a tool takes), resolved against the rule the decide's own snapshot holds, never a rule, a gate or a list whole, which a caller could form only from a read made elsewhere; a node confirmed behind the configuration (`StoreOptions.Current`) or holding no chart decides none (`ErrNotServing`), and neither does the eviction gate's store, handed no chart and no policy. `RepoKind` FOLLOWS THE NAME (gitref): a project is one label, a SCRATCH repository a seat's own `<handle>.<slug>` at full_access, unprotected, and a rename keeps its kind's form for every actor. A repository NAMES HOW IT IS SEARCHED (`SearchMode`, `RepoSettings.Search`, `RepoRef.Search`; replicated 0079's `code_repos.search`, beside `code_repos_search_idx` over the active and archived repositories by mode and name): `indexed`, from an index of its default branch that a search across the company's repositories reads, or `grep`, read only by a search naming it — a setting like visibility, carried whole by every create, settings and restore record and compared by the reconcile, whose default is applied where its settings are formed: `indexed` for a declaration (config's `CodeSearchMode.Resolved`), `grep` for a tool's create, and a SCRATCH repository `grep` for good (`RepoSettings.Validate`; a settings change asking its index is refused `not_permitted`, a person's included), since an index of a seat's expiring experiment would cost every git node keeping one. The column's default is `''`, no mode, so an apply that forgot it reads back as none. This build records, serves and changes the mode, and indexes and searches nothing. Two caps: `repo_limit` over the PROJECT repositories, declared and tool-made, every status short of purged, read from `code_repo_kinds` (replicated 0068: a row a kind, counted in by a create's apply and out by the purge's, never a count over the rows) at `config.CodeMaxRepos`; `scratch_limit` over every scratch repository a seat owns until purged (`code.scratch.max_per_seat`, a seek of `code_repos_scratch_idx`). A RENAME (`OpRename`, on the new name's subject) leaves the old name REDIRECTING (`code_names.redirect_until`, `RedirectWindow`, a month; `Reader.Resolve` answers `RepoRef.Redirect`) and applies WHOLE OR NOT AT ALL — a repository no longer under its `From`, or a new name another holds, applies as nothing, so two renames of one repository land one — and the code-retention pass releases an old name past its window (`OpReleaseName`, the record's own instant deciding), a purged repository's old names included, which the purge leaves to that release so its own rows stay bounded. The pass then EXPIRES a scratch repository idle past `code.scratch.idle_days` (`last_activity_at`, moved by every push, rename, settings change and restore, never a read or a hold) that no LIVE TURN HOLDS: `Retention.Holds` (the engine's: every run row, and every seat holder's running turns) is asked for each page AFTER the pass fixed its cutoff and the page's position (`ScratchExpiry`), `ExpireScratch` refuses a repository active since the cutoff or with any record past the page, and a turn holding one idle `HoldRecordAfter` (22 h) or more RECORDS its hold too (`OpHold`, decided at the read it orders, `LifecycleRequest.Seen`, moving only `version`), so the broker orders the two; holds that cannot be read expire nothing (`ScratchExpiryUnread`). THE REF HOLD (M3-decisions M3-O61, M3-O64): a seat's branch a live turn names is held on the same set, row and reader, keyed `RefHoldKey` (the repository's id and 32 hex digits of the ref's SHA-256, fixed width so a run row stays small whatever the ref), and only a branch `Holdable` admits, since only a ref a seat set in its OWN namespace names one (`code_refs.seat`, replicated 0087, written by the push apply's `seatOf` from the PRINCIPAL'S KIND, never its seat, since a person's principal names their human seat). A READ — which moves no ref — of a branch unset `RefHoldRecordAfter` (`RefExpiry` less two hours) is a hold record (`Hold.Ref`) moving the ref's `held_position` and the repository's version and nothing a cache or an idle clock reads, so a deletion listed before the read is ordered against it PER REF — the per-repository version moves on every push to any branch; a hold on a branch deleted since answers `ref_deleted`, one on a branch no seat set needs no record, and an archived repository's is recorded too. `ResolvedRef` carries the row's `at` and `seat`, which a hold decides its record from. Every op and field of it is base format under the domain's stated condition, each naming the `versionedFields` row it would take. THE REF LIFECYCLE (M3-decisions M3-O54, M3-O62, `retention_expiry.go`, `expiry.go`) reads those holds: the retention pass's LAST step deletes every branch a seat set in its own namespace and nobody set for `RefExpiry` (14 days) through a PUSH on the engine's own account (`PrincipalSystem` with `ViaExpiry`, paired both ways by `Proposal.Validate`, deletions in seats' namespaces alone; `Principal.Validate` refuses the kind as anybody's asker) to the repository's committer, never a write of its own — so it is decided against every push and hold on the repository's one subject and its push row keeps each branch's `Old`, the restore's source. Its ORDER is the scratch expiry's (cutoff, then the page over `code_refs_expiry_idx`, replicated 0091, and its position as the proposals' `Seen`, then `Retention.Holds` for an active repository's refs alone), and the decide's rule 4b (`ExpiryRule`, pure, judged on the proposal's own clock, never the committer's) keeps a branch set by an earlier push of the batch, held by a record past the page, the default branch, a live change's source (its sentence naming the change, M4-D32) or target, or one no seat set last — and the page leaves a live change's branches out (`expiryListingSQL`'s two `NOT EXISTS` seeks), so a branch under review is never proposed on every pass. A record of expiries alone moves no activity clock (`onlyExpiry`). Its pushes are cut by `MaxCommandsPerPush` and by `EntryRoom`, as `crewlet code import` cuts its batches; `busy` ends the step. AN ENGINE WRITE'S WIRE is this package's too (`write.go`, ADR-0062), as a read by object id's is, because the tool that asks one, the router that carries it and the git node that builds it import this package and nothing of each other: `EngineWrite` — six kinds (`EngineWriteKinds`), `apply_suggestions` among them, whose `SuggestionOp`s carry findings' suggestions by the blob and lines each was read at, each placed in the head's file by the one rule a finding is shown by, `Reanchor`, or fed a hunk at a time as a diff streams by (`Placing`, `anchor.go`, so no reader holds a diff's hunks) — its principal a seat with the run that made it — its turn, never a coding run, whose box pushes through git — or a person through a credential bound to their human seat, a token bound to nobody writing nothing — `EngineWriteResult`, `Conflict` (its base, ours and theirs stage ids tagged wire fields, each readable whole by its id) and the CHANGE'S own refusals (`WriteRefusals`, UNRECORDED: decided on the git node before anything is stored), every party holding a write to ONE rule (`EngineWrite.Validate`), its message to gitref's `CommitText`, every path it names to `MaxTreePathDepth` components (by `validWritePath`, beside the name's own rule, so a check's report path is held to no write's bound) and every new path to `ValidTreePath` — the EARLY, NAMED refusal of what git's fsck refuses of a name, and more (a control character, a backslash, a code point HFS+ ignores, `.git` in every spelling git's `is_ntfs_dotgit` and `is_hfs_dotgit` read as one), never of what an object holds, which the build's `index-pack --strict` judges — and a link is put by its target's text alone, never by a blob id, and never at a name git reads as one of its control files (`.gitmodules`, `.gitattributes`, `.gitignore`, `.mailmap`, in NTFS's short spellings too), which git refuses or ignores as a link. Its push id is an UNNAMED derivation of the caller's operation (`WritePushID`) — a canonical UUIDv7, which a scratch's name and a proposal's push id must be — so a retry anywhere is the same push; its bounds are argued at each constant: `MaxCommitPayloadBytes` 1 MiB of operations or suggestions and message AS JSON SPELLS THEM, for every kind (a revert's or a cherry-pick's message too, an apply's suggestions and message), never by the text's length, since the encoding is what every hop holds and a `<` is six bytes of it, `MaxFileOps` 1 000 and `MaxWritePackBytes` 4 MiB (both PROVISIONAL, re-derived by the engine-commit benchmark), `WriteBuildDeadline` one read's 10 s, `MaxTreePathBytes` 4 096, `MaxTreePathDepth` 64 components (a JUDGMENT: with `MaxFileOps`, what bounds the directories one build rewrites, its `mktree` levels and its read of what its paths are, which git walks from the root at a cost growing with the square of the depth) and `ConflictMarkedBytes` 2 KiB; and `MinProposeAttempt` is the ONE sum of what a push's proposal needs of its caller's deadline, which the estate router's write floor and the code host's pre-upload rule both read, so the two never disagree on it. A proposal the engine built (`ViaEngine`) moves one ref and carries no pack past `MaxWritePackBytes`, refused by every committer otherwise as one no git node built. NOT A RECORD: nothing of a write rides the code log but its push. A READ BY OBJECT ID's kinds (`GitReadKinds`) are estate requests' and no record's — a kind added moves no `RecordVersion` — and among them a path's HISTORY (`GitLog`: `LogQuery` with a literal `Grep`, never a pattern, a `Since` the git node formats as a number, a `Limit` of 1 to `MaxLogLimit` 100, 25 by default) and a file's BLAME (`GitBlame`: a range of at most `MaxBlameLines` 500), whose every field git leaves unbounded is DESCRIBED BY ITS SIZE past its bound, never cut — a subject past `MaxLogSubjectBytes` (4 KiB), an author's name or address past `MaxLogPersonBytes` (1 KiB, a judgment: RFC 5321's 254-octet address with room), a blamed line past `MaxBlameLineBytes` (8 KiB), each measured ENCODED as the code tools send it, and a merge's parents past `MaxLogParents` (64) by their count — so the widest commit and the widest line with its commit always fit a page; the push behind each commit is the answer's `PushedBy`, as every read's is. A REPOSITORY'S CHECKS ARE A FILE OF ITS OWN (`checksfile.go`): `.crewlet/checks.yaml` (`ChecksFilePath`), read by ONE STRICT PARSER, `ParseChecks`, that walks the YAML tree itself — a key it does not know, a key written twice, a number or a boolean where text belongs (`name: 1.10` is a number), a fraction where whole seconds belong, an alias, a merge key, a second document and every value past its bound are refused — a LIST PAST ITS BOUND BY ITS COUNT ALONE, none of its items read or judged (`checkList`, the one statement of each list's count rule, which `defFaults` holds a record's definition to as well), so a parse costs the file's bound and never what a 64 KiB file can pack into one list — EVERY fault at its authored path and line as a `config.Fault`, the first `MaxChecksFileFaults` (128) listed and the rest counted by one more (one unexported join that IS `ErrInvalid`: no estate operation answers a checks file's refusal, so no wire carries it), so `crewlet validate -tier checks` reports a file as it reports a company document — into `CheckDef`s: a name (`config.ValidCodeCheckName`, the one rule a merge gate's required checks are held to as well, never a copy of the domain's own), a command handed to `sh -c` whole (`MaxCheckRunBytes` 4 KiB), a timeout of 1 s to `CheckTimeoutCeiling` (config's six hours, GitHub Actions' per-job ceiling), at most `MaxCheckPaths` gitref path globs, JUnit and SARIF report paths held to `ValidTreePath`, a `CheckCell` (config's `CodeCheckCells`, container or e2b, with direct and self refused BY NAME in config's own sentence, U1 a — the company's `code.checks` names its cells from the same list), a cone held to the sandbox's own `ConePathProblem` or `none`, and at most `MaxCheckEnv` variable names, each held to config's `ValidCodeCheckEnvName`, the rule the company's `code.checks.env` keys are; at most `MaxChecksPerFile` checks in `MaxChecksFileBytes` (64 KiB). `CheckDef.Validate` holds a definition a record carries to the same rules (`defFaults`, their one statement). The file that governs a change's checks is its TARGET's, and which checks a merge requires is a protection rule's, never the file's. WHAT BECAME OF A CHECK IS ROWS ON A SUBJECT OF THE REPOSITORY'S CHECKS (`KindChecks`, `checks.<repo>`, `ChecksPath` `c/k/<repo>`, appended last to `ObjectKinds`; replicated 0075): `code_checks`, one row an attempt of a check of a commit FOR ONE TARGET BRANCH (keyed by the repository, the commit, the target, the check and the attempt, replicated 0085: the branch whose checks file defines the check and whose protection requires it, so one commit checked for two branches, a head two changes into two branches share, is two checks, each its own branch's, and the `CheckKey` every record of an attempt carries names it), and `code_check_logs`, the log a failed run kept (`CheckLogReferences`, Required) — written by `check_queued`, `check_started`, `check_finished` and `checks_sweep` (`CheckOps`, checks.go) and by no other subject's record but the repository's purge, whose parts take them last in the same rows-and-bytes budget and whose scope names `ChecksPath` for it. The subject is ADDITIVE (`Arbitrated` false: no runner's claim waits behind another's), so every apply is a JOIN (`apply_checks.go`) and its records commute, as `PatternAdditive` requires: the state is DERIVED and only rises, a claim tie keeps the lower (StartedAt, Runner) and the box is set once by its holder, an ending tie keeps a measurement (passed, failed) over infrastructure's (errored, cancelled, skipped), then the earlier, and the log follows the ending kept. Rows are kept `CheckHistory` (30 days): every ending removes its own repository's oldest past its broker instant (at most `CheckHorizonRows`, `MaxRecordRows`/8, and `CheckHorizonBytes`, weighed by a covering read of `row_bytes`), and the retention duty's checks step (`Store.ChecksSweep`, listing `code_checks_recent_idx`) sweeps a repository whose checks stopped ending — LISTED PAST THE HISTORY BY `CheckSweepGrace` (a day), which no repository whose checks keep ending reaches, and SWEPT BACK TO THE HISTORY, so the duty never repeats what an ending's horizon does and sweeps a repository at most once a grace; the deletion gate reads the repository off the subject. Every payload's bounds are its `Validate` (`CheckQueued`, `CheckStarted`, `CheckFinished`, `ChecksSweep`), base format under the domain's stated condition, each op naming its `versionedFields` row. A CHECK'S RECORDS ARE WRITTEN BY THE REPOSITORY'S COMMITTING DATA NODE (`checkswriter.go`): `ChecksWriter`, the committer's sibling, asked through the estate's `code.checks` (`ChecksRequest`: exactly one of `Queued`, `Started`, `Finished` — the engine's own, refusing an `Asker` — or `Rerun`, `Report` — a seat's or a person's, naming its `Asker`, so `code.checks` is declared `judgesGrant` for a rerun (`JudgesChart`) and the writer refuses one while behind or holding no chart (`ChecksWriterOptions.SeatRules`, REQUIRED, and `Current`) — under the asker's op id and `Seen`; `ChecksResult`: outcome, `Unchanged`, a `CheckRefusal`, the `Holder` and the attempt's `CheckRow`), takes ONE REPOSITORY'S CHECK WRITES ONE AT A TIME — at most `MaxCheckWritesWaiting` (256) of a repository holding or waiting for its turn, `ChecksWriterNodeWrites` (1 024) in all and `ChecksWriterNodeBytes` of them, a write past any answered busy at once, since each holds its estate answer slot while it waits (`flight.go`, the change writer's single flights too) — each held from its decide to its record's apply here, so every decide reads what the last left and a duplicate appends only across a writer failover, where the join converges it. Its decide is the ATTEMPT ARITHMETIC — a dispatch is a commit's first attempt for its target and one queued already is answered `Unchanged`, the next attempt follows an ENDED one (`not_finished`), the home's requeue only an infrastructure ending (`CheckReason.Requeued`: runner lost, box or setup failed; else `rerun_genuine`) below `AutoRequeueAttempts` (3), the home's dispatch past the first only a dispatch's own `defs_invalid` error (else `rerun_genuine`), none past `MaxCheckAttempts` (`attempts_exhausted`) — and THE CLAIM: another runner's or an ended attempt's claim is refused `claimed` before it appends, naming the holder; the holder's own sent again is unchanged and its box record sets the box once; an ending the join would not keep is answered from the row. A rerun and a report — a principal's, judged by a grant this writer holds no chart for — are no request it decides (`ErrInvalidProposal`). An ending that applied, uncollapsed, and that its row keeps is announced ONCE, from here, as `code_check_finished` on the node's event stream (`ChecksWriterOptions.Events`; never by an applier); one that REPLACED an ending the row kept — a measurement after an infrastructure ending, a slow runner's exit after its attempt was errored `runner_lost` — names the replaced ending (`Replaces`, as the decide read it) and is counted under `replaced=<that state>`, never as the attempt's second ending, so a day files it as a correction; it counts `crewlet.code.checks.queued`, `.finished` and `.claims`. A RERUN AND A REPORT ARE A PRINCIPAL'S (`checkasks.go`), decided in the same flight from one snapshot and neither a record of its own: a rerun queues the attempt after the latest its asker read, copying that attempt's queue (purpose, change, definition, required) — a SEAT only an `errored`/`cancelled` attempt (`rerun_genuine` for a measurement), only with `AccessWrite` or as an author of the change (`not_permitted`), held to its turn's requester (`WriteCeiling`); a person any ended attempt — and a REPORT is a person's CI's claim (`running`, placement `external`) or ending (reason `reported`, naming the person), QUEUING NOTHING, which is how a CI's row is told from the engine's: a report of a check the engine queued, and a rerun of a reported one, are `native_check`. A check sweep stays `Store.ChecksSweep`'s, which no write of the subject races.
