The checks are read (`checksread.go`) as `checks` — a repository's newest ended first, a change's, the attempts that judge it (its head's, or a commit it names, CHECKED FOR ITS OWN TARGET, on `code_checks`' key, whichever change's dispatch queued them; no index by change, which a head another change into the same branch shares would read empty), or a commit's for every branch it was checked for, by target, name and attempt, or every readable repository's ended since `CheckQuery.Since` (the window), each a keyset held to the asker's grant (`listedReposAmong`, shared with the changes listing) and each row naming its repository by name (`CheckRow.Repo`, which a box admission's read leaves out) — `check`, one attempt whole (`ErrCheckNotFound`), and the home's `checks_due` (refusing an asker, STALE by default, its `ChecksDueQuery.RefreshBefore` REQUIRED): the live changes whose head has no DISPATCH for its target naming the checks file the target's reading holds, or whose head's latest attempt of a check for its target its infrastructure ended below `AutoRequeueAttempts` (a REQUEUE: `code_checks_requeue_idx`, replicated 0088, partial on those, the next attempt's queue whole), each with its `CheckWork` — the names its head holds for its target (`Held`), those an earlier dispatch errored for a file that did not run (`Invalid`, queued again as the next attempt), its target's gate's required names (`Required`, `GateRulesOf`'s) and the change's attempts not yet ended at a head it moved on from or for a target it left (`Stale`, `code_checks_live_idx`, replicated 0088, unless a live change into the attempt's target heads its commit) — and the targets whose branch moved past the tip their file was read at once that reading is older than the home's refresh (`DueTargets`, a repository's first page). The dispatch is a record of its own (`checksdispatch.go`: `CheckDispatched`, op `check_dispatched`, on the checks subject through `code.checks`) — the home's reading of a target's checks file at its tip, `DefsOID` empty for none, and the heads it dispatched under it, after queuing their checks — kept in `code_check_targets` and `code_check_dispatches` (replicated 0084), each row a JOIN keeping the reading of the later tip, then the later record, and both purged with the repository. So the list converges on what the home DECIDED, never on attempts: listed while its commit held no attempt, a head whose target defines no checks — every repository that has not adopted them — was listed for ever, and a check a target's file gained never reached a head holding an attempt of the old one's; the targets' readings are refreshed at the home's pace, a record a moved target, never a record a waiting head a push. `CheckQueued.Invalid` records a required check ERRORED at its dispatch (`defs_invalid`, no definition, the file's refusal its summary). A principal's `checks` listing narrows every shape — a repository's, a change's, a commit's, the window — by a check's `Name` and a `State` (`CheckQuery`), TESTED ON THE ROWS A PAGE READ beside the grant and never a seek: an index per state or per name is one more write on every attempt's every transition for a filter a person applies to a page, so a narrowed page may be short, or empty with a cursor. And `check_box` (`ReadQuery.CheckBox`, the engine's own, refusing an asker and every other field): ONE attempt's row by its KEY, a point read of its repository's checks subject, one row or none — what a check's box's admission reads on every request (code/host), a kind of its own rather than a `check` read naming no asker, so a principal's read that forgot its asker is refused rather than answered ungated. `checks_queued` (the engine's own, a page and a cursor alone): every repository's queued attempts OLDEST QUEUED FIRST on `code_checks_queued_idx`, each with its definition and its repository's name (`RunnableCheck`), those of a deleted repository skipped by its key — a git node's claim walk (code/checkrun). `checks_running` (`ReadQuery.ChecksRunning`, the engine's own): the repositories holding a running attempt (`code_checks_running_idx`'s leading column, grouped) or one repository's running attempts in one page — the home's sweep of a runner that is gone. A read by object id made FOR A CHECK (`GitQuery.Check`, the API's `/box/code/`) is the engine's own as a revision's facts are (`GitQuery.EnginesOwn`, the query server's one test): no asker, no grant, recorded as nobody's read, and held by `Validate` to the attempt's commit — a tree or a file at it, a diff whose head it is — whatever node built it; a build predating the field refuses it as a read naming no asker. `CheckBoxGrace` (`sandbox.DefaultSetupTimeout`, ten minutes) is how long a check's box, and the token it fetches with, outlive the check's own timeout. `QueueDue.Enqueued` is what the merge-rate alarm counts a queue as asked: every entry enqueued on the target within `MergeRateWindow` (1 h), live, landed, ejected or dequeued alike, counted on `code_queue_live_idx` and `code_queue_ended_idx` up to `MergeRateScan` (4 096) — the home times the rounds, since only it builds the trains. `Reader.ChecksBacklog` is the `code_checks_backlog` alarm's reading: the queued attempts past their OWN definition's `timeout_seconds` (ADR-0015: the threshold is every check's, never the alarm's), walked IN THE ORDER THEY FELL OVERDUE on `code_checks_overdue_idx` — each row's `overdue_at`, its queued instant plus its timeout, which the applier writes from the attempt's queue (replicated 0083) — so every row read is one past its time and the count is exact up to `BacklogScan` (1 024), past which it says there are more; never walked oldest QUEUED first, which with timeouts from a second to six hours put a thousand attempts of a long check, none overdue, ahead of a one-minute lint stuck for an hour. The longest wait among those counted is named by its repository's name, commit, check and attempt. An attempt of a repository that is deleted, being purged or gone is NOT counted, tested by the repository's key as the walk reads it: every write of its checks is refused `repo_deleted` — a runner's claim and the home's cancel alike — so its attempts wait for the purge, claimable by nobody, and counted they raised the alarm on every data node for the retention's days. `Reader.RefsNearLimit` is the `code_refs_near_limit` alarm's reading (`refsnear.go`): the ACTIVE repositories whose live refs and the refs set within `RefExpiry` together reach `MaxRefsPerRepo` (`NearLimit`, judged `>=` as the push decide judges what a repository holds, so one sitting at the bound with nothing recent is counted; ADR-0015: the threshold is the push decide's bound and the ref lifecycle's window, never the alarm's — at that rate the bound is reached within the window, and nothing set within it is the lifecycle's to delete), found by a KEYSET WALK of `code_repos_name_idx` (the reconcile's `livesSQL` idiom, never a scan, which the plan gate refuses) of those holding at least half the bound (`refsCandidateFloor`) — the only ones that can reach it, recent refs being live ones — and each one's recent refs counted over its key's `repo_id` prefix, tombstones never (a deletion is no ref set); every statement its own STALE read: a listing statement visits every `code_repos` row of the name index until it has found `lifePage` candidates, so it is bounded by the table, not by the page, and a count is one primary-key range of the candidate's live refs and tombstones, 200 000 entries at the most. `BenchmarkRefsNearLimit` (10 000 repositories, three of 100 000 live refs and 100 000 tombstones) reads in 142 ms, under the one second past which the count would take an index `(repo_id, at)` of its own, so the primary key serves. A CODING BOX'S GRANT is its seat's HELD TO ITS RUN'S (`Grant.Within`): no command at all where the run was given the repository to read — its own namespace's included, which needs only the seat's tier and relation — and every row outside its namespace capped at what the run was given; judged at the code host's box admission alone, since a run's grant is coordination state and the decide reads no run row, while the seat's is judged there and again by rule 2b. A SEAT'S RE-SYNCS ARE HELD TO THE CENSUS (`resync.go`): `NewResyncs` is a git node's ONE set of buckets for them on `internal/costlimit` (`Resyncs`: `Box` draws a box's, keyed by `RunResyncs` or `CheckKey.Resyncs` and counted `crewlet.code.box.resyncs`, `Turn` a tool's) — a box's run, or a tool's turn, on its seat, one seat bucket for both, `ResyncBurst` (twice `MaxRunRepos`, 16, so the run bound moves it) refilled at `statelog.LinearizableReadsPerSeatDay` (125 a day, borrowed) — `CheckKey.Resyncs` a check's box's key on them, its attempt on its repository's checks — and `NotRejudged` the clause an answer at the floor carries past them. In a turn a colleague seat woke the box is held to that colleague's own grant as well (`Grant.Under`, the requester ceiling of M3-decisions U40): a read needs both grants, and a command outside the seat's own work needs the colleague's RefRule to pass it too — its namespace and a repository its seat owns are its own work (`Grant.OwnWork`), which whoever messages it steers; read by the launch's precheck and the box admission, never by the decide. A PERSON'S CODE INBOX: `person.<handle>` (`KindPerson`, arbitrated, scope `c/p/<handle>`) carries `marks` records — one `inbox.Gesture` decided on the person's own record by whichever data node it is asked of (`Marks`, the estate's unkeyed `code.mark`), the positions of the notices it names read from their rows inside the decide and carried on the record, so the apply (`code_person_marks`, replicated 0092) is pure over the record, its instant and the row before; refused for anybody but the person holding the seat. `ReadInbox` pages a person's notices off `code_notifications_inbox_idx` with every mark filter bound into ONE statement, each notice held to the asker's grant on its repository and carrying its change's title, state, work item, description's first KiB, open agent findings by severity and, for a live change, its readiness's counts and first sentence, judged at the read as the listing judges it (`readinessIn` over the page's rows, read once by `changesByIDIn`). A CHANGE MERGED THROUGH A TOOL is an engine write of its own kind, `WriteMergeChange` (`merge_change`, M5-D11): the change by its number, its target as `Ref` and its head as `ExpectedHead`, a `MergeMethod`, `DeleteSource`, a person's `Override` and the `CloseTask` an enqueue records — `EngineWrite.validMerge` holds its shape, and no other kind carries any of them; it is answered `EngineWriteResult.Merge` (`MergeAnswer`: queued with its entry and place, a `MergeRefusal` with every unmet precondition, how many times it was rebuilt), `not_fast_forward` a `WriteRefusal` and `target_moving` a `MergeRefusal` (the builder's, after `MergeRecomputes`, 4). A kind's build budget is `EngineWriteKind.BuildDeadline`: `MergeBuildDeadline` (30 s, three of `WriteBuildDeadline`, which `code/home`'s `MergeabilityDeadline` is) for a merge, `WriteBuildDeadline` for every kind that lands as one push. A CHANGE REBASED is `WriteRebaseChange` (`rebase_change`): the change by its number, its source as `Ref` at the head its caller read, nothing else — one forced push of the source, built under `RebaseBuildDeadline` (40 s, PROVISIONAL, `BenchmarkEngineRebase`) of at most `MaxRebaseCommits` (1 000) commits, `too_many_commits` a `WriteRefusal` past it, a merge among them `merge_commit`, and a commit git would not write back as it was `rejected_object`. A REPOSITORY'S MIRRORS AND ITS IMPORT (`mirror.go`): a PULL MIRROR (`RepoSettings.MirrorFrom`), a PUSH MIRROR (`MirrorTo`, pushing as one seat's own identity, a seat that reads the repository — `MirrorSeatRefusal`, which config's `CodeRepo.mirrorSeatReads` restates and the engine holds it to) and a create's IMPORT (`CreateRepo.Import`), each URL read by `gitref.ParseRemoteURL` and each credential ONE WHOLE `${VAR}` reference, recorded as the reference and resolved only by the dial, so no record carries a token; a create that imports — its own import or a pull mirror's first fetch — makes the repository `importing` (`RepoStatusImporting`, a status of no DDL that every live listing, the reconcile's walk and a restore read, while the compactor's, the publisher's and the expunge's partial listings pass it by), and `import_source` fixes what it fetches at the create (replicated 0095). An importing repository takes NO WRITE — a push, a change's, an archive, an expunge — refused `repo_importing`, and a pull mirror (`RepoRef.Mirrored`, the answer's field the code host's admission reads) no push and no change but a close and a landing, refused `repo_mirrored` (`pushRepo.writeRefusal`, one order for the push decide and the change decide), and none of a merge's steps either — a merge, an enqueue, a queue's candidate or its landing — each refused `repo_mirrored` or `repo_importing` as a `MergeRefusal` (`decideMerge`, `decideQueue`; a dequeue and an eject still land on a pull mirror, moving no ref, so it sheds its queue); `ImportingDetail` and `MirroredDetail` are the one sentence each. ONLY A PERSON OR THE CONFIGURATION SETS A MIRROR OR AN IMPORT (U49 a, `mirrorsAsked`): a seat is refused `not_permitted` whatever its grant, asked before every other grant rule. BASE FORMAT AS A STATED CONDITION: the three fields and the status name the `versionedFields` rows they would take. WHAT A HOME FETCHED LANDS AS RECORDS OF THEIR OWN (`decide_remote.go`, `apply_remote.go`): an import's chunks (`import_chunk`, ONLY IN ITS TURN — `Seq` one past the row's `import_seq`, else `stale`) and its end (`import_done`, the status back to active, `import_error`, the source's HEAD as the default branch only where the declared one did not arrive), and a pull mirror's syncs (`mirror_sync`, refused `expunging` unrecorded against a moved `drop_epoch`, M6-D4), each through the repository's one queue alone (`Committer.ProposeImport`/`ProposeMirrorSync`, estate `code.import`/`code.mirror_sync`), each command's old the row's tip or the whole record `stale`, and each landing as ONE PUSH through `landPushes` — a push row `via import` or `via mirror` by the engine's own account, its several packs the record's (`recordPacks`, kind `import`/`mirror`, so the row names no single pack), the ref drops and the changes it moves; `imports_due` (one walk of `code_repos_importing_idx`) and `mirrors_due` (push mirrors by id, then pull mirrors least recently synced first, on their two partial indexes) are the home's listings. A CHAIN (`ChainBuild`, `ChainEntry`, `ChainChange`/`ChainChangeOf`, `ChainCommit`, `ChainResult`; chain.go) is the home's in-process ask of its git node's builder for a run of queue entries merged in one scratch — never a record, so held to no record format — at most `MaxChainEntries` (64 = `MaxPushesPerRecord`, the most one landing names), its pack at most `MaxChainPackBytes` (64 × `MaxWritePackBytes`), each entry's author date its entry's instant (`QueueEntryID` keeps the enqueue's), `Validate` naming the field. A ONE-REPOSITORY CODE SEARCH is a git read (`GitSearch`, `search.go`: `SearchQuery` carries codeindex's query AS ITS PARTS — text, mode, case, paths, ref — never a compiled pattern, so the answering node builds the expression and its plan by the one grammar the asker validated with; `SearchAnswer`, `SearchHit`, `SearchVia` `index`/`grep`; `MaxSearchHits` 500, `MaxSearchLineBytes` 256, `MaxIndexedFileBytes` = `codeindex.MaxFileBytes`, `SearchBudget` the resolve budget), an estate request rather than a record, with the unready reason `index_building` (`UnreadyIndexBuilding`, passing no fill) |
| `knowledge` | The backend-neutral seam the turn-start knowledge prefetch and the `search_knowledge` builtin both read through. Exactly ONE backend per company, because two would make "what do we already know about this" depend on which searcher was asked. A caller passes a seat, plain text and ancestor exclusions — never a CQL fragment, a space key or a project list — and search is BEST EFFORT: every failure path is an empty result, so a turn never dies because a wiki was slow — and a search that never ran says so (no mode served, none of the corpus covered), so "the knowledge base could not be searched" is never told as "nothing matched". It also declares the ONE bound on a query's text (`MaxQueryBytes`, 400 bytes) for both ranked searches — this seam's and the tracker's — which every surface, the dashboard included, refuses past and none cuts, because a search on part of a question answers another one |
| `providers/llm` | The model contract, sized to what a coding CLI can be handed and can honestly report — EVERY `providers.llm` entry is one (`cliagent`), and the only other implementations are the chain and test doubles. Messages and tools in (offered, never forced: there is no tool-choice field), an answer, its calls and its usage out, and NOTHING ELSE: no temperature, no output cap, no effort, no reasoning field, because no CLI takes one per call, and a field no backend sends is a setting every caller believes it changed — a caller that needs a short answer says so in its prompt. TWO stop reasons (`end`, `tool_use`), the two a CLI can name; a CLI that hit its ceiling or declined says so in prose or fails. It does NOT retry (fallback belongs to the chain) and does NOT decide what a failure means beyond a coarse kind. An entry has TWO modes: `text` drives the CLI as a model behind the engine's own tool loop, `agent` hands the executor to the CLI's own loop as a detached coding run with the seat's tools bridged in — and only the executor branches, so every other phase stays a text call on the same entry |
| `providers/llm/chain` | The seat's model fallback: an `llm.Provider` over its entries, tried in order. Fallback is per CALL, not per round — a member that fails is dropped with what it produced and the next starts the call from scratch, so a conversation is never stitched from two models — and a stream that moves on says so (`Delta.Restart`) rather than concatenating two half-answers. A FATAL kind stops the walk — a call the engine or its host cannot make (a prompt it cannot render, a CLI it cannot start), which is the operator's to fix — and nothing else does: a CLI that RAN and failed, however it failed, is its vendor's outage or its own crash, and the next member shares neither. It names the member that answered on the answer itself (`Completion.ProviderKey`), because a chain shared by concurrent callers has no other place that fact is true |
| `providers/llm/cliagent` | The ONLY model backend: a coding CLI driven as a process, because each vendor's own harness — the request shape each model takes, how its thinking is carried, caching, which sign-in bills which account — is work the vendors keep current and a copy of it here was permanently behind. Three problems make it more than "shell out": SHARED MEMORY (every call runs in its seat's own home with an ALLOWLISTED environment, `workspace.go`, `env.go` — and a call that hands its CLI a KEY in a KEY HOME of its own, never seeded with the login and never carried back from, its credential files dropped with each generation, because a CLI may keep a key's session in its login's own file: Cursor Agent writes `CURSOR_API_KEY`'s, the key among it, into the `auth.json` its login lives in, which carried back became every subscription seat's sign-in), NO TOOL CHANNEL (the catalogue and a response contract ride the prompt and a parser that never errors reads the envelope back, `prompt.go`, `envelope.go`) and VENDOR DRIFT (every flag and JSON path is profile data, `cliprofile`). A SPENT PLAN is recognised by the vendor's verbatim sentence plus the reset instant it carries, never by keyword — and on a run the CLI did not report failed, only where that sentence OPENS what it said, because what a clean run says is otherwise the model's own prose and tool arguments, and a sentinel matched inside them threw the answer away and benched a working entry (stderr, a failed run's output and a CLI's own failure event are matched throughout) — and BENCHES the entry on this node (`bench.go`): calls fail at once as `rate_limit` until that reset, capped at an hour, or for a minute where none was named — so a seat moves to its next model without a process launched to hear the same sentence, and NOT shared with the fleet, since each peer learns it from its own calls once per window — at most `max_concurrent`, because a call that queued for a slot is refused once it holds one — and a coordination read in front of every call would put the store under every turn. A missing LOGIN is never benched: `crewlet llm login` mends it from another process, and the next call must find it. Usage is the CLI's own report, cache reads and writes summed back into the input; a CLI that reports none is charged four characters a token, which `doctor` says. A CLI that STARTED is never `fatal`: `run`'s error means only that none did, and everything that happened to one that ran is a result its caller classifies (`exec.go`). Its output is on pipes the engine owns, because which stream a helper the CLI left running still holds decides the call — a held stdout is an answer nobody can show is whole, refused as `server`, while a held stderr costs only diagnosis and the answer stands — and os/exec, handed writers, reports both as one `ErrWaitDelay`. Building a provider writes NOTHING (the workspace is a process-wide map keyed by the absolute state directory AND the CLI, each CLI keeping its files under `<state dir>/<agent>` so two CLIs on one directory never meet — keyed by the directory alone, an entry moved to another CLI was refused by every running node — and a configured bundle is restored by `RestoreConfiguredBundle`, never by `New`), so `crewlet validate` is not a write to the host. A credential BUNDLE NAMES THE CLI it was exported from, in a manifest (`cliprofile.BundleManifest`) that is never written into the credential directory, and a restore or an import refuses one naming another CLI or none (`ErrForeignBundle`) before anything is written: a bundle is keyed by the entry, which can move to another CLI, and a file name cannot say whose a login is — six CLIs keep an `auth.json` — so judged by its files' names alone, codex's bundle was written into pi's empty login, which then turned the right bundle away as a login already present |
| `providers/llm/cliagent/cliprofile` | What each coding CLI ACCEPTS — the profile table (`profiles.yaml`): its binary and flags, the JSON shapes its answer and usage arrive in, where it keeps its login, which variable carries its key (`api_key_env`) and its headless token (`token_env`), and the grammar its model flag reads (`model_names_provider`) — plus the merge of an entry's `overrides` into it and the rules a merged profile is held to. A built-in may carry a `platforms` section (`darwin`, `linux`) merged in BEFORE the overrides, so an operator's field outranks it, and every built-in is validated as each platform reads it, not only the one CI runs on. A LEAF beside the backend rather than inside it, because `config` judges an entry against its profile, so a typo in `overrides` or a credential no variable of the CLI carries is refused by every write path — `crewlet validate`, every API admission and every apply — rather than by the epoch build alone, which the API never runs. Standard library and the YAML decoder only, because `config` imports it |
| `providers/embeddings` | The vector backend: the knowledge corpus duty, each knowledge search's query vector, and diary and episode recall. It owns the FACT of what a model accepts — `Limits` in bytes of `Prepare`d text (whitespace collapsed and each stray byte a U+FFFD, as the request's JSON encoding makes it, so every bound, digest and cut is of the text the server decodes), resolved from `config.EmbeddingModels`, because every tokenizer these models use emits at most one token per byte plus a wrapper, so no tokenizer is needed — and enforces it: an input past the bound is refused before anything is sent (`ErrTooLong`), and `EmbedBatch` packs a batch into the requests the model's limits admit (`Limits.Requests`). The POLICY for a longer text is the caller's: the corpus embeds its `Opening`, a turn's ask is `EmbedWhole` — pieces cut between words, one call, a length-weighted mean of their directions. Nothing retries, because what a failure costs differs by caller; what every caller gets instead is the CLASS — `ErrRefused` (refused again unchanged), `ErrTransient`, `ErrConfiguration` — so the corpus can set aside one poison input rather than resend its batch for ever. A batch request has its own ceiling (`BatchTimeout`, a minute, a fifth of the corpus tick's progress budget) apart from a single call's fifteen seconds. The fake enforces the same limits by the same code and fails on request in the same classes. It is also the engine's one model-vendor SDK client (`openai.go`, `vendor.go`), since no coding CLI computes embeddings: built with NO SDK RETRIES and with every `OPENAI_*` setting the SDK reads from the process environment undone (`withoutAmbientEnvironment`), so an admin key, an organization header or a custom header exported for something else never reaches the endpoint an embedder names, and with what the endpoint SAID about a refusal (`detail`) on the error, since the SDK's own text names the status alone |
| `sandbox` | Code work as a suspended Execute phase. A coding run is DETACHED: the tool starts it, the loop suspends, and the engine resumes that same loop — minutes later, possibly after a restart, possibly on another node. Nothing parks a goroutine on a running job. Two real backends behind one contract — a remote VM per run (E2B, cloud or self-hosted) and the engine host itself — held as a CATALOGUE keyed on `Placement`, because `providers.sandbox` configures which boxes exist and each seat picks its cell with `role.sandbox.run_in`. The conversation is written before the flip to `running` that makes it resumable, and a call to the log before its count and cost reach the row (`LaunchRecord.BridgedCalls`), so no reader of the row finds missing what the row names; a relaunch removes the launch it replaced's two, the run's end its last launch's after the record, and the coordination store's sweep whatever a write racing that end leaves. `CloneRecipe` is THE ONE WAY a box is given such a repository (a coding run's blobless clone of the default branch ALONE — a repository's other branches are mostly other seats' work, which a clone of every branch would send to every box and a default refspec pull into it on each fetch — its base fetched by id wherever it is; a check's single commit, fetched by id or BORROWED — `CloneBorrowed` checks it out of a git node's copy mounted read-only, named as git's one alternate, nothing fetched and the remote no promisor, so a missing object fails loudly rather than costing the host the bytes the borrow saves), after `GitVersionStep` holds the box's git to the client floor; git's FILE-SYSTEM MONITOR is THE CELL'S to give, never a clone's: `CodingSteps` — the version check, each coding clone, and `MonitorStep` after them — gives the monitor's step only in a cell whose boxes own their kernel (`Placement.OwnsKernel`: `e2b`), since every `direct` or `container` box spends the engine user's inotify watches and instances, a rootless runtime's user namespace included (the kernel charges a namespace's watches to the user that made it too), beside the other seats' coding CLIs; the step turns the monitor on in each checkout's OWN configuration where the box's git is 2.55.0 or newer and EVERY checkout at the top of the box — an earlier job's in a reused box too — takes at most HALF of the watches (the least of the host's and the namespace's) and of the instances, else off with any daemon stopped, never a failure: the half is headroom for a tree that grows past its clone, since a daemon refused a watch dies and every later command starts it again, slower than no monitor (`BenchmarkBoxWorktree`'s fsmonitor=starved); A `Mount` — a directory of the engine host given at a path of the box's own, outside its home, whose mount point the runtime would otherwise make inside the box's own directory as root — is a container's alone (`Placement.TakesMounts`): refused for any other cell where every box is made (`mountsFit`, since the remote backend reads none) and by the local backend for its own, never dropped. `LaunchRequest.RunEnv` is asked once the store has named the job, and the launch's environment reaches every setup step — the telemetry endpoint alone is minted at the job's start, its token's span running from its minting.
