three links a live pack and one each for HEAD, config and packed-refs, never a GET or a git process (a full round's own index aside), and one sync a directory it wrote, once, before the rename, the installed chain's two among them and none at its layer step (four to six fsyncs, six to nine for a copy serving from a published chain, whose two chain files it writes from the rows; synced step by step it took up to eleven), counted by `crewlet.code.cache.swaps` by `graph`; a maintenance run on the old copy stopped and waited for first and counted as a run of the duty cycle (M3-O15), the old copy's index of pushes taking no commit while the swap is the entry's (`entry.swap`), and what is learnt of the old copy's files afterwards the copy serving them's (`serving`): a verdict reached over them, what a git process over them reports (its scrub's evidence, never a drop, as is what the round's own index reports from the staged directory, `swapAt`), and a request whose git failed over the old copy, answered from the copy serving as it stands so it runs once more with its repair unspent (`swappedAnswer`). A swap that FAILS leaves the old copy serving — a superset of the rows — for the repository's next round to try again (a live pack, HEAD, config or packed-refs gone from it marks it to be read whole), and a CRASH before the rename (`codefault.SyncSwapBeforeRename`) leaves it the highest in `repos/`, its state naming the floor the retirements of what leaves it are above (`LeavingSince`), so the boot's first round reads them as retirements and keeps the graph; an incarnation nobody can hold yet (unconverged and unpinned: a boot's first round, a rebuild's product) sheds what leaves it in place. A DROPPING PACK IS SERVED AND NEVER RECEIVED OVER (M6-D3): a pack only rows an expunge's begin left dropping (`code.PackDropping`) lives in the incarnation's DROPPING VIEW, `objects-dropping/pack` (`gitproc.DroppingDir`), where its rows place it (`packPlace`: the install, the live set, a delta's leaving and a full round's strays, the swap's links, the check, the scrub, Verify, a rebuild's seed and the boot's walk all read the place, so a pack whose rows move it is downloaded to its new place and its old files leave by the swap), never under `objects/`, so a push's scratch and an advertisement view, which lean on `objects/` alone, and a compaction's build, whose store links its inputs from `objects/pack` and is refused a dropping one (`mergedLive`), never rely on one; every process that SERVES the copy names it (`Synced.Dropping`, the view while the live set places a pack there: the host's upload-pack and its advertisement, `Missing`, every `code/query` read — and the round's peel, a placement's canary and Verify's connectivity check through the view the directory holds, `heldView`), `TestOnlyServingReadsNameTheDroppingView` holding every git process that names it to a closed table both ways over the writers gate's walk; no index covers it and no layer names it; the budget counts it (the state's `DroppingBytes`, the running total `crewlet.code.cache.dropping.bytes`); and a push carries the drop epoch the copy's `objects/` holds (`Synced.DropEpoch`) — the rows' once nothing is leaving and the installed chain holds no pack for a row that is not live, the epoch before while a begin's inputs are still in it, a delta that finds the rows past it taking every pack the chain holds for no live row out with its swap — so a push checked over a copy holding what a begin made dropping is refused `expunging` (`TestTheImageHoldsExactlyLiveAndDroppingPacks`). AN EXPUNGE'S COMMIT IS THE ONE RETIREMENT THAT DROPS THE COMMIT-GRAPH (X04's one drop, `EventPackExpunged`: a delta's retired row whose live entry was dropping, a full round's stray in the view no row names, each event of a full round's strays its own `dropFor` over its own packs): the commits it takes out leave the repository, and over a graph still naming one git 2.56 answers `rev-list -1 <it>` and walks through it; the memo goes with it, an answer perhaps quoting what was purged, and so does the node's tip, while the index and the published layers, which never cover the view, stay (`TestOnlyAnExpungeDropsTheCommitGraph`, a delta's swap and a boot's full round). `Holds` answers, from memory and starting nothing, whether the cache holds a copy of a repository — an incarnation this process converged or its disk kept — or has a round filling one now, never an entry whose rounds all ended with none: what a git node asks before a read it was not asked to fill (`code.GitQuery.Home`, ADR-0059), because a round once begun runs on after its reader stops waiting, and every node free to start one turned a read of a repository nobody held into a download on every git node the read was asked of. Each janitor tick stamps the LAST USE of every repository used since the tick before on its state file's modification time, which the boot reads back, so a repository read all day and pushed to rarely is not the oldest entry after every restart — and EVERY STATE WRITTEN carries the last use too (`writeState` through `stampUse`), never the moment of the write, since the maintainer, the backfill and the memo's trims write states of copies nobody read, which came back from a restart as the copies read last. `Stop` decides whether anything of the cache's is running in the directory under the cache's OWN lock, never against its context: nothing, and the claim is back before it returns whatever the context says; something, and it waits bounded by its context and keeps the claim until that work has ended; a pin released after it moves nothing, since the directory is the next `Open`'s. `Sync` writes it as a FUNCTION OF ROWS — refs exactly the repository's `code_refs` at the position its state names, packs exactly its `code_packs` — by CONVERGENCE: a DELTA after every push on every node, priced by what moved (the state rewritten only when the directory or the repository's row changed, every field of the row but its position (`sameRepo`), a delta neither listing nor sweeping it), and a FULL read only on an incarnation's first round in a process, which sweeps the directory to its image (config and HEAD put right before any git runs there, whatever in `objects/pack` is no pack's file — a lock a maintenance run killed with its node left, over which git would refuse the round's index — before that index, the index over exactly the snapshot's packs before the refs' peel reads them, the packs it drops after the new refs are in) and is what makes it exact from any starting point while reusing every pack it holds — and NOTHING IS ANSWERED AT ONCE until that round has run, because a previous process's state names positions that a log restored while the node was down gives to other records. It never moves backwards for a STALE COPY — a delta behind it on its own stream changes nothing, and a full answer behind it or a `not found` read below linearizable is asked ONCE again at the log's head before anything is concluded, so a lagging copy neither purges a repository nor takes a cache back — and it does follow the LOG back: one RECREATED under it (provisioned anew, its stamp not the one the cache read under, seen in any answer) and one RESTORED from an older copy (ADR-0053 keeps its stamp, so only a linearizable answer below a position this process read — any repository's, since one log is under all of them — can say so) each mark every repository on the node to be read whole before it is answered at once again, counted `crewlet.code.cache.rebuilds{reason=stream|rewound}`. A broker restored under a RUNNING node and written past every position the node read before any of its linearizable reads lands is the one shape that escapes, and the restore runbook's stopped fleet rules it out. A snapshot is read across pages as the domain's contract has it: restarted from page 1 at the SAME position on `ErrSnapshotMoved`, at most `MaxSnapshotRestarts` (3) times, a ref a delta serves twice taking its later row, and every row validated before anything is written (`ErrBadSnapshot`), because a ref goes into a file git parses line by line. A FULL READ of a repository's rows holds one of the node's `MaxFullRounds` slots (8, `store.MinReaderConns`: the fewest reader connections a data node's store opens) from its first page to its last — every full round's, and a check's and a hint's reads of a whole snapshot — granted first come, first served and gauged as `crewlet.code.cache.full_rounds{state}`, while a delta holds none (a delta the log answers in full holds one from its second page), and every page the estate answers is timed as `crewlet.code.cache.read.page.duration{kind}`: every page is one read transaction on a data node — one of the 64 answers its lane gives the whole fleet's reads at once, and one of the reader connections each of those reads takes, which database/sql hands to a waiting read drawn at random (on a node that holds the data, its own store's) — so a node coming up with hundreds of repositories asks no more of a data node than the smallest scans at once; a round waits for its slot under the cache's lifetime and its callers under their own sync budgets, never a budget of its own — PROVISIONAL, from `BenchmarkColdJoin`'s model of the smallest data node (a node restarted over its kept cache came up at the readers' own rate at eight and no sooner above it, 7.6 s at four, the other reads waiting 105 ms at eight and 323 ms at sixteen; four nodes joining kept them at 792 ms at eight, where sixteen broke the one-second rule). The byte path is S3's: each push's pack BUNDLE downloaded with ONE GET, split into its `.pack`, `.idx` and `.rev` as it streams, held to its row by `code.CheckBundle` and renamed in `.pack`, `.rev`, `.idx` LAST — no git process, no `index-pack` anywhere but the receiver's — largest first, `FetchConcurrency` (16) a round under `NodeFetches` (32, `httpx.MaxIdleConnsPerHost`, counting objects rather than requests) a node: HALF THE NODE A REPOSITORY, so a second repository is never starved behind the first's longest downloads — which a lone cold sync pays for, MEASURED (`BenchmarkColdSync`, git 2.56.0, the S3 fake at a 30 ms first byte, 2 048 pushes): 389 bundles a second at sixteen against 705 at thirty-two — sixteen bound by its GETs (454 a second at the 35 ms each took), thirty-two by its GETs and the node's own side of each bundle, its files created, written, synced and renamed (about 700 a second, `BenchmarkBundleFiles`) — never by git, which it does not run; at S3's own 100–200 ms first byte the fake gave 77–149 at sixteen and 149–292 at thirty-two, bound by the GETs alone, so a cold sync's length is its pack count — a compacted repository's fifty in under a second, the 2 048 a repository holds at most in 27 s at 200 ms — and the widths stay at the bucket's own transport's warm connections (s3obj's clone of httpx's, HTTP/1.1, `httpx.MaxIdleConnsPerHost` of them). A node slot is ONE GET AND ITS 256 KiB COPY BUFFER, handed out together, taken only once the bundle's three files exist and given back — with the round's share — the moment the store's reader closes, before the install's checks, syncs and renames — so the node's download memory is 32 buffers however many syncs wait, and neither the node's GET rate nor a round's is spent on its disk; what bounds that disk work is the packs IN HAND, two shares a round and 64 a node (`installsPerFetch`), and a bundle's three files are synced AT ONCE beside every other bundle being placed, so their syncs share journal commits. Every pack row is held to `code.PackRef.Check` with the rest of the snapshot, before any byte moves; a bundle that is not its row's is `ErrCorruptPack` and never retried; a pack the store lacks is `ErrPackMissing` only once a read at the log's head made AFTER the store's answer still names it — asked whatever level the round read at, since even a linearizable round's barrier predates that answer and a row can leave the log under a long round (a purge's part, or a retirement whose grace the round outlived, ADR-0051), and DECIDED BY THAT READ — asked in full whenever the answer that named the pack was, since no delta from the copy's position reaches a pack at or below it: one still naming the pack ends the round at once, nothing installed again (applied again, it fetched every pack the not-found had cancelled and, on a forced round, hashed every index file again, for the same not-found), and one that does not converges the round to what the log holds, a pack the store lacks from it asked the same way — EACH NOT-FOUND BY THE READ AFTER ITS OWN, never by an earlier one, which called a pack whose row left the log after it missing — while one the store holds DAMAGED is final at once, and one it fails to hand over for no reason that is an answer about it is `ErrStoreRead` — marked on the STORE'S OWN SIDE of the copy (its reader's reads and close), never by elimination, so this node's disk refusing a write into the bundle's staged files is the node's failure and never sends an operator to the store — as the estate failing a page is `ErrLogRead`, the one failure of a sync that is the code log's (its answers, no such repository and a history that moved, passed on as themselves). `packed-refs` is PEELED (`# pack-refs with: peeled sorted`, one `cat-file` a round over the tags it sets — 5 000 tags advertise in 7 ms peeled and 28 ms not), merged IN PLACE as a stream on a delta — hashed as it is read and held to the digest this cache wrote, allocating for what moved rather than for the file — and rewritten whole when it is not the file this cache wrote (gone, unreadable or altered). A full round that leaves `MidxRoundPacks` (256) packs and NO multi-pack-index FILE writes one over exactly the snapshot's packs (`--stdin-packs`) without a bitmap, in the READ lane, before the refs' peel and before it answers — never over an index on disk, since git deletes a bitmap beside an index it is asked to write again — and a round under that count writes none, because the index is a git process on the very request the round was synced for and, MEASURED over a source repository's history split into packs (`BenchmarkPackLookup`), its cost follows the objects while its saving follows the packs: at 256 packs the cold clone alone pays it back over 100 MB and over a gigabyte (0.55 s against a 0.13 s write, 11 s against 1.3, six runs each), while at 128 a clone saves nothing either size shows and only the requests after it — 130 to 280 fetches — could repay the write; the maintainer's own follows NOT a debounce later but once the packs have stood still and the runs ahead of it have ended — one at a time on four cores, most packs first, so a node that just joined waits out the runs of every repository it synced, and what its clones miss meanwhile is the maintainer's BITMAP (1.4 s against 9.6 at 128 packs) far more than the round's index. `MidxPacks` (64) is apart from it, the count of packs gathered beside the maintainer's index that makes a repository due again: MEASURED (`BenchmarkMidx`'s stale arms, on the node pin) each such pack costs a request that reaches it about 0.06 ms of walk at 100 MB and 0.08 at a gigabyte, against a run of 1.16 s and 14.2 s, so a run every M pushes and the walks between them cost least at 48 at 100 MB for sixteen requests a push, and 64 costs a twenty-fifth past that. The `crewlet.code.cache.bytes` and `.repos` gauges are RUNNING TOTALS every install, removal and incarnation moves, so a gauge update is O(1) rather than a walk of every entry. One round in flight a repository and one queued behind it: a linearizable caller joins only a round that started after it arrived, and every round runs under the cache's lifetime, never a caller's. It is EVICTED TO ITS BUDGET AND FOR NO OTHER REASON — least recently used first, never a repository a request or a maintenance run holds, one larger than the whole budget served and evicted as its last reader lets go, the janitor kicked by a round that takes the cache past the budget and by that last unpin rather than left to its tick, a file in `scratch/` counted only when it has no other name (an advertisement view's `packed-refs` and an adopted push's pack are an incarnation's own, linked), an entry gone from under that measure counted as the nothing it now is and never logged as unmeasured, at whatever depth the walk meets its absence (the host moves a view out, and a push's receive-pack removes its object quarantine, between the listing that names it and the walk's open of it), and the decision made under the cache's lock with the rename after it, so no sync on the node waits on one — because an idle repository costs disk the budget already bounds while evicting it costs a GET a push at its next fetch. `receive-pack` NEVER RUNS IN A CACHE — under protocol v2 any object present is fetchable by id, so a pack left in a cache before its row applied would be served by one node alone and connected against by a later push — so a push runs in a SCRATCH repository (`NewScratch`: empty refs, the incarnation's objects its one alternate, that incarnation PINNED so the budget never evicts it from under the push) and its advertisement in a VIEW (`AdvertiseView`) whose `packed-refs` is the incarnation's own file hard-linked, a snapshot no sync's rename moves, run with `AdvertiseConfig` (`core.alternateRefsPrefixes` at a prefix no ref can carry) so it does not list its alternate's refs a second time — 18 ms against 26 at 10 000 refs and 117 against 222 at `code.MaxRefsPerRepo`, measured — and NEVER on a push's scratch, whose connectivity check takes exactly those refs as its negative tips; the pushed pack enters only once its row has applied — by `Adopt`, a hard link of the scratch's three files held to the row's checksum and every section's size, rather than a download of the receiving node's own push. The entry's write lock is WEIGHTED: a round and Verify's listing take ALL of it, an adoption ONE, so adoptions of one repository never exclude one another, and one that finds the lock taken or wanted HANDS its staged links to the holder rather than waiting — the round installs them in place of that pack's GET, and whatever it did not reach is installed as the lock is let go, before its callers are answered — so the node that took a push downloads it only when a round that read the push's row had already begun that pack's GET before the adoption handed it the staging, or when its links fail; a directory sync that fails leaves the pack installed and is returned, and every outcome is counted (`crewlet.code.cache.adoptions{outcome}`). A COMPACTION AND A PUBLICATION (`code/compact`) ask the cache what they plan, build and adopt from, and WRITE NO INCARNATION (ADR-0060, ADR-0061): the live rows of the incarnation it synced, to plan from (`Packs`, copied from the live set under the entry's lock); the published chain as the copy holds it (`Chain`: the rows' chain, the prefix installed and the layers refused, ONE reading under the entry's lock, so a plan never pairs an installation with a refusal of another moment) and the repositories whose copy holds a refused layer (`Damaged`, a maintainer's REPAIRS); its ADMISSION to the maintenance lane (`AdmitCompaction` and `AdmitPublication`, ONE `admitBackground` — two copies of the slot-and-charge rule are how one drifts — each an `Admission`, a publication's charged at the LARGEST of its steps, which run one after another in its one slot: `PublishBytesPerObject` (600, measured: a root publication's bitmap held 384 bytes an object of 10 MB, 560 of 100 MB and 287 of a gigabyte) an object of the WHOLE repository for a ROOT's bitmap, the figure `MaintenanceCharge` places a repository's maintainer by beside a merge of every object (and `PlacementCharge` the same over the declarations the build ships, for a node that opens no cache — a person's purge names the maintainer by it), and `PublishTailBytesPerObject` (350, measured: 176, 312 and 90) for a layer over a base, every publication after the first, `GraphLayerBytesPerObject` (350, measured) an object of its layer and `GraphBaseBytes` beside it for its commit-graph layer, and a compaction's for its repack; a publication refused for memory kept on the incarnation for the maintainer's walk to count toward `code_maintenance_refused` until one is admitted or the rows' chain moves) — a slot reserved, as a check's is, and the build's memory charged AS A RUN of the maintainer's, at its limit of slots and never the one kept for a verify, beside the room the latest pass left STANDING for the repository it held back (`maintFlight.standing`, which a compaction admitted between passes would otherwise take, the pass then holding that repository back again), at `CompactBytesPerObject` (550, measured by `BenchmarkCompact`'s history arms and a publication's repack in `BenchmarkMidx`) an object it merges, with room beside them for a transfer of as many (`chargeOver` over the inputs alone at `gitproc.TransferBytesPerObject`, never the clone of the WHOLE repository every step of the maintainer's keeps room for, because the build is the one background step whose refusal is not slower: the maintainer alone merges a repository's packs, a memory refusal is refused again at every poll, and a repository at `code.MaxPacksPerRepo` refuses every push carrying a pack — so a room that grew with the repository refused every merge, and in time every push, of a repository past some 39 million objects on a node of 16 GiB and four cores, about 13 GB and under the 20 GiB default, and of one past some 3.2 million on a node of 2 GiB a core, for as long as the node's memory stayed as it was), and `CompactWindowBytes` (six times `max_blob_bytes`, measured by its blobs arm: the build's `pack.windowMemory` — twice the largest blob, a per-thread bound the pool's one thread makes the whole window — caps the window and not what the build holds around it) — refused `ErrNoMemory` and logged once an incarnation (`code_cache_compaction_refused`, `maintenance.refusals{step=compact}`), and given back, once however often, when the build's last git process ends; a SCRATCH named by the compaction's id and leaning on a STORE OF EXACTLY THE PACKS IT MERGES (`NewMergeScratch`: `scratch/merge.<repository>.<id>.git`, each input's `.pack`, `.idx` and `.rev` hard-linked from the incarnation and nothing else, removed with the scratch, a git report over it routed to that incarnation by `mergeAt`) — because `pack-objects --stdin-packs` walks every commit, tree and blob it can reach to fill in its name hashes and only an excluded pack stops it, so over the incarnation a roll of a few small packs walked the repository's whole history (1.39 s at 400 032 objects at review; in `BenchmarkCompact`'s roll arm a roll of eight 20 KB pushes took 451 ms and 100.8 MiB beside the 100 MB source history, and over its store 19.6 ms and 0.8 MiB — a roll of sixteen, the floor since, 22.9 ms and 0.95 MiB) — and listed by `Scratch.Packs` — or a publication's of HARD LINKS of every pack, the refs and the base layers, its chain files naming EXACTLY its base (`NewLayerScratch`, refused past the installed prefix), into which its repack's outputs, built in a merge's scratch of their own (`NewMergeScratch`, as a compaction's are), are gathered by link (`Scratch.Gather`) — never built there, where its links of every pack would have `--stdin-packs` walk the whole history; and `Adopt` of every output of that scratch in ONE call once their rows applied — one share of the lock, one `dropFor`, one directory sync — and `AdoptLayer` of a publication's layer, so the maintainer downloads nothing it built, while the inputs leave its copy, as every node's, by the next round's swap. AN EXPUNGE asks for two scratches more (`expunge.go`), each made UNDER THE ENTRY'S WRITE LOCK so the packed-refs it links and the live set it reads are one moment's (a round records a pack in its live set only after renaming the refs that reach it in): `StageExpunge`, `scratch/expunge-<id>.git` holding links of the inputs and leaning on a merge's store of EVERY OTHER LIVE PACK — by the live rows, never the directory, whose chain-held retired packs would hide an input's objects from the build — refused while any row is dropping; and `StageRescue`, `scratch/rescue-<id>.git` holding links of the dropping view's packs and leaning on the incarnation's objects/ alone, refused `ErrInputsLeaving` while `Synced.DropEpoch` is below the row's (the begin's inputs, which hold every dropping object, still in objects/); `DroppingPacks` is what a commit retires, `Scratch.RefTips` the tips the linked packed-refs name, `Scratch.StorePacks` the rest a dropping build excludes by name, and `AdmitExpunge` (a third background step, `expunge`, its refusal logged `code_cache_expunge_refused` and raising no alarm) charges a merge's figure of what it sorts beside a clone's room of the WHOLE repository, since its walk reads the history. The live set keeps each row's content date (`livePack.content`, `Packs` answers it), learned from a round's rows and kept across an adoption, which names none — a purge's cycle chooses its inputs by it. `Missing` is one `cat-file --batch-check --buffer` FED A FETCH'S WANTS AS A STREAM — read from wherever the request is, memory or a spool, one id as cat-file takes it, never joined or held whole — ended at the FIRST absent id, which is all a fetch's one linearizable sync needs (14 ms against 22 unbuffered, measured), each id refused by name as it is read unless it is an object id, since what reaches cat-file's input is a revision expression to git — and a check that met DAMAGE answers the damage, never an absent id (`gitproc.Result.Damage`, a stderr line's kind whatever the exit status: cat-file calls an object behind a truncated index `missing` and exits 0), so the host repairs the copy under the fetch rather than let upload-pack refuse the want in band, past any retry. `Spool` is the file a fetch request past the host's in-memory part is written to, and the one a revision's facts write a change's patch into for `git patch-id` to read back (`code/query`), made under `scratch/` and UNLINKED AT ONCE: the open file and nothing else, so the kernel removes it however the request or the process ends, and the janitor, which measures `scratch/` by its names, never counts it against the budget — a spool draws on its caller's own bound (the host's allowance, the facts' `query.PatchSpoolAllowance`), and counted here one reader's large request would evict warm repositories. A CHECK (`AdmitVerify`, `Verification.Run`) holds a repository to its rows: the image grammar the sweep keeps (one set of predicates for both), every row's pack hashed as the bundle the push stored, `packed-refs` against the digest written with it, and `fsck --connectivity-only` — ADMITTED BEFORE IT READS ANYTHING, a free maintenance slot (`gitproc.Pool.Reserve`) and its fsck's memory taken first and held to its end, refused rather than queued, so a busy node's refusal costs nothing and the lane bounds the checks (admitted at its fsck, a busy answer came after reading up to twenty gibibytes, and so did every retry), and ONLY OF A COPY THE NODE HOLDS (`ErrNotHeld`), since a check that built one would download the whole repository and evict a warm one to keep it — EACH PASS BOUNDED by what the copy holds (`CheckBudget`: the fsck's `gitproc.PackWorkDeadline` and `VerifyHashPerMiB`, about 1.1 s a mebibyte against a measured 9 ms) and ended there (`ErrPastBound`), its bound told to the caller as the pass begins, so a check wedged on a disk holds neither its slot nor the operator's sweep — and the copy BROUGHT TO ITS CALLER'S FLOOR before it is checked (`VerifyRun.Fresh`: a sweep names its listing's position, read at the head once, so it appends one barrier to the code log rather than one a repository; none is the head) by a sync that REPAIRS NOTHING, the damage its own git meets answered to the check as its first finding rather than repaired under it, so a check never downloads the repository unasked nor reports clean the damage it was asked to name — listing with the repository's rounds held off and a maintenance run on it LEFT TO FINISH, the files it holds while it runs admitted and none started from the listing to the fsck, so no step half done reads as damage and an operator's check never throws away an hours-long bitmap; asked to repair, it repairs AS WHAT IT FOUND ASKS (`reason=verify`, each way counted once it has read the copy whole): where every problem is the copy's OWN DIRECTORY out of place — an entry outside the image, a fixed file missing or not what a full round writes, a pack no row names, a shape the pool refuses, an fsck failure over packs that all hash to their rows — it reads the copy whole IN PLACE (`repairInPlace`: the full round's sweep, every pack, index, bitmap and graph kept, no GET, no new incarnation — but a pack no row names, which leaves the copy the check holds by a swap, its index and graph with it, still no GET), because rebuilt for what a sweep puts right a copy served bitmapless clones until the maintainer wrote its index again, and for good on a node whose memory now refuses the bitmap; DAMAGE — a pack missing, at another size or hashing to another bundle, a derived file failing its checksum, damage the sync's git met that is no shape — and whatever the read in place leaves, a shape written past its sweep included, it REBUILDS — the read in place being a CHECKING caller as the sync before it is, so damage its own git meets is the second check's finding rather than the repair a git process asks for, which rebuilt it unseeded under `shape` or was refused by that line's window — under none of a git process's windows, SEEDED with every pack it has just found whole — linked rather than downloaded, so one damaged pack among thousands costs one GET — and either way checks the copy again, and a repair it saw whole ENDS ITS LINE, so that incarnation's next failure is rebuilt at once. `Rebuild` REPAIRS a damaged repository — a pack or an index git could not read, an object a row names that no pack holds, or a directory the pool REFUSES for its SHAPE (its config not `gitproc.RepoConfig`, a `commondir`, an objects directory not its own, a planted alternates file: `gitproc.ErrRepoShape`), each named by the ONE reading of a git failure, `DamageReason` (git's format strings' `Part`: a multi-pack-index, a commit-graph, or a chunk-format file of either, ALONE, is `derived`, dropped in place — from a request's git or a round's own; the sentence naming a corrupt packed object — git 2.56's through a bare `%s`, and the same sentence's ROW on 2.55, whose message the pool reads as a hint beside the damage — is a hint confirmed by `ConfirmHint`'s hash of the pack it names against its row, a confirmed one naming THAT pack to the repair (`Failure.Packs`) — the row read from the snapshot's PACKS PHASE alone (`readPacks`, no refs), at the incarnation's own position and at the log's head only where no row names it there — each pack hashed ONCE however many hints name it and ON THE CACHE'S LIFETIME, never a request's: every hint about a pack being hashed waits for that hash as long as its own caller does, and a caller that leaves (`gone`) leaves the hash running for the next, since damage on a hot repository fails every request in flight on it together and a balancer's cut would otherwise restart a slow disk's hash from byte zero for ever; a round's own git is confirmed the same way AFTER the round lets the entry's write lock go AND ENDS — its incarnation pinned instead, the round queued behind it started at once — because a linearizable caller never rides a round begun before it asked and every answer that names a ref is one, so a round kept running through its hash held every fetch's and push's advertisement on the node behind it; only that round's own callers wait on a hash (queued again for a round on the current incarnation should a rebuild replace theirs meanwhile); a verdict is kept with the pack's three files' IDENTITY (device, inode, size, and the mtime of each but the `.pack`, below) and reused while they are those files, whatever else is written into the copy, so a refused rebuild's next failing request takes the verdict rather than another hash; bounded by `HashBudget` of the bundle's bytes (`CheckBudget` less its fsck term) and hashed a `CheckPieceBytes` (64 MiB) piece at a time under a READ SLOT each (`gitproc.Pool.HoldRead`), so N repositories damaged together hash on the read lane's cores rather than beside them; a hash with no slot in `SlotWait`, past its bound or whose files moved decides nothing (`unread`) — counted `crewlet.code.cache.hints{outcome}` per hint and `crewlet.code.cache.hint.hashes{outcome}` per hash; and the wants check's chunk-format report is narrowed to the index its `cat-file` loaded, `gitproc.Damage.Narrow`) (`ShapeReason` for a process in a scratch or a view, which only the shape of its alternate — `ShapeError.InAlternate` — is the cache's), whether the host's process or a round's own peel found it, the round then answered by the repair under the same rules. A SHAPE IS REPAIRED IN PLACE: the pool refused the directory before git read a byte, so no pack is lost, and the incarnation is marked to be read whole (counted under `shape` once that read converges, logged `code_cache_reshaped` with what it moved) — the full round's sweep rewrites its config and moves aside whatever the image never holds, reusing every pack, with no GET, no second copy on disk and none of a rebuild's refusals, where a rebuild was a download of the whole repository (thousands of GETs for the default largest) and every fetch and push of it on the node waited behind it; that sweep then holds the directory to the pool's own `gitproc.CheckShape`, so only a shape that SURVIVES a full round — something that keeps writing into it — is rebuilt, by the rules below. A DAMAGED DERIVED FILE IS REPAIRED IN PLACE THE SAME WAY (`derived`, asked by the host with the files the damage names — its `Failure`'s `Drops`: the index, the graph, or both for a chunk-format message, and its `Began`, so a file this process wrote after the failing process began is spared — or marked by a round whose own git met it): the full round DROPS them (`dropDamaged` in `apply`, through `dropFor`, only the declarations the damage named, since the graph names no position in the index) off a mark of its own (the incarnation's `drop` declarations) — whatever else the copy is already marked to be read whole for, its shape or a log recreated under it, which the read is then counted under, since keyed on the read's reason that round kept the damaged index and the fetch's retry was answered still damaged — so the round or the maintainer rewrites sound ones over the same packs (a graph-less copy's graph first) — no download, no new incarnation, logged `code_cache_derived_repaired` with its `files` — and damage that survives it (a pack the index named is itself damaged) escalates to `corrupt`: on a request's retry (`host`'s `repair`), and in a round that had itself dropped every file the damage names. A PACK'S OR AN OBJECT'S DAMAGE A REQUEST MET — OR A ROUND'S OWN PEEL — IS REPAIRED IN PLACE FIRST, in one `Rebuild` (a round's in the same steps, `damagedRound`'s `repairDamaged`, each waiting caller from the step it has reached, so each passes each step once and a pack the store will not hand over ends, while its download is held off, at the store's own refusal of it — no forced round that could fetch nothing and no rebuild asking the store for the same pack (`repairMark.fetchable`, `heldOffFor`) — where a round's own damage was read whole into a new incarnation, thousands of GETs for the default largest): a failure whose process began before the cache last wrote a REPAIR into the copy (`incarnation.repairWrite`: a pack installed again over its files or put back after it was lost — by WHICHEVER ROUND puts it back, a request's, a sync's own repair or any that reads the copy whole, since whatever puts it back ends a failure its loss caused (`lost` in install.go, which no answer below the copy's own position satisfies: only a log gone back gives one, and its positions past the copy name other records) — a derived file dropped — never a push's pack installed or adopted, which replaces nothing a failing process read, and counted so answered every failure on a repository pushed to every few seconds from the damaged copy) is answered from the copy as it stands, after any round still repairing it (it joins the round queued behind a running one; Kind `SyncNone`, `reports{outcome=stale}`) — it may have failed on what the repair replaced, and the host counts that answer no repair and runs it once more (a sync whose own round met it so is synced again by a round of the copy, `standOrSync`, never answered from a copy no round converged); otherwise a FORCED full round — whose check SPENDS what forced it once it has run, whatever the round meets after, what it found going on the repair mark (`recordCheck`), where its forces, settled only past an install, made every later round of a copy whose round failed at its install — a pack it adds the store does not hand over — a forced round again, every index file hashed again under the write lock until an install succeeded, and a round sent back to a second apply by a not-found hash them all twice — at most once a `ScrubCooldown` while nothing is written (`forcedLately`: a forced check with no repair written since it ended, its verdict recorded as its round ends whatever that round met after it — the round's own repairs ending it — sends the next hour's failures straight to the scrub, so a busy repository failing on one damaged pack body costs one read of every index file rather than one a request), holds every index file to its trailer whatever its vouch and installs again over its files, one GET a pack, every pack whose index files fail, that the copy LOST (the rows name it at a position the copy had read — a push it has not read yet repairs nothing, `lost` in install.go) or holds at another size, or that a confirmed hint named, answering from the repaired copy (counted `stored`, or `derived` when it only dropped); the guard is asked again before the scrub and before the rebuild (`Cache.escalate`), since a repair may be written into the copy while the request's runs — the scrubber's own repair of the same damage beside the request's scrub — and such a repair answers the request as it stands; a round that repaired nothing runs the copy's SCRUB at once for its callers (`scrubFor`, the node's one scrub, taken ahead of every scrub the scrubber has due and stopping a routine one at its next piece; answered from its verdict when a scrub of the copy began after the failing process, or found nothing within `ScrubCooldown` with no repair written since, and by the repair when what an earlier scrub found still waits for it — either beside what a round's check found since that waits too, every pack a waiting finding names left out of the packs it answers whole, which a rebuild links: a check's finding alone answers nothing there, since a check reads no pack body), whose findings the round it asks installs; and only past both is the repository rebuilt, SEEDED with every pack the scrub found whole, so damage in the rows downloads nothing — escalated only while the round's incarnation is the one the request failed on (`waiter.failedOn`), since a rebuild that replaced it meanwhile is the answer; a full round whose `packed-refs` is already byte for byte what it would write leaves it in place and wrote nothing (refs.go), or every in-place round read as written into and a rebuild of what the last one read was never refused as unchanged. What neither repair in place ends is read whole at linearizable into the NEXT incarnation BESIDE the one its readers hold and swapped in only once converged (a failed one goes into `trash/`, the old one still serving, and the syncs it was queued over are answered by an ordinary round on it, never with its failure): one rebuild however many requests failed on one directory, none for a request whose incarnation was already replaced, NONE FOR A DAY of an incarnation holding exactly what a rebuild that did not end the failure read (the same verified rows and objects make the same directory) — unless anything writes into it meanwhile, a round's packs, refs, index or sweep or the maintainer's index or graph, and only a day because the store can come to hold what it lacked and a disk can damage what a rebuild wrote — a line of attempts that keep failing spaced `RebuildCooldown` (10 min) doubling to `RebuildBackoffCap` (a day), and a line ENDED by `RebuildQuiet` (that day) with no rebuild asked, refusals included: the cache never sees a request succeed, so quiet is the one sign a rebuild ended its failure, and a shorter quiet would rebuild damage asked about just over ten minutes apart 144 times a day; counted by `crewlet.code.cache.rebuilds` under the reason git gave, `corrupt`, `derived`, `missing` or `shape` (a shape surviving its sweep ends `unmoved`, since no round makes a directory of another shape, so only the doubling cooldown holds it back), and every refusal by `crewlet.code.cache.rebuild.refusals{reason,rule}` and logged `code_cache_rebuild_refused` once a cooldown — the measure of whether the window is ever reached; every reason and every rule (a type of its own, `refusalRule`) is held to both instruments' reference text by a test reading the declarations. NOTHING AN INDEX FILE SAYS IS SERVED FROM A FILE THIS PROCESS DID NOT WRITE OR CHECK (check.go): every commit-graph, multi-pack-index, bitmap, `.idx` and `.rev` is held to git's own hashfile trailer — the SHA-1 of the bytes before it, a `.idx`'s and a `.rev`'s pack checksum beside it, by `code.HashTrailer`, the rule's one implementation, which a published layer's check shares, and every other hash of this node's files (a hint's of a pack) by its piece loop `code.HashPieces` — by a full round BEFORE it answers, because git checks none when it reads and a damaged one answers wrong without a word (a graph naming the wrong parent forced a protected branch through the force check on 2.55 and later, an `.idx` offset made a clone ship the wrong object, a bitmap made every clone incomplete): at an incarnation's first full round in a process, and after only what this process has neither written nor checked, trusted by IDENTITY (device, inode, size, mtime — never ctime, which a hard link moves, and never a `.pack`'s mtime, which git FRESHENS without changing a byte whenever a process writes an object the pack holds: an engine write's build in a scratch leaning on the copy does, and read into the identity, every such write would spoil the scrub's verdict on the pack and the repair mark naming it, so a pack an agent kept freshening could go unscrubbed and unrepaired; nothing writes a pack through its own inode, so its inode and size say every change of it but media damage, which is the scrub's) in memory and never persisted, a maintenance run vouching only for what it changed (git keeps a bitmap beside an unchanged index, damaged or not); a VERDICT HOLDS ONLY FOR THE FILE IT HASHED (the path Lstat'ed before, the open file and the path after: replaced meanwhile, no verdict) and a repair acts only on a file still at that identity; a derived file failing it is DROPPED — never written over — and written again at once (the graph graph-first, the index as an `indexRepair`, in a slot of the maintainer's own), and a pack whose `.idx` or `.rev` fails, or that a full round finds at the wrong size, is INSTALLED AGAIN WITH ONE GET RENAMED OVER ITS FILES in the same incarnation, never removed first (removed, its objects were missing to every reader for the whole download); WHAT THE CHECK FINDS GOES ON THE REPAIR MARK as it ends (`recordCheck`), at the identity each file failed at, and comes off once a round has installed or dropped it (`settleFound`) — so a round that cannot act on it, its install failing on a pack it adds or the finding's own download failing, leaves it to the next round rather than to the next check, which skips a file damaged in place after this process vouched for it, and A FINDING FOUND AGAIN STAYS AS IT WAS FOUND: a file the mark already holds at the identity a check finds it failing at keeps its finding, so a scrub's stays the scrub's, which answers a request's scrub and covers a report while it waits (`scrubWaits`) — taken for the forced check's of a request failing on that very damage, it answered neither, and the whole copy was read again once an hour for as long as the store refused its pack; hashing holds ONE READ SLOT A PIECE (`CheckPieceBytes`, 64 MiB; `gitproc.Pool.HoldRead`) and none while the round runs git, a slot not had in `SlotWait` failing the round busy — a refused slot and an ended request decide nothing, while a READ THAT FAILS does: the file is counted `unreadable` and repaired as damaged, since a file the disk cannot read is one git cannot serve, and failing the round over it left the repository unserved for good; the files read are 23% of a copy's pack bytes, 0.18–0.23 s a gigabyte of packs from the page cache and the volume's own throughput cold (39 s for the 20 GiB default largest at 125 MB/s), once a process, measured (`BenchmarkCheck`); and Verify holds every derived file to its trailer too (the one check that names a damaged bitmap) — an installed layer's files by its layer pass alone, which holds each to its trailer as it holds it to its row, so a layer's every byte is read once rather than twice, naming first what its sync's own check repaired (`VerifyReport.RepairedBySync`, apart from its problems: the sync put those files right in place before the check read anything, so the check asks no rebuild for them and the copy it reports on is clean). EVERY BYTE IS SCRUBBED BEHIND THE SERVING (scrub.go) — each `.pack` against its own trailer, which is its name, and every index file by the check's rule, vouched or not — because on a copy the maintainer has served from, upload-pack's pack-objects copies a stored object as it is stored — damaged bytes from any pack, a damaged entry header from the bitmap's preferred pack, which `single` reuse sends without reading — to the client byte for byte and no git on the node names it (the node pin and the floor), holding the packs before a copy first answers would hold the largest repository's first request for as long as reading twenty gigabytes takes, and damage landing in a vouched file with no change of its identity is seen by no vouch (a bitmap damaged so drops an object from every clone of its branch, upload-pack saying nothing): once after the first round of a copy a previous process left (one this process built was held to its rows as it was placed, so its interval starts there; and that first scrub reads every pack but no index file the round just checked), every `ScrubInterval` (`RebuildQuiet`'s day) while the copy is USED — a reader took it, let it go or made a push's scratch over it since its bytes were last held to their checksums, so a copy nobody reads is never scrubbed, and the FIRST such use, of any kind and wherever in the interval it falls, wakes the scrubber, which has no other wake for it (`used`, the one path every use takes: woken only by a reader taking it, a copy whose first use after a scrub was the release of a clone held across its end was never scrubbed again) — and AT ONCE when a git process over it reports a pack or an object damaged, names a pack in words or crashes, but not within a `ScrubCooldown` (an hour) of a scrub that found nothing, whatever is written meanwhile (`reports{outcome=cooldown}`), nor within one of a scrub whose findings still wait for their repair (`reports{outcome=covered}`, the repair its answer) — past it such a report scrubs the copy again, at most once a `ScrubCooldown`; ONE SCRUB ON THE NODE AT A TIME, IN THE ORDER OF ITS EVIDENCE (`scrubSlot`: a failing request's, then a report's, then a copy's first, then the interval's, the least recently scrubbed first among equals) — a request's or a report's stopping a routine scrub at its next piece, which is due again after it — a read slot a piece waited for however long, NO LOCK of the repository and a pin that is no use, so a scrub never makes the next one due; what it finds — a file failing its trailer or one the disk could not read — goes on the repair mark at the identity it failed at, and the repair round installs a pack again over its files with one GET (`crewlet.code.cache.rebuilds{reason=stored}`, once a round) or drops a derived file to be written again — only while the file is still the one that failed, and never under a check; a pack whose `.pack` alone failed keeps the multi-pack-index, bitmap and graph built over it (the same pack byte for byte), one whose `.idx` failed takes them; a GET that fails (`code_cache_pack_reinstall_failed`) ends nothing — the round goes on, the copy serves, and that pack is held off `RebuildCooldown` doubling to `RebuildBackoffCap` (`repairMark.fetchFailed`), so a store that is down neither wedges every round on the copy nor is asked once a round — nor once a failing request or sync, which takes no forced round naming only a held-off pack and no rebuild that would download one; it reads 1.13 times a copy's pack bytes, 0.86–1.38 s a gigabyte of packs from the page cache and the volume's own throughput cold (18–30 s and 194 s at 125 MB/s for the 20 GiB default largest), and a request beside it waits for a read slot only when every other is busy, at most one 64 MiB piece (51–59 ms measured from the page cache, the piece's read at the volume's throughput cold — about half a second at 125 MB/s), `BenchmarkScrub`. WHAT ANY GIT PROCESS REPORTS DAMAGED IS REPAIRED, whether or not a request failed (report.go): git reads around most damage to a derived file — it warns and answers from the packs, and the maintainer's own write over a damaged index fails every time — so the pool hands every run to the cache's observer (`gitproc.Pool.Observe`, a reservation's runs too; installed by `Open`, one cache a pool, given back by `Stop`), which routes it by the process's directory (a scratch's or a view's derived parts and its crash to its alternate; its pack and object parts and its hints may be the client's pack and are counted `unrouted`), narrows a chunk report to the files its process loads (`gitproc.Cmd.Loads`), and records it under the entry's mutex, never its write lock — a pack, an object, a hint or a crash dropping nothing and making the copy's SCRUB due at once (below), UNLESS ITS REQUEST REPAIRS IT: a run in the incarnation whose Cmd names one of the copy's readers (`gitproc.Cmd.Repairer` from `Synced.Repairer`, minted a reader by `pin` — the host's processes over the incarnation and `Missing`'s check — and the round's own peel, naming a reader the round mints and settles once its answer is known, `roundReader`, `settleReader`) is HELD for that reader (`incarnation.held`), CONSUMED by the repair it asks once that has answered it (`awaitRequest`, `reports{outcome=request}`: a forced round, the request's own scrub and the rebuild answer exactly what it names, and made due beside them it read the whole copy — 1.13 times its pack bytes — beside a one-pack repair and raced it to that pack), CARRIED by an in-place repair of a shape or a derived file — which reads no pack and answers none of it — to the reader that repair hands the copy to (`carry`, `awaitInPlace`: the request lets go of the pin it failed over before its retry, so held under that pin a crash beside a damaged graph went to the scrub while the retry escalated to the pack beneath it), and handed to the ordinary rules by the reader's RELEASE when it ends with anything held (past its first byte, a failure the host does not repair, none, a caller gone before its answer, or a shape or a derived file repaired alone and then served: no timer, the request's lifetime the bound), a derived part a drop repairs and a scratch's or a view's report never held — queueing for a derived part a FULL LINEARIZABLE round no request waits for (a full sweep removes what its snapshot does not name, and an adoption installs past the incarnation's position) while the copy keeps answering at once; a part this process rewrote after the reporter BEGAN is spared (`gitproc.Result.Began`, compared on the monotonic clock, `stale`), nothing is repaired under a check (`deferred`, its release asks the round), and a part dropped for damage again within `RebuildQuiet` of its last drop for damage — whatever was written of it between, the maintainer's ordinary writes included, since they land on the same disk — is dropped and left to the ordinary schedule (`code_cache_derived_damaged_again`, once an incarnation; `again`), so a disk that keeps damaging costs one rewrite at once a day — counted by `crewlet.code.cache.reports{part,outcome}`; and the maintainer is woken as a full round LETS THE REPOSITORY GO (`entry.maintainerOwed`), never from inside it, where a pass met the round still running and left the copy to the next tick. The MAINTAINER writes a multi-pack-index with its bitmap and the bitmap's lookup table, and a commit-graph with version-2 CHANGED-PATH FILTERS (every one its commits lack, with no `--max-new-filters`: own mode's first write computes them all and every later one reads them back, the tip computes its tail's at each write — a bound never bounded what a write holds, the trees its filters' diffs keep in git's delta base cache up to its 96 MiB cap, and left a gigabyte's reads diffing most of its history for thousands of writes: measured, a blame and a path's log 13 and 22 times slower after a first write of 100) — recorded apart, so a graph refused a slot is due on its own, and a copy read whole with NO COMMIT-GRAPH gets its graph FIRST: due at once (no debounce, no interval), ahead of every index and bitmap in the pass, on its own charge, and the GRAPH ALONE in ANY slot of the lane, the one kept for a verify included — an index it is due for as well only in a slot of the maintainer's own, since a run takes a slot for each step in turn and lent the kept slot it would hold it through the bitmap (no slot of the maintainer's free, or no room for the index's charge, and the run writes the graph alone) — so the window in which every walk goes by date and a push dated before its parent walks every commit is the graph's own write rather than the maintainer's queue (hours on a node that just joined); a graph-only run is no run the duty cycle counts, one whose graph step failed waits for the ordinary rules (`graphFailed`), and a full round does NOT write the graph itself, which would put a walk of the commits on every cold request to spare the rare skewed push a window as long — in the MAINTENANCE lane (half the cores, never waiting), A REPOSITORY A SLOT in every slot but ONE — kept for a person's `crewlet code verify`, whose fsck is refused rather than queued in a full lane, unless the lane is one slot — and only as many as `MaintenanceMemoryPerCore` (256 MiB a core) holds BESIDE THE LARGEST run in flight, each charged at its STEP'S OWN FIGURE AN OBJECT of its rows (measured on the node pin, peak anonymous memory sampled every millisecond: `BitmapBytesPerObject` 600 — 388 over 32 300 objects of a source repository's history, 560 over 296 000 and 287 over 3 million, a share rising to 100 MB and falling past it, no fixed part at the small end — `IndexBytesPerObject` 80 and `IndexBaseBytes` 1 MiB for the index without a bitmap, `GraphBytesPerObject` 67 and `GraphBaseBytes` 100 MiB for the commit-graph, the latter its filters' diffs' delta base cache) plus room for ONE TRANSFER of the same repository beside it (`gitproc.TransferBytesPerObject`), the largest uncharged because a node serving a repository holds its bitmap once whenever it is written — and NEVER A STEP THE NODE'S MEMORY CANNOT HOLD: the budget is the node's memory, as its pool read it and READ AGAIN AT EVERY PASS (a container resized in place is held to its new limit; a failed read keeps the last), less `gitproc.TransferMemory` for every transfer the pool admits and `ServingMemoryPerCore` (896 MiB, what `CoreMemory`'s 2 GiB a core leaves) for every core the memory BACKS at 2 GiB a core, never for more, so 64 cores with 16 GiB keep an eight-core node's reserve and 2 GiB of budget rather than nothing; and everything in flight — every run, the largest included, a full round's own index and `Verify`'s fsck (`FsckBytesPerObject` 192), each charged the same way — is held to it TOGETHER, and that budget is ADVERTISED as it stands (`Cache.BackgroundBudget`, which the engine's presence row carries on every beat beside its status as `coord.GitBackgroundKey`), so the git node a repository's maintenance is placed on is one whose own admissions would hold the work — the figure, never a second estimate of it. A run's plan takes every step due whose charge fits the budget alone: a BITMAP REFUSED leaves the repository indexed WITHOUT one, at the `MidxPacks` cadence, so a refused repository's pushes keep joining an index (the 20 GiB default largest needs some 50 GB of bitmap and clone), a bitmap the node wrote while it could kept until `MidxRoundPacks` packs gather outside it; the graph runs on its own charge; a step refused is logged once an incarnation (`code_cache_maintenance_refused`, by step), counted (`crewlet.code.cache.maintenance.refusals{reason=memory,step}`), a refused bitmap or graph gauged (`.maintenance.refused{step}`) and alarmed (`code_maintenance_refused`, at the budget itself, read through `Cache.AlarmReading`) rather than OOM-killed at every duty cycle with the engine as likely a victim as git, a round's index the budget cannot hold beside what is in flight answered without (`code_cache_round_index_held`), and a check past the budget alone refused (`ErrNoMemory`); and the first repository in a pass held back for room is given it — nothing that would take it starts behind it — so the largest is never starved by a stream of small ones — a git node's largest repository with a bitmap is bound by its memory before its disk; a pass waiting on no run, so an hours-long bitmap holds nobody else's index back and a joining node's largest repositories never all run at once; only on an incarnation this process has read whole and holding a pack (a history imported in one push needs its bitmap as much as one of thousands), once its packs have stood still for `MidxDebounce` (10 s) or the oldest has waited `MidxMaxWait` (the 10-minute interval: a repository pushed to without pause is indexed as often as one that pauses), and no oftener than `max(MidxMinInterval, MidxDutyCycle × its last run)` (10 min, ×10; MEASURED by `BenchmarkMidx` over a source repository's composition at 11.6 seconds a gigabyte of packs at 100 MB and 14.2 at 1 GB on the node pin (on git 2.43, 16, 21 and 30 at 100 MB, 1 GB and 5 GB), so the interval paces every repository under about 3 to 4 GB and the duty cycle those past it — the 20 GiB default largest, extrapolated, about a quarter of an hour a run, rebuilt at most every two and a half hours): it holds a pin, never the repository's round, and a full round stops it before its sweep, since an index naming a pack the sweep removes fails every read; a run stopped from outside (Stop, a sweep, a retire) is no run and pushes nothing back — but a swap's stop, counted as the repository's last run (M3-O15), since a repository compacted more often than its index run lasts would otherwise have every run stopped and the next due at once — and one that fails removes the lock files and the `tmp_bitmap_*` a killed git leaves. `TestNothingButSyncWritesACache` holds the cache a function of rows from both sides: a two-sided allowance, keyed by file and by kind with a reason each, of every file under `internal/code` that writes the filesystem or runs a git subcommand outside a CLOSED set of readers, each refused in every spelling git may accept of the one option that makes it write (`fsck --lost-found`, `rev-list --output`) — a subcommand it has not heard of is a writer until argued, since `merge-tree --write-tree`, `pack-objects` with a base name and `hash-object -w` write wherever they run; every `gitproc.Cmd` literal is found by its TYPE through `sourcetree/decltype` (an alias's, a defined type's and an elided element's included) and judged by its `Args`, and a Cmd handed to ANY call — the pool's `Run`, a helper, an interface, a function value, a method it is the receiver of — that the call does not spell as a literal is a row of its own however its arguments were built (assigned after the literal, from `new`, changed in place by a helper), since a Cmd reaches git only by being handed on, as is a value handed to a call when the walk can type neither (never the TYPE `new` or `make` is handed, which hands nothing on); the one handoff passed is gitproc's own plumbing passing on, singly to a Cmd parameter, a Cmd PARAMETER of its own function — never a named result or a receiver — and in gitproc's files a Cmd parameter written, addressed or copied (it, its `Args` or an element of them) is a row where that happens, handed on or not, since gitproc's one exec takes a parameter's `Args` — and sixteen seeded interleavings of what a node's cache meets (pushes, refused pushes received into a scratch, adoptions, syncs at every level and killed at each fault point, crashes leaving a killed maintenance run's files, evictions, rebuilds, maintenance, swept tombstones, a log recreated backwards, compactions and the swaps that take their packs out, publications by this node's own compactor and by another git node's, the installer's passes, a layer's part the store lost or spoiled, the node's tip), every repository checked every ten steps against a cache rebuilt from nothing at the same position — its installed chain held to a prefix of the rows' and to the fresh cache's, but for a layer it installed before the store lost a part of it — and the seeds held to reaching every outcome — a round's own index and a maintenance run over the maintainer's own index among them, at thresholds lowered for repositories of a dozen packs, since at the production counts no seed reaches either. AN EXPUNGE'S interleavings (`interleavings_expunge_test.go`) run the production expunger (`compact`) over the same cache beside those operations — a person's purge request, a cycle, one with a branch revived between its scan and its begin, one over a copy whose commit-graph lists what it drops, one crashed at each point of `codefault.ExpungePath` and resumed from the rows, and a push building on a dropping commit — holding every copy to `git fsck --connectivity-only` with its dropping view named, and every commit a commit dropped to one the copy's commit-graph no longer lists and `git rev-list` no longer walks. A repository's HEAT (`heat.go`) — its fetches, clones, pushes and bytes out over a 1 h and a 24 h window of twenty-four buckets each, counted from the cache's opening on the monotonic clock — read under the lock each window is counted under and never behind the latest slot it was acted at (`heatNow`), so a caller whose reading went stale waiting for the lock judges no repository idle that a later caller had just counted — IN MEMORY and this process's alone, since it describes one node and a repository's id is no closed set for a metric — is counted by the host as it answers and DECIDES NOTHING (the budget still evicts by last use, and the health tools set no thresholds over it); beside it the cache's own evictions and repairs over the same windows, a repair counted into `crewlet.code.cache.rebuilds` and the windows by ONE method (`rebuilt`) so the two never disagree; `Status`, `Copy` (a repository's copy in service) and `Heat.Hot` (the `HotRepos` = 20 hottest of the hour and of the day, one list readable either way) read it back for the host's status. An incarnation is in CHAIN mode — the INSTALLED PUBLISHED CHAIN (`layers.go`): every layer's `.midx`, `.bitmap` and commit-graph layer byte for byte as the repository's maintainer wrote them, and both chain files, which the cache writes from the rows' order because a layer's files never name its base — or in OWN mode, the maintainer's whole index and graph; never both, since git deletes either for the other (`EventLayersMoved` drops own mode's files, the maintainer plans no own-mode write in chain mode, and a round writes no index beside either shape, `indexed`). A FULL round installs the chain before it answers, every part queued behind its packs; a DELTA records the chain and hands what the copy lacks to the INSTALLER (`Start`'s goroutine: at most a round's share of the node's fetches, and ONE SLOT of the read lane across all its tasks for its checks' pieces and ONE for its placements' canaries (`installReadSlots`, as the scrub's: sixteen tasks are there for sixteen GETs, and bounded by its tasks alone it held the whole lane of a node of sixteen cores or fewer through a mass install; a slot for each kind because a canary runs under its repository's write lock, which in the checks' slot waited behind their hashing while the repository's rounds waited behind it), a failure that may pass tried again a minute later doubling to `installRetryCap`, 30 min; each repository held ONCE, due in order, waiting on a heap by when its wait ends, or running — a kick meanwhile holding it once more as its task ends — so a pass costs what it moves and takes up, never what the installer holds, since one runs at every kick and a walk of every repository held drained 10 000 in millions of steps under the lock every round's kick waits on) and answers. The chain HOLDS ITS PACKS: a retired pack an installed layer names stays — no stray to a round or to Verify — until a placement replaces the layer, or a stale layer is cut after `installRetryCap` OF ITS OWN: the clock is keyed by the row the layer was installed from, never by the repository, since an episode can end where the installer does not see it (a full round placing the replacement), and a repository's clock cut the next episode's stale layer at its first failed fetch; an install that finds nothing due forgets the repository's wait. A placement is a SWAP on an incarnation a reader may hold (the swap's layer step) and in place on one nobody does, with a CANARY read through the bitmap before the chain is visible: a layer file does not name its base, and one over the wrong base passes `multi-pack-index verify` and fails the first bitmapped read — the canary's VERDICT is git's alone (an exit that is not its answer, or damage named), so one that read nothing of the chain (no read slot within `SlotWait`, its deadline) refuses nothing and the layers are tried again, where taken for a verdict they were refused `corrupt` until the next publication. `prefixOf` is THE ONE CUT both chains share — a pack leaving, a failed check or the rows' chain moving cuts below the first layer it touches (`Prefix`), never a drop — and a git report on a chain's file makes the scrub due rather than dropping it. A layer missing (asked once at the log's head first — a head that cannot be read decides nothing, and the try waits as one the store failed does), corrupt or from a newer git (`gitproc.ParseRelease`) is REFUSED — counted, logged once a copy, never an error — and the copy serves from the prefix below; a rebuild seeds every layer it holds to its row (`code.CheckLayer`) over packs it linked; Verify holds each to its row; `AdoptLayer` spares the maintainer its own GET, and the installer never downloads a layer twice for a copy that moved under it: a placement that finds the copy or its chain moved since its plan stages what it fetched for the next plan (`holdFetched`; a round's install claims it too), which drops any it no longer wants (`dropFetched`), and a round serving the very chain a plan was made over is no move (`chainGen` counts changes to the chain or to what is installed, never reads). No node writes a multi-pack-index layer of its own: on the pin a bitmapped layer costs the walk of the whole history however few objects it adds, and an unbitmapped one switches off every bitmap beneath it. The one file a node writes over a chain is its TIP: git's split commit-graph layer (`--split=no-merge`) of the commits the installed graph layers lack — the TAIL's, the live packs no layer names, counted as the live set and the chain move (`incarnation.tail`, so a pass reads it in one load) — over exactly those layers, written WHOLE each time (the chain file rewritten first to name them alone, so git expires the old tip rather than stacking a new one on it), due at once on a copy that holds none and otherwise by the ordinary cadence once the tail's packs are no longer those it was written over (`tipWanted`: an order-free digest of them, `tailDigest`, persisted beside the tip so neither a restart nor a full round over the same packs writes it again), and charged `TipGraphBytesPerObject` (50, measured) an object of the tail and `GraphBaseBytes` (100 MiB) whatever its length — its filters' diffs — with room for a clone of the whole repository (`Write.charge`, every declared step's one charge, a declaration's `BaseBytes` its fixed part), refused onto its own gauge step (`tip-graph`) and never the alarm, its copy served through the chain's bitmap; it crosses a swap only where the whole chain beneath it does and every pack leaving is a retirement (`tipGoes`), any other change takes it, a failing tip is dropped and written again rather than fetched (`repairAction`), and a write of a chain file in place is marked (`chainWrite`) so a scrub or Verify that read the record before it gives the file no verdict (`chainVerdict`). WHICH ENTRIES ARE HOTTEST IS ONE RULE (`Hottest`): the top of each window by its own count, kept by place so one repository on two git nodes is two entries, ordered hour then day — what `Heat.Hot` cuts one node's list by and what `code_status` merges the fleet's lists by, so the fleet's hot list ranks by no rule a node does not. ITS FILE-NAME PATTERNS COMPILE AT FIRST USE (`lazyPattern`), never at init: the code host's proc-receive hook is this binary, exec'd once a push, and pays every linked package's initialisation before its first statement (cmd/crewlet's `TestTheHookPaysLittleForTheEnginesInit`) — a hook that opens no cache. THE CODE INDEX (`codeindex.go`, M6-D12) is the trigram index of a `search: indexed` repository's default-branch tip, kept INSIDE the incarnation (`crewlet/codeindex/seg.<n>`, `tip.<n>`, no main file: its record in the state says it is there) so a compaction's swap and a rebuild carry it by HARD LINK (S18) — its postings are keyed by blob, which no pack moving changes; a new tip reads only the blobs no segment holds, through one `cat-file` in the maintenance lane; the INDEXER (`Start`) keeps one for each repository of the `indexed` listing this node is among the first `code.IndexHolders` of in `Placement.Order` (`estate.Router.GitOrder`), filling a copy it does not hold, nudged by a round that moves refs and polling every `IndexPoll`, and drops one the order moved past; the indexes are held to `CodeIndexShare` of the budget by their own trim, least recently used first, never by a repository's eviction. |

THE CHECKS WORKER (`checks.go`) reads a repository's check work (`code.read` kind `checks_due`, its `RefreshBefore` the home's clock less `ChecksFileRefresh`, 10 min, PROVISIONAL: a moved target's checks file is read again at the home's pace, never at every push) and asks the repository's checks writer (`code.checks`) — and, for a protected target's TIP WORK (`tips.go`, ADR-0068), its committer (`code.queue`): for each protected target holding a landing neither green nor broken (`code.ReadAnswer.DueTips`, read whole in one snapshot), its NEWEST landing dispatched alone (purpose `tip`, coalesced) under the checks file at the BASE of the first landing above the green, never at the tip, which a landing's edit of the file would let define its own measurement; a `green` record once every required check passed there; and where one failed, the failed checks BISECTED over the landing rows (purpose `bisect`, the landing midway between the last they passed on and the first they failed on), never over git's history, down to the first red landing, recorded `broken` once — each decided again by its own writer, so a step decided from a moved snapshot is refused and read again; for each TARGET due a reading, its checks file read at its tip; for each change, its REQUEUES, and where its DISPATCH is owed, the attempts its change moved on from ended `cancelled superseded`, then every check the checks file at its TARGET'S TIP defines that the head holds no attempt of — read through the git reads (`code.GitChecksFile`, never at the head: a change is never measured by a definition it brought), once a tip and parsed once a blob a turn — queued, or recorded skipped where the head touches none of its `paths` (`gitref.PathGlob`, against the head's facts read at that tip, `touches_all` running every one), a required one marked required, one an earlier dispatch errored for a file that did not run queued as its next attempt, every required name of a file that is none that runs recorded errored `defs_invalid`; then the page's dispatches RECORDED LAST (`code.CheckDispatched`, a target's heads at most `code.MaxDispatchHeads` a record, a reading with none in one), so one cut short is the next turn's. THE MERGE (`merge.go`) of a change owing one: this node's copy synced to the listing's position and pinned, a `cache.NewScratch` beside it named `MergeScratchID(change, revision, tip position)` (an unnamed derivation, minted at the change's instant) and removed after, ANCESTRY FIRST (`merge-base --is-ancestor`: a tip holding the head is the change LANDED, D7b), then ONE `merge-tree --write-tree -z --name-only --no-messages --allow-unrelated-histories` in the MAINTENANCE lane within `MergeabilityDeadline` (30 s, three reads' git, PROVISIONAL — `code.MergeBuildDeadline`, the budget an engine-built merge of the same two commits is given), its streamed output held as a record holds it (`code.ConflictsHeld`), recorded through the change writer (`code.Mergeability`), a merge past its deadline `unknown` — IN A SCRATCH, NEVER A CACHE, since merge-tree writes the merged tree and every marked blob into the repository it runs in (the cache's writers gate's row for this file; `TestMergeabilityRunsInAScratch`). A HISTORY SHARING NO COMMIT WITH ITS TARGET'S merges from the empty tree, as its facts measure it, rather than fail every pass. A NEW head revision is merged at once; a MOVED TARGET'S changes (the listing's rechecks) once their merge is `MergeabilityRefresh` (10 min, PROVISIONAL) old, the oldest first, at most `MergeabilityPerPass` (64, PROVISIONAL) a repository in one window of the backstop whichever turns run them (`recheckBudget`: a rate, never a list, its ended windows forgotten once a window), so a target moving on every push costs its home 64 merges a minute at most; `BenchmarkMergeability` measures what those three are read against. THE MERGE PREVIEWS (`preview.go`, `Previews`) answer a git node's `code.merge_preview` (`estate.GitServer.MergePreview`) by THE SAME MERGE (`mergeIn`), so a preview and the change it would open say one thing: a SERVER OF READS BESIDE THE LOOP, never part of it, built by every git node's composition beside its query server from exactly a read by object id's inputs — cache, pool, the node's one seat git-work share, the chart, the reads ledger — and answered as such a read is: a seat refused about the node behind its configuration or holding no chart, `not_held` off the walk's home, the copy synced within `statelog.ReadBudget`, the grant judged (`code.JudgeRead`), both ids found as commits with one `cat-file`, a refusal concluded only at the log's head, the merge in its own scratch in the READ lane under ONE unit of the share within `gitproc.ReadDeadline`, a `visibility: unit` repository's preview recorded as its asker's read (`PreviewReadKind`); stopped with the git reads, cutting what is in flight. THE MERGE QUEUE (`queue.go`, M5-D12, M5-D13, M5-D15, M5-D27) reads one repository's queue work a target a page (`code.read` kind `queue_due`, `code.QueueDue`: the target's tip, its gate's depth, batch and required checks, its live entries and its live train with each candidate commit's latest required attempts, ONE snapshot) and takes each queue target's next step: a train BUILT onto the tip where none stands there (or one tests an entry no longer live) — candidates of `BatchOf` = min(B, ⌈W/D⌉) entries up to depth D as ONE chain through `Chains` (internal/code/host's `BuildChain`, settled applied once the `candidate` record applies; the record states the drop epoch of the copy the chain was built from, `ChainResult.DropEpoch`, and a `land` the epoch its snapshot read, `QueueDue.DropEpoch`, so a train built before an expunge's begin is refused `expunging` and built again), conflicts and non-fast-forwards `eject`ed — the standing train's tips queued the required checks defined by the train BASE's checks file (purpose `candidate`), the LONGEST GREEN PREFIX landed in one `land` record (entries it refuses `gate_unmet`/`head_moved` ejected), a red first candidate BISECTED over its entries' commits (purpose `bisect`, midway between the last good and the first red) and its culprit ejected `check_failed`; a required check the base defines in no form that runs ejects the train's entries. The queue worker also measures each target's MERGE RATE (`mergerate.go`, M5-D28): its enqueues within `code.MergeRateWindow` (an hour), counted from the rows by every page of its queue work (`QueueDueQuery.EnqueuedSince`), against `config.LandingCapacity` at the MEAN ROUND this node timed its own trains at, build to landing — a train still testing counted at its age once it outlasts the mean — and `Loop.AlarmReading` hands the engine `code_merge_rate_over_capacity`. NODE-LOCAL, as the compactor's last pass is, because only the home builds a queue's trains and so only it can time them: a home new to a queue judges it from its first train, a target whose queue drained is dropped by the next turn that reads every page, and a figure lapses after one window, the target forgotten by the next reading once it times no train either (a queue whose home moved, or whose repository was deleted, is never turned here again). Nothing of a train is held in the worker, so a new home resumes it from the rows. Once a land APPLIED, each landed entry with `close_task` has its work item closed AS ITS ENQUEUER through `Tasks` (the engine's tracker, internal/engine/codequeue.go: `update_work_item` decided for the enqueuer, status `done`, op `StepOpID(land, "close-work-item", entry)`). `Options.Chains` and `Options.Tasks` are required. | `code/checkrun` | A REPOSITORY'S QUEUED CHECKS RUN ON A GIT NODE: a `Runner` on EVERY git node walks the queue oldest queued first (`code.read` kind `checks_queued`, the engine's own, each attempt with its definition and its repository's name, on `code_checks_queued_idx`, a page of `WalkPage` 256) and ranks each attempt by its repository's GIT ORDER (`estate.Router.GitOrder`: every live git node in rendezvous order, its home first): rank r claims only once the attempt has waited r × `ClaimStagger` (2 s, JUDGMENT: ten healthy claims' rounds) since it was queued, so the home claims first and its runner-up a stagger later where the home cannot — NO LEASE AND NO COORDINATOR (rejected: home-only runners, past one node's cores at a large company's ~1 700 concurrent checks; a lease per check, ~15 coordination writes).
